# 2-Day Tent-Camping Hike (Friday Evening – Sunday), Starting from Ustroń — Research Report

> Decision-support research: 14 candidate PL/SK/CZ mountain ranges for a weekend tent-camping trip (Friday-evening start from Ustroń -> Sunday return), two nights. Fields marked ⚠️ contain parts the research agent flagged itself as an estimate/uncertainty (e.g. a map-read distance, not a routing-engine one) — the content is still complete; treat ⚠️ as a to-verify list for departure week, not a data gap. Fully empty fields are omitted.

## Details

### 1. Beskid Śląski (Silesian Beskids, PL)

**Access & Travel**

- **Distance / time from Ustroń ⚠️:** ZERO approach — Ustroń sits inside the range and is the official kilometre-0 of the Główny Szlak Beskidzki (GSB), so the only candidate where the trip can start on foot from the front door. Trailhead 'Ustroń Kropka' (near the Ustroń Zdrój / Ustroń rail stations) is a 10-30 min walk from anywhere in town. If a car-supported variant is preferred: Wisła Głębce ~22 km / ~30 min via DW941; Przełęcz Kubalonka ~19 km / ~28 min; Wisła Czarne (Przysłop trailhead) ~28 km / ~40 min; Istebna ~25 km / ~35 min. DATA SOURCE FLAG: all driving figures are map estimates, NOT verified in a routing engine. Practical consequence: this range wins on approach cost but loses on legal-camping geography — the only currently-open legal bivouac zones sit 16-25 km away by trail, in the Wisła/Istebna forest district, not around Ustroń.
- **PL/SK/CZ border:** The range is entirely in Poland, but its western ridge IS the PL/CZ state border: Czantoria Wielka (995 m), Soszów, Stożek Wielki (978 m) and Przełęcz Jabłonkowska all straddle it, and the GSB runs on or beside the border line for ~15 km. Schengen — no checks, no passport control, you can step across freely and PL/CZ trails interconnect at Czantoria, Beskidek, Stożek and Jabłonkowska. Practical differences if you drift onto the Czech side: KČT marking (same white-colour-white stripe logic but different signpost design and 'rozcestník' time-based signage instead of PL km/hours), Czech maps (mapy.cz) rather than PL, CZK currency for the Czech chaty, and — importantly — a DIFFERENT camping regime (CHKO Beskydy rules, see the Moravskoslezské Beskydy item). Poland's 'Zanocuj w lesie' permission does NOT extend across the border; do not pitch on the Czech slope assuming Polish rules.
- **Parking ⚠️:** Largely irrelevant in the recommended train/on-foot scenario, which is the point of this range. If driving: Ustroń Kropka / Ustroń Zdrój street and P&R parking, mostly paid in the spa zone (~4-6 PLN/h, roughly 20-30 PLN/day) [uncertain — spa-town tariffs change seasonally]; Przełęcz Kubalonka has a large free lay-by/ski-area lot that never fills in summer; Wisła Czarne / Przysłop trailhead has a paid forest lot; Wisła Głębce station has free parking next to the terminus. Overnight standing is not explicitly forbidden at the Kubalonka and Głębce lots (public road/station lots) but IS forbidden on Lasy Państwowe forest roads and inside barriered forest lots. Capacity is not a real constraint here — unlike Vrátna or Šútovo, nothing fills before dawn; the honeypots (Równica summit road, Czantoria gondola lot, Barania Góra/Wisła Czarne) can fill by ~10:00 on a sunny July Saturday. [uncertain — no live occupancy data found].
- **Public transport ⚠️:** STRONGEST public-transport case of any candidate, and the reason the linear A->B scenario works. Koleje Śląskie line 190/157 runs Bielsko-Biała – Ustroń Poniwiec – Ustroń – Ustroń Zdrój – Ustroń Polana – Wisła Uzdrowisko – Wisła Głębce (terminus), i.e. both ends of the range are on the SAME local line with Ustroń in the middle. Reported frequency ~8 train pairs on weekdays and ~10 on weekends/holidays; Ustroń -> Wisła Głębce takes roughly 25-35 min and costs single-digit-to-low-teens PLN [uncertain — exact 2026 timetable and fare not verified; check portalpasazera.pl / koleo.pl in the departure week]. This enables the canonical plan: Friday evening train out to Wisła Głębce (or walk out of the house), Sunday afternoon train back from Wisła Głębce / Wisła Uzdrowisko — no loop needed, no car shuttle, no second driver. Backup: PKS/ZTZ buses along DW941 over Kubalonka to Istebna and Koniaków.
- **Vignettes ⚠️:** None. The whole route is inside Poland on toll-free national/voivodeship roads; Polish e-TOLL applies only to vehicles >3.5 t, so a passenger car pays nothing. No SK e-známka and no CZ e-dálniční kupon are needed. Only relevant if you deliberately detour through Czechia on the D48 (Český Těšín – Frýdek-Místek) — then a CZ e-vignette is required, ~279 CZK for 10 days / ~449 CZK for 30 days [uncertain — 2026 tariff not re-verified]. There is no reason to do this for this range.

**Camping Legality**

- **Protection status:** NO national park — this is the key legal advantage. The range is covered by Park Krajobrazowy Beskidu Śląskiego (Silesian Beskids Landscape Park, created 1998, ~38.6 thousand ha plus buffer), which is a LANDSCAPE park: it restricts development and land use but does NOT by itself prohibit hiking or pitching a tent. Overlaid: Natura 2000 site Beskid Śląski PLH240005, and several strict nature reserves where camping and leaving the trail ARE banned — Barania Góra (383 ha, covering the summit and the Biała/Czarna Wisełka source basin), Wisła, Czantoria, Stok Szyndzielni, Kuźnie, Zadni Gaj, Dolina Łańskiego Potoku, Jaworzyna. Everything else is ordinary State Forest (Nadleśnictwo Ustroń, Nadleśnictwo Wisła, Nadleśnictwo Bielsko), where the Forest Act — not park law — is the binding constraint. Net: the binding regime is forestry law + reserve boundaries, which is far more permissive than the national-park items on this list.
- **Camping rules ⚠️:** DEFAULT RULE: Ustawa o lasach art. 30 forbids camping in State Forests outside places designated by the forest district — so 'wild' pitching anywhere in the Beskid Śląski is an offence by default. THE LEGAL CHANNEL is the Lasy Państwowe programme 'Zanocuj w lesie', which converts named forest compartments into legal free-bivouac zones. Nadleśnictwo Wisła's zone is 1,510 ha and is defined compartment-by-compartment: Obręb Wisła — Leśnictwo Dziechcinka (oddz. 16-20, 23-27), Leśnictwo Przysłup (52, 53, 70-73), Leśnictwo Barania (79, 80a-d/f/g/i-l, 81g-i/l-n), Leśnictwo Czarne (58-62, 74, 82, 85a-c/w, 87a-c, 88), Leśnictwo Malinka (84, 86, 89-92f-k, 93i-n); Obręb Istebna — Leśnictwo Gańczorka (69-90, 92-95). Rules inside the zone: max 9 people at one spot, max 2 consecutive nights, no permission or fee needed below those limits; larger groups or longer stays require an e-mail notification to wisla@katowice.lasy.gov.pl at least 2 working days ahead, with written confirmation constituting consent. Hammock/tarp preferred; a tent is allowed on firm ground without damaging the litter layer or vegetation. NO open fire except at sites explicitly designated by the district (Nadl. Wisła designates none inside the bushcraft zone as far as could be verified [uncertain]); certified gas stoves are allowed but forbidden at fire-danger level 3 and forbidden on peat, in young stands under 5 m canopy, and among tall grass/heather. No motor vehicles, no wood cutting, no litter, no approaching wildlife, dogs leashed, no entry to areas signposted 'zakaz wstępu'. Nature reserves (esp. Barania Góra) are excluded — you may not pitch there even if the surrounding compartment is in the zone. Nadleśnictwo Ustroń also has a 'Zanocuj w lesie' zone on paper, but it is SUSPENDED (see current_status_2026). Second legal channel: paid pitches — a handful of PTTK shelters and valley campsites accept tents, typically 25-60 PLN per person, but no shelter in this range advertises a formal camping meadow the way the Beskid Żywiecki bacówki do [uncertain].
- **2026 status (live blockers) ⚠️:** CHECKED 2026-07-28 — and this field is the single biggest blocker for this range. (1) Nadleśnictwo Ustroń SUSPENDED its 'Zanocuj w lesie' programme on 8 May 2026 because of extreme drought and 'enormous' forest-fire risk in the Beskid forests; the district said only that the programme 'will be resumed if fire danger decreases' and no resumption announcement was found as of 2026-07-28. This removes every legal wild-camping option in the Ustroń/Równica/Czantoria half of the range — exactly the half you can walk to from home. (2) Nadleśnictwo Wisła's programme page still presents the zone as ACTIVE, so the Wisła/Istebna compartments listed above are currently the legal anchor — but this could be withdrawn by district order at any time on the same drought grounds, and the district publishes fire-hazard-linked restrictions. (3) Nationally, ~29 of 429 forest districts had temporary forest-entry bans (zakaz wstępu do lasu) in force around 19 July 2026, mainly windthrow damage from the severe storm episodes of 28 June-1 July, 7 July and 19 July 2026; the Beskid districts were not confirmed on that list but standing damaged timber is a real hazard. (4) Nadl. Ustroń has declared an absolute prohibition on any use of fire in its forests. MANDATORY RE-CHECK IN THE DEPARTURE WEEK, in this order: zakazywstepu.bdl.lasy.gov.pl (entry bans), wisla.katowice.lasy.gov.pl and ustron.katowice.lasy.gov.pl (programme status + fire-danger degree), mBDL app (to confirm your pitch is inside a listed compartment), and the daily fire-danger forecast (level 3 = no gas stove).
- **Hourly / seasonal restrictions:** No time-of-day limit on walking — Poland has nothing equivalent to the NP Malá Fatra 06:00-21:00 window, and there is no national park here, so you may hike and arrive at your pitch after dark. Nor is there a Czech-style 'pitch after 18:00, strike before 09:00' rule; the Polish 'Zanocuj w lesie' permission is round-the-clock inside its compartments. What DOES constrain you: (a) forest operations in Nadl. Wisła run Monday-Thursday, with Friday from 15:00 through Sunday declared work-free — which happens to align perfectly with a Friday-evening departure; (b) areas under active logging or windthrow clearance are signposted and closed, and their boundaries move; (c) collective driven hunts, marked with yellow warning boards, occur roughly October-January — irrelevant in July but relevant if the trip slips to autumn; (d) nature reserves, above all Barania Góra, are trail-only year-round with no bivouac at any hour; (e) drought-driven district orders (the Ustroń suspension) are the real seasonal restriction in 2026. No seasonal trail closures of the Malá Fatra type exist in this range.
- **Fine risk:** Low-to-moderate financial risk, but real enforcement presence. Pitching outside a designated place in State Forests is a wykroczenie punished by a fine typically issued as a mandat karny up to 500 PLN by Straż Leśna or Policja; press and legal summaries consistently quote 'up to 500 PLN, or a reprimand' for the camping offence itself. Offences stack: littering, damaging the litter layer, collecting deadwood, disturbing wildlife or driving into the forest can push a combined mandate to ~1,000 PLN, and cases sent to court can go materially higher (some sources cite up to 5,000 PLN for the aggravated forest offences a forest guard can charge). Using open fire in a forest or within 100 m of one is a separate and far more serious matter, especially under a declared fire prohibition. Practical risk profile: Straż Leśna in Nadl. Ustroń/Wisła is active precisely because this is a heavily-visited resort range, and the 2026 drought has raised patrol attention. Staying strictly inside the Nadl. Wisła compartment list, with a gas stove and no fire, reduces this risk to essentially zero — that is the whole point of using the programme rather than improvising.

**Route & Camps**

- **Suggested route:** RECOMMENDED (legal-anchor-driven, linear A->B, no car): FRIDAY — train Ustroń -> Wisła Głębce (~30 min), then red GSB / local trails up toward Przełęcz Kubalonka and the Leśnictwo Gańczorka or Dziechcinka compartments; ~4-6 km, +350-450 m, 1.5-2 h -> Camp 1 inside the 'Zanocuj w lesie' zone. SATURDAY — GSB red eastwards: Kubalonka -> Stecówka -> Schronisko PTTK Przysłop pod Baranią Górą -> Barania Góra (1,220 m, the range's second summit and the source of the Vistula) -> descend into the Czarna Wisełka / Malinka valleys; ~15-18 km, roughly +800/-700 m, 6-7 h with breaks -> Camp 2 in the Leśnictwo Czarne / Malinka compartments (NOT in the Barania Góra reserve). SUNDAY — Malinka/Czarne -> Wisła Uzdrowisko or Wisła Głębce station; ~8-12 km, mostly downhill, 3-4 h; train home. Total ~28-36 km, ~1,300-1,500 m ascent, technically easy (forest roads and well-marked ridge trail, no exposure), suitable for a loaded pack. PURIST 'walk out of the house' VARIANT: Day 1 Ustroń Kropka -> Równica -> Czantoria Wielka -> Przełęcz Beskidek -> Schronisko Soszów (16.4 km, ~6 h 20); Day 2 Soszów -> Stożek -> Kiczory -> Przełęcz Kubalonka -> Stecówka -> Przysłop (20.6 km, ~6 h 50) — this is the textbook GSB stage split and DOES hit 16-21 km/day, but Day 1 ends inside the SUSPENDED Nadl. Ustroń zone, so as of July 2026 it has no legal camp at the end unless a shelter pitch can be arranged. Choose the recommended variant while the Ustroń suspension stands.
- **Friday-evening feasibility:** BEST Friday-evening profile of any candidate — because there is effectively no drive. Sunset in Ustroń at the end of July is around 20:25-20:40 CEST with usable civil twilight to roughly 21:05-21:15 [approximate; verify for the exact date]. Timeline for the recommended variant: leave work/home 17:00-17:30, catch a Koleje Śląskie train from Ustroń around 17:30-18:30, arrive Wisła Głębce 18:00-19:00, walk 1.5-2 h -> tent up 19:45-20:45, i.e. inside daylight or at worst 20-30 min of head-torch walking on a forest road. That comfortably satisfies the '≤1.5 h of restricted-visibility walking' guideline. Slack is large: even a 19:30 departure still gets you to Camp 1 by ~21:30 on an easy, wide, low-consequence forest track. For the purist walk-from-home variant the arithmetic fails — leaving Ustroń on foot at 18:00 buys only ~2.5-3 h of daylight, about 8-10 km, which reaches the Czantoria/Soszów area but NOT any currently-legal pitch. Also note the useful coincidence that Nadl. Wisła declares Friday from 15:00 onwards work-free, so you will not walk into active logging.
- **Camp 1:** Leśnictwo Gańczorka block, Obręb Istebna (compartments 69-90 and 92-95) — forested slopes on the Istebna/Kubalonka side, straddling the GSB corridor between Kiczory and Przełęcz Kubalonka. Legal basis: named compartments in Nadleśnictwo Wisła's 'Zanocuj w lesie' order (1,510 ha zone), so free, no booking, max 9 people / 2 nights. Character: spruce-beech stands with logging roads and flat shelves, well off the honeypot summits, so effectively private on a Friday night. ALTERNATIVE if arriving late or in bad weather: the Leśnictwo Dziechcinka block (compartments 16-20, 23-27) directly above Wisła Dziechcinka — the closest legal ground to the Wisła Głębce railhead, roughly 45-75 min uphill from the station, which is the fastest legal tent-up in the whole range. VERIFY ON SITE with the mBDL app that you are inside a listed oddział before pitching — the zone is compartment-precise, not a fuzzy area, and adjacent forest is off-limits. Water must be carried up from Wisła; these blocks are ridge-side and dry.
- **Camp 2:** Leśnictwo Czarne / Leśnictwo Malinka blocks (Czarne: compartments 58-62, 74, 82, 85a-c/w, 87a-c, 88; Malinka: 84, 86, 89-92f-k, 93i-n), in the Czarna Wisełka and Malinka valleys below the Barania Góra massif — reached by descending off the GSB after the summit, via Przysłop. Legal basis: same Nadleśnictwo Wisła 'Zanocuj w lesie' order; free, max 9 people / 2 nights. Advantages over Camp 1: valley-floor stands beside the Czarna Wisełka give a real water source, the ground is sheltered from ridge wind, and you are already positioned for a short Sunday walk-out to Wisła Uzdrowisko/Głębce. HARD CONSTRAINT: the Barania Góra nature reserve (383 ha, wrapping the summit and the Vistula springs) is EXCLUDED — no pitching, no leaving the trail inside it, so drop clearly below the reserve boundary before looking for a spot. SECONDARY OPTION: compartments of Leśnictwo Przysłup (52, 53, 70-73) near Przysłop/Stecówka, if you want to stop short of Barania Góra — this shortens Day 2 to ~10-12 km and drops the camp spacing further below target.
- **Actual spacing vs 15-25 km target ⚠️:** EXPLICIT MISMATCH WITH THE 15-25 km CRITERION — and it takes deliberate effort to even reach the bottom of the band. Straight-line separation between the Kubalonka/Gańczorka block and the Czarne/Malinka block is only about 8-12 km. The recommended Day 2 gets to ~15-18 km of walking only because the route deliberately detours over Barania Góra and down into the Czarna Wisełka valley rather than taking the direct line; without that detour the honest figure is 10-12 km. Estimated, not GPS-measured: derived from published GSB stage lengths (Ustroń Kropka -> Soszów 16.4 km; Soszów -> Przysłop 20.6 km; Przysłop -> Węgierska Górka 16.4 km) and map interpolation of the compartment blocks — treat as ±2-3 km. Structural cause: the whole Beskid Śląski GSB section from Ustroń to the Żywiec basin is only ~35-40 km, and the currently-legal anchors are clustered in one half of it, so 20-25 km/day is achievable only by walking the entire Ustroń -> Przysłop traverse in a single day (37 km, unrealistic with camp gear) or by accepting one long day and one short one. VERDICT: the range meets the criterion at its lower bound at best; it wins on approach and legality-of-access, not on inter-camp spacing. If 15-25 km/day is a hard requirement, Beskid Sądecki, Veľká Fatra or Nízke Tatry serve it better.
- **Water sources:** Better than the Slovak ridge candidates, but 2026 drought degrades the small sources. RELIABLE: taps and shops in Wisła (fill 3-4 L before going up); PTTK shelters Stożek, Soszów, Przysłop pod Baranią Górą and Równica sell water/tea in season and are spaced 3-4 h apart along the GSB; the Czarna Wisełka and Biała Wisełka are permanent streams in their valleys and are the reason Camp 2 is placed there — filter or boil (forestry catchment, beaver activity, hikers upstream). SEMI-RELIABLE: the springs of the Vistula on the NW slope of Barania Góra (Biała Wisełka source ~1,100 m, Czarna Wisełka 'wykapy' ~1,130 m) — inside the nature reserve, ~15 min off the main trail, do not camp there. UNRELIABLE / DRY: the whole ridge line Równica - Czantoria - Soszów - Stożek - Kubalonka has essentially no water between shelters; the small side-streams that normally trickle off the ridge are the first to fail, and 2026 has been an exceptional drought year (severe enough to trigger the Ustroń bivouac suspension). PLANNING RULE: treat the ridge as waterless, carry 2.5-3 L per person off the last tap, and place Camp 2 in a valley rather than on a saddle. Carry a filter — do not rely on finding a spring.
- **Fire / cooking ⚠️:** NO OPEN FIRE. Inside 'Zanocuj w lesie' zones fire is permitted only at sites explicitly designated by the forest district, and Nadl. Wisła designates none within the bushcraft compartments as far as could be verified [uncertain]; independently, Nadl. Ustroń has declared an absolute ban on any use of fire in its forests during the 2026 drought, and Polish law separately prohibits fire in a forest or within 100 m of one. Gas/liquid-fuel stove only — a certified gas stove is expressly allowed under the programme rules, BUT is forbidden when the daily fire-danger degree reaches level 3 (red), and is never permitted on peat, in young stands with canopy below 5 m, or among tall grass and heather. In a July 2026 drought, plan for the realistic possibility of a no-flame trip: carry food that works cold, and check the fire-danger forecast the morning of departure.

**Safety & Costs**

- **Rescue / insurance:** No insurance required and no rescue bill — the decisive cost advantage over every Slovak candidate. Mountain rescue in the Polish Beskids is provided by GOPR Grupa Beskidzka and is FREE at the point of use under the 2011 Act on safety and rescue in the mountains and on organised ski runs; there is no HZS-style invoice, no ~400 EUR ground-rescue charge and no 2,000-4,000 EUR helicopter charge as on the Slovak side. Call 601 100 300 (mountain emergency), 985, or 112; install the 'Ratunek' app, which transmits GPS coordinates. Private NNW/travel insurance is optional and worth having only for medical transport and equipment, not for rescue itself. Note the asymmetry on the border ridge: if you cross onto the Czech slope at Czantoria or Stożek you leave GOPR's free regime; Czech mountain rescue (Horská služba) is publicly funded for the rescue act but treatment/transport falls under EHIC — carry the EHIC card.
- **Bears / ticks:** BEARS: low concern here. The Beskid Śląski is NOT a permanent brown-bear breeding range — confirmed permanent reproduction in Poland is limited to the Bieszczady, Tatras and the neighbouring Beskid Żywiecki. Occasional transient bears do wander in from the Żywiec/Jałowiec area (multiple 2025 sightings there, including an agitated animal on the Jałowiec trail in May 2025), so standard hygiene still applies — cook away from the tent, hang or bag food, make noise in dense stands — but a bear-proof plan is not the driver of camp choice as it would be in Beskid Żywiecki or the Slovak ranges. Wolves and lynx are present and are a non-issue. TICKS ARE THE REAL RISK: Śląskie is one of the historically endemic TBE (KZM) regions of Poland, and incidence is climbing — 793 registered national cases in 2024 versus 659 in 2023, with śląskie among the voivodeships showing growth, and the virus now detected essentially nationwide. Late July is peak season, and forest-floor camping in exactly the habitat (beech/spruce edge, tall grass) is the highest-exposure activity there is. Mitigation: permethrin-treated clothing, DEET/icaridin on skin, long trousers tucked into socks, full body check every evening and again after the trip, tick removal tool in the kit. Vaccination is the only real protection against TBE and needs to be started well before the season — nothing you do on the trail prevents it. Lyme borreliosis risk is high and unvaccinable; watch for erythema migrans for weeks afterwards.
- **Weather / storms:** Typical July-August pattern: warm humid mornings, convective build-up from early afternoon, thunderstorms concentrated roughly 14:00-19:00, then clearing. Exposed ground here is limited — most of the GSB in this range is forested — but Czantoria, Równica, the Kubalonka open saddle and above all the Barania Góra summit ridge are lightning-exposed and should be cleared by about 15:00. Standard rule: be over the high point and heading down before mid-afternoon, which the recommended Day 2 supports if you start by 07:00-08:00. 2026-SPECIFIC: the season has combined severe drought (the trigger for the Ustroń bivouac suspension on 8 May) with violent squall lines — damaging storms on 28 June-1 July, 7 July and 19 July 2026 caused enough windthrow that dozens of forest districts imposed entry bans. Consequence for a forest bivouac: deadfall and leaning/hung-up trees are the top overnight hazard. Never pitch under a damaged crown, a leaning spruce, or in a fresh windthrow patch; prefer open forest roads and clearings. Forest air is otherwise a plus — the low altitude (max 1,220 m) means no snow, no cold-exposure risk, and mild nights around 10-14 °C.
- **Bail-out plan ⚠️:** Densest bailout network of any candidate — you are never far from a road, a shelter or a station. STAFFED SHELTERS on or beside the route: Równica, Czantoria (with a gondola down to Ustroń), Soszów, Stożek Wielki, Przysłop pod Baranią Górą (74 beds, ~35-45 PLN/person, a genuine bad-weather fallback with a roof and hot food). ROADS: DW941 crosses the ridge at Przełęcz Kubalonka with bus service to Wisła and Istebna; the Wisła Czarne valley road reaches Przysłop; the Ustroń Polana road reaches Równica. RAIL: line 190 to Wisła Głębce, Wisła Uzdrowisko, Ustroń Polana, Ustroń Zdrój and Ustroń — from most points on the route a 1-3 h descent puts you on a train home, which makes an aborted trip trivially cheap. GSM: coverage is generally good across the range because of the resort infrastructure and the summit transmitters (Skrzyczne, Czantoria); expect weak or absent signal deep in the Czarna Wisełka valley and in the Barania Góra reserve's northern combes [uncertain — no coverage-map verification]. Emergency numbers 601 100 300 / 985 / 112, 'Ratunek' app.
- **Total fees ⚠️:** Essentially FREE — the cheapest candidate on the list. 'Zanocuj w lesie' costs 0 PLN (no fee, no permit, no booking). There is no national-park entry ticket (contrast TPN 11 PLN/day). Realistic total for the recommended variant: two Koleje Śląskie tickets Ustroń <-> Wisła Głębce, roughly 8-14 PLN each [uncertain — 2026 fare not verified], so ~16-28 PLN for the whole weekend, plus whatever you choose to spend on tea or a bed at a shelter. Car variant: 0-30 PLN parking depending on where you leave it. Optional extras: a pitch at a valley campsite or shelter meadow 25-60 PLN/person if the forest zone is closed; a bunk at Przysłop ~35-45 PLN/person as a weather bailout. BUDGET LINE: 0-30 PLN typical, under 100 PLN even with a shelter night.

**Season, Crowds & Logistics**

- **Season fit:** Very well suited to late July-August, with one caveat. The range tops out at 1,220 m (Skrzyczne 1,257 m in the eastern part), so there is no snow, no altitude issue, no cold-exposure risk; nights around 10-14 °C mean a light bag suffices. Beech and spruce forest covers most of the route, giving shade on hot days — a real advantage over the open Slovak ridges in a heatwave. Trails are dry and firm in summer, so the notorious Beskid mud is a non-issue. The season-specific negatives are all consequences of a hot dry July: peak fire danger (which is what suspended the Ustroń bivouac zone and can suspend the gas stove too), dry ridge streams, peak tick activity, and afternoon storms. Wild blueberries and raspberries are at their best in late July. Verdict: seasonally excellent, legally fragile in exactly this season.
- **Crowd level:** The busiest range on the whole candidate list — Ustroń and Wisła form the most heavily visited resort cluster in the Polish Beskids. On a sunny July Saturday expect crowds on Równica, Czantoria (gondola-fed), Barania Góra, the Vistula springs trail and Przełęcz Kubalonka, plus family traffic on all the shelter approaches. That said, the crowd is concentrated in a predictable pattern: day-trippers on 10:00-17:00 out-and-back walks from the valleys to a single summit, almost all of them on the marked honeypot routes. The 'Zanocuj w lesie' compartments sit off those routes in ordinary working forest, and are quiet even in high season. A Friday-evening start is a strong structural advantage here: you are walking uphill while everyone else is driving home, you get the trail to yourself for the first evening, and you are already high when Saturday's wave arrives. Sunday morning in the Czarna Wisełka/Malinka valleys is likewise quiet before ~10:00.
- **Navigation / maps:** Marking: Polish system, white-colour-white horizontal stripes; the Główny Szlak Beskidzki is RED and starts in Ustroń, waymarked and signposted with walking times. Density of trails is high and junctions are signposted, so navigation is easy — the hard part is not the trail but the compartment boundaries. ESSENTIAL TOOL: the mBDL / Bank Danych o Lesie mobile app or map portal (bdl.lasy.gov.pl/portal/mapy) — it is the only way to confirm you are standing inside a listed 'Zanocuj w lesie' oddział, and the zone is defined by compartment number, not by a drawn blob; the Nadl. Wisła site also publishes an interactive map and a PDF annex to the designating order. SECOND ESSENTIAL: zakazywstepu.bdl.lasy.gov.pl for live forest-entry bans. GENERAL MAPS: mapa-turystyczna.pl (best Polish trail layer with kilometrage), mapy.cz (excellent coverage of Polish Beskids too, and indispensable if you cross onto the Czech ridge), OsmAnd or Locus Map with offline OSM for the field, plus a paper Compass/Sygnatura sheet for the Beskid Śląski as backup. PL/CZ DIFFERENCE on the border ridge: Czech KČT signposts give times not distances and use a different post design — trivial once noticed, but a reminder that Polish camping permissions stop at the border line.

> ⚠️ _Fields above contain self-flagged agent estimates/caveats (verify before departure): Distance / time from Ustroń, Parking, Public transport, Vignettes, Camping rules, 2026 status (live blockers), Actual spacing vs 15-25 km target, Fire / cooking, Bail-out plan, Total fees_

---

### 2. Beskid Żywiecki (Żywiec Beskids, PL — Worek Raczański / Lipowska–Rysianka group, excluding Babia Góra NP)

**Access & Travel**

- **Distance / time from Ustroń ⚠️:** Second-closest candidate after Beskid Śląski and Beskid Mały. Estimated road distances/times from Ustroń (map estimates, NOT verified in a routing engine — DATA SOURCE FLAG): Zwardoń ~55-65 km, ~1 h 00-1 h 15 (either the scenic line Wisła – Kubalonka – Istebna – Koniaków – Milówka, or the faster Skoczów – Bielsko – Żywiec – Milówka run on S52/DK69); Rycerka Górna / Rycerka Kolonia (Wielka Racza trailhead) ~65-70 km, ~1 h 10-1 h 20; Ujsoły ~75 km, ~1 h 20; Soblówka and Glinka (Rycerzowa / Krawców Wierch trailheads) ~80-90 km, ~1 h 25-1 h 45 — the last 15-20 km are slow single-carriageway valley roads through Milówka, Rajcza and Ujsoły with no bypass. Practical read: the Racza end (Zwardoń/Rycerka) is a genuine 'after work on Friday' drive; the Soblówka/Glinka end costs an extra 20-30 minutes and eats meaningfully into the daylight budget. Note there is no motorway on any variant, so no toll.
- **PL/SK/CZ border:** The entire main ridge of the Worek Raczański — Wielka Racza (1,236 m), Przełęcz Przegibek, Wielka Rycerzowa (1,226 m), Bendoszka Wielka, Przełęcz Glinka — runs exactly on the PL/SK state border, with Kysucké Beskydy and CHKO Kysuce on the southern side. Schengen: no checks, no passport, and marked crossings at Zwardoń, Przegibek and Przełęcz Glinka; you can and will step onto Slovak soil repeatedly along the ridge. THREE PRACTICAL CONSEQUENCES: (1) LEGAL — Polish 'Zanocuj w lesie' permissions and PTTK shelter pitches stop at the border; the Slovak side falls under zákon o lesoch 326/2005 and CHKO Kysuce rules, so do not drift 200 m south to 'find a better spot'. (2) RESCUE — GOPR's free service is Polish-side only; a callout from the Slovak slope goes to HZS, which invoices (see rescue_insurance). This is the single most expensive mistake available on this ridge. (3) NAVIGATION — Slovak KČT signposts give times rather than distances and the border trail is red on the Polish side; carry a map that shows both. No currency needed unless you deliberately descend to Nová Bystrica or Oščadnica.
- **Parking ⚠️:** Free or cheap and never a bottleneck — this is one of the least-visited corners of the Polish Beskids. Zwardoń: free lots by the railway station and the border crossing. Rycerka Górna Kolonia (Wielka Racza trailhead): roadside and small trailhead parking, free or a token fee. Soblówka (Rycerzowa trailhead) and Glinka (Krawców Wierch / Rycerzowa yellow trail): village roadside parking and small trailhead lots, generally free, occasionally a private meadow charging ~10-20 PLN/day [uncertain — no verified 2026 tariffs found]. Złatna Huta (Rysianka/Lipowska trailhead) and Żabnica-Skałka: small paid forest lots, ~10-15 PLN/day [uncertain]. Overnight standing is tolerated at the village and station lots; it is NOT permitted on Lasy Państwowe forest roads or behind barriers. Capacity/fill-time: nothing here behaves like Vrátna or Šútovo — even on a July Saturday the Racza and Rycerzowa trailheads have space at 10:00; the only lots that genuinely fill are Przełęcz Krowiarki and Zawoja on the Babia Góra side (excluded from this plan) and Złatna Huta for the popular Rysianka loop.
- **Public transport ⚠️:** Workable but clumsy — much weaker than Beskid Śląski. Rail: Koleje Śląskie line 139 runs Katowice – Bielsko-Biała – Żywiec – Węgierska Górka – Rajcza – Zwardoń, so ZWARDOŃ AND RAJCZA ARE ON THE RAIL NETWORK, which makes the Zwardoń -> Wielka Racza -> Rycerka Dolna traverse a genuine linear A->B with no car shuttle. However, from Ustroń this needs a change (Ustroń -> Bielsko-Biała or Czechowice-Dziedzice -> Zwardoń), realistically 2-2.5 h door-to-door with waits [uncertain — 2026 timetable not verified; check portalpasazera.pl]. Buses: local operators run Żywiec/Rajcza – Ujsoły – Soblówka – Glinka, but the service is sparse, mostly school-timed and thin at weekends, so Soblówka and Glinka are effectively car-only for a Friday-evening arrival [uncertain]. VERDICT: if you want the linear no-loop option, use the Zwardoń–Rycerka rail corridor; if you want the best camp spacing (Rycerzowa -> Krawców Wierch), plan a car-based LOOP back to Glinka rather than relying on a Sunday bus.
- **Vignettes ⚠️:** None. Every approach is within Poland on toll-free DK/DW roads (S52/DK69/DW945) and the Polish e-TOLL system applies only to vehicles over 3.5 t. No Slovak e-známka and no Czech e-vignette are required — the Zwardoń border crossing is right at the trailhead but you never need to drive through it. Only relevant if you deliberately shortcut via the Slovak D3 (Skalité – Čadca), which would need a Slovak e-známka (~12 EUR / 10 days [uncertain — 2026 tariff not re-verified]); there is no reason to do so, as the Polish valley roads reach every trailhead.

**Camping Legality**

- **Protection status:** NO national park in the planned area — deliberately so. The Racza/Rycerzowa and Lipowska/Rysianka groups sit inside Żywiecki Park Krajobrazowy, a LANDSCAPE park that restricts land use but does not ban hiking or tents, plus Natura 2000 sites and a scatter of strict reserves (Dziobaki, Pod Rysianką, Lipowska, Muńcuł, Butorza, Śrubita, Oszast, Romanka, Pilsko) where you must stay on the trail and may not camp. BABIOGÓRSKI PARK NARODOWY (Babia Góra, 1,725 m, a UNESCO biosphere reserve) is a genuine national park with an absolute camping ban and a paid entry ticket — it is EXCLUDED from this plan, which is the right call and also the reason the corridor cannot be extended eastwards past Przełęcz Jałowiecka. The binding regime in the planned area is therefore forestry law (Nadleśnictwa Ujsoły, Węgierska Górka, Jeleśnia) plus reserve boundaries, exactly like Beskid Śląski.
- **Camping rules ⚠️:** TWO INDEPENDENT LEGAL CHANNELS, which is what makes this range strong. (A) PAID PITCHES AT PTTK SHELTERS — the densest network of shelter camping meadows in the Polish Beskids, no reservation needed, arrive and pay: Bacówka PTTK na Rycerzowej (1,120 m, above Soblówka) ~18 PLN/person; Schronisko PTTK na Wielkiej Raczy (1,236 m) ~20 PLN/person [uncertain — not confirmed on the shelter's own 2026 site]; Bacówka PTTK na Krawcowym Wierchu (1,038 m) 15 PLN/person; Schronisko PTTK na Hali Lipowskiej (1,290 m) 10 PLN; Schronisko PTTK na Hali Rysiance ~30 PLN/person (PRICE HAS RISEN — the 20 PLN figure in the brief is stale; the shelter's own site now quotes 30 PLN, includes toilets, hot showers and boiling water, no reservations, and the field OPERATES ONLY UNTIL THE END OF SEPTEMBER); Schronisko PTTK na Hali Krupowej ~10 PLN [uncertain — site returned HTTP 403, price not re-verified]. Check-in typically from 16:00, checkout 10:00. (B) FREE WILD BIVOUAC UNDER 'ZANOCUJ W LESIE' — Nadleśnictwo Ujsoły runs an ACTIVE zone in Leśnictwa Okrągłe, Złatna and Glinka, i.e. the forested approaches beneath the Racza, Rycerzowa and Lipowska/Rysianka ridges, and unusually it designates fire sites (bring your own firewood). Nadleśnictwa Węgierska Górka and Jeleśnia also participate (Jeleśnia: Leśnictwa Czernichów, Kocierz Rychwałdzki, Zakocierz, Koszarawa Cicha). Standard programme rules: max 9 people, max 2 consecutive nights, no permit or fee below those limits; beyond them e-mail the district (Ujsoły: jaroslaw.jonkisz@katowice.lasy.gov.pl) at least 2 working days ahead and get written confirmation. Certified gas stove allowed but banned at fire-danger level 3; no vehicles; leave no trace; dogs leashed; nature reserves and signposted no-entry areas excluded. Outside these two channels, camping in State Forest is an offence (Ustawa o lasach art. 30).
- **2026 status (live blockers):** CHECKED 2026-07-28. Materially better than Beskid Śląski. (1) Nadleśnictwo Ujsoły's 'Zanocuj w lesie' programme is presented as ACTIVE on the district's own page, with no suspension notice found — in contrast to Nadleśnictwo Ustroń, which suspended its programme on 8 May 2026 over drought and extreme fire risk. Węgierska Górka and Jeleśnia also list active zones. (2) The paid shelter camping meadows are unaffected by the forestry suspensions and are the robust fallback: they are private/PTTK ground with sanitation, so a drought order that closes wild bivouac does not close them. Hala Rysianka's field closes at the end of September, which is irrelevant for a July trip. (3) National context: around 19 July 2026 roughly 29 of 429 forest districts had temporary forest-entry bans (zakazy wstępu), mostly windthrow damage from the storm episodes of 28 June-1 July, 7 July and 19 July 2026 — Ujsoły was not confirmed on that list, but the district separately warns about large-scale removal of dying spruce, which creates moving no-entry zones. (4) Babiogórski PN's new visitor regulations took effect 1 March 2026; camping remains banned there regardless. MANDATORY RE-CHECKS IN THE DEPARTURE WEEK: zakazywstepu.bdl.lasy.gov.pl; ujsoly.katowice.lasy.gov.pl programme page; a phone call to the two chosen bacówki to confirm the tent field is open and the price; the daily fire-danger degree (level 3 = no gas stove).
- **Hourly / seasonal restrictions ⚠️:** No time-of-day limits on hiking anywhere in the planned area — no national park, so nothing resembling the NP Malá Fatra 06:00-21:00 window, and no Czech-style 'pitch after 18:00 / strike before 09:00' rule. You may walk in after dark and arrive at a bacówka late; the practical limit is the shelter's own reception rather than the law (accommodation day typically 16:00-10:00, so an arrival at 20:00-21:00 is normal, but phone ahead if it will be much later). SEASONAL: Hala Rysianka's tent field operates only until the end of September; several bacówki close or reduce service outside the main season [uncertain]. Collective driven hunts run roughly October-January, signposted, with entry banned during them — irrelevant in July. Nature reserves (Pod Rysianką, Lipowska, Muńcuł, Romanka, Oszast, Śrubita and others) are trail-only, no camping, year-round. Active spruce-dieback logging in Nadl. Ujsoły can close individual compartments at short notice. If the plan ever extends over Babia Góra, note that BgPN has ticketed entry and its own regulations, with some summit routes seasonally or weather-closed.
- **Fine risk:** Same Polish regime as Beskid Śląski: camping outside a designated place in State Forests is a wykroczenie normally handled by a mandat karny of up to 500 PLN issued by Straż Leśna or Policja, with stacking offences (littering, damaging the litter layer, taking deadwood, disturbing wildlife, driving into the forest) pushing a combined mandate towards ~1,000 PLN, and court referral available for the aggravated cases (sources cite up to 5,000 PLN). Open fire in a forest or within 100 m of one is a separate and far graver offence, especially under the 2026 drought. Inside Babiogórski PN the penalties are national-park-grade and enforcement is active — another reason to leave Babia Góra out. RISK IN PRACTICE IS NEAR ZERO IF YOU USE EITHER LEGAL CHANNEL: a paid pitch at a bacówka is unimpeachable, and a bivouac inside a listed Nadl. Ujsoły compartment is expressly permitted. The one trap specific to this range is the border: pitching on the Slovak slope of the ridge exposes you to Slovak administrative fines under zákon o lesoch and, worse, to a paid HZS rescue.

**Route & Camps**

- **Suggested route:** PLAN A — 'THE ONE THAT ACTUALLY HITS 15-25 km' (car loop from Glinka/Soblówka): FRIDAY — drive Ustroń -> Soblówka or Glinka (~1 h 25-1 h 45), walk up the yellow/blue trail to Bacówka PTTK na Rycerzowej (1,120 m); ~4-5 km, +450-500 m, 1 h 15-1 h 45 -> Camp 1 on the bacówka's tent meadow (18 PLN). SATURDAY — the long ridge day: Rycerzowa -> Wielka Rycerzowa (1,226 m) -> Bendoszka Wielka -> Przełęcz Glinka -> blue border/ridge trail east, finishing on the yellow trail to Bacówka PTTK na Krawcowym Wierchu (1,038 m); published trail length between the two bacówki is 21.1 km / ~7 h 10 -> Camp 2 on the Krawcula meadow (15 PLN). SUNDAY — descend the yellow trail south-west to Glinka (~6-8 km, 2-2.5 h) closing the loop at the car; optional extension north over Trzy Kopce to Hala Rysianka/Hala Lipowska if you want a third summit before dropping to Złatna. Totals ~32-35 km, ~1,600-1,800 m ascent, technically easy but a genuinely long Saturday with a loaded pack. PLAN B — 'the classic, rail-friendly, shorter' (linear): FRIDAY drive/train to Rycerka Górna Kolonia or Zwardoń, walk up to Schronisko PTTK na Wielkiej Raczy (1,236 m) -> Camp 1 (20 PLN). SATURDAY the border ridge Wielka Racza -> Przełęcz Przegibek -> Wielka Rycerzowa -> Bacówka na Rycerzowej, 14.5 km / ~4 h 47 -> Camp 2 (18 PLN). SUNDAY descend to Soblówka/Rycerka Dolna (~6-8 km) and take the train from Rajcza/Zwardoń. This is the well-trodden 'two-day traverse of the Worek Raczański' (full ridge 50 km, the two-day version covering ~80% of it from Zwardoń to Rycerka). PLAN C — Hala Rysianka/Lipowska group from Złatna Huta: easiest and prettiest but the two shelters are 15 minutes apart, so it cannot produce useful camp spacing; treat it as a bad-weather or short-format fallback.
- **Friday-evening feasibility:** Feasible but tighter than Beskid Śląski, and the choice of trailhead decides it. Sunset in the Żywiec Beskids at the end of July is around 20:20-20:35 CEST with usable civil twilight to roughly 21:00-21:10 [approximate; verify for the exact date]. PLAN A budget: leave Ustroń 17:00-17:30, drive 1 h 25-1 h 45 -> Soblówka/Glinka at 18:30-19:15, walk 1 h 15-1 h 45 -> tent up 19:50-21:00. That is inside the guideline, but with almost no slack: a 18:00 departure means the last 30-45 minutes on a head torch, on an easy forest track with a lit bacówka as the target, which is acceptable. PLAN B budget is more comfortable on the drive (Zwardoń/Rycerka ~1 h 10) but the climb to Wielka Racza is longer (~1 h 30-2 h from Rycerka Kolonia), netting out about the same. MITIGATIONS specific to this range, and they are strong: both Camp 1 candidates are STAFFED SHELTERS, so the destination is lit, has water and a warm room, and the pitch does not have to be found in the dark — a materially lower-consequence late arrival than hunting for a legal forest compartment with a torch. Phone the bacówka in the afternoon to say you will arrive late. If departure slips past 18:30, switch Camp 1 to a Nadl. Ujsoły 'Zanocuj w lesie' compartment in Leśnictwo Glinka or Złatna low in the valley (10-20 minutes from the car) and start the ridge fresh on Saturday.
- **Camp 1 ⚠️:** BACÓWKA PTTK NA RYCERZOWEJ, 1,120 m, on Hala Rycerzowa above Soblówka in the Ujsoły commune, on the Polish side just below the border ridge between Wielka Rycerzowa and Bendoszka. Legal basis: a formally operated PTTK shelter camping meadow — pitching costs 18 PLN per person, no reservation, accommodation day 16:00-10:00, PTTK/large-family/blood-donor discounts apply to beds. Facilities: shelter kitchen and hot drinks, toilets, water, a bed as fallback (from ~38 PLN, floor space ~23 PLN, bedding 10 PLN if you have no sleeping bag) — which is exactly the insurance you want for a Friday-evening arrival. Access: ~1-1.5 h up from Soblówka (a well-known short loop), or a yellow trail from Glinka. Why here rather than a wild pitch: it is the only anchor within a realistic Friday-evening radius that is simultaneously legal, staffed, watered and positioned at the START of the 21 km ridge day. ALTERNATIVE (Plan B): Schronisko PTTK na Wielkiej Raczy, 1,236 m, tent field ~20 PLN/person [uncertain], sauna and full shelter service, reached from Zwardoń or Rycerka Kolonia. FREE ALTERNATIVE: any listed compartment of Leśnictwo Glinka or Złatna in the Nadl. Ujsoły 'Zanocuj w lesie' zone — 0 PLN, fires allowed at designated sites with your own wood, but no water and you must verify the compartment in mBDL.
- **Camp 2:** BACÓWKA PTTK NA KRAWCOWYM WIERCHU, 1,038 m, on the Krawcula meadow just below Krawców Wierch, inside Żywiecki Park Krajobrazowy. Legal basis: PTTK shelter tent meadow — 15 PLN per person to pitch in front of the bacówka (beds 40 PLN in the 10-bed room, 50 PLN in smaller rooms if the weather turns). This is the deliberate choice that makes the spacing work: the published blue-then-yellow trail link from Bacówka na Rycerzowej is 21.1 km / ~7 h 10, squarely inside the 15-25 km target. Access/egress: yellow trail south-west down to Glinka (closes the car loop), blue trail north-west down to Złatna, blue border trail up from Przełęcz Glinka, and a blue link north-east over Przełęcz Bory Orawskie and Trzy Kopce to Hala Rysianka (11.1 km / 3 h 40) if you want to continue. ALTERNATIVE (Plan B): Bacówka PTTK na Rycerzowej as Camp 2 instead of Camp 1, 14.5 km / 4 h 47 from Wielka Racza along the border ridge. FURTHER OPTIONS in the eastern group: Schronisko na Hali Lipowskiej (1,290 m, tent 10 PLN — the cheapest pitch in the range) and Schronisko na Hali Rysiance (tent 30 PLN, hot showers, no reservations, closes end of September); the two are only ~15 minutes apart, so pick one, not both. AVOID: anything east of Przełęcz Jałowiecka — that is Babiogórski PN, absolute camping ban.
- **Actual spacing vs 15-25 km target ⚠️:** THIS IS THE ONE POLISH RANGE ON THE SHORTLIST THAT CAN ACTUALLY MEET THE 15-25 km CRITERION, but only on one specific pairing. Measured against published trail data: Bacówka na Rycerzowej <-> Bacówka na Krawcowym Wierchu = 21.1 km / ~7 h 10 (blue trail east, final section yellow) — IN BAND, and comfortably so. Wielka Racza <-> Bacówka na Rycerzowej = 14.5 km / ~4 h 47 — just BELOW band, and note this is meaningfully longer than the ~12 km assumed in the brief, because the marked ridge line via Przegibek and Wielka Rycerzowa is not the straight line. Bacówka na Krawcowym Wierchu <-> Hala Lipowska = 11.1 km / 3 h 40 — below band. Hala Rysianka <-> Hala Lipowska = ~1 km / 15 min — far below band, effectively the same camp. Hala Rysianka <-> Hala Miziowa = ~10 km — below band. Whole-ridge context: the Worek Raczański main ridge is ~50 km end to end, and the standard two-day Zwardoń -> Rycerka Dolna traverse covers ~80% of it (~40 km, i.e. ~20 km/day) — another in-band configuration if you accept a linear route with rail return. DISTANCES ARE PUBLISHED TRAIL FIGURES from mapa-turystyczna.pl route data and shelter/tourism sources, not personally GPS-measured; treat as ±1-2 km. HONEST CAVEAT: the corridor dead-ends at Przełęcz Jałowiecka because Babiogórski PN forbids camping, so you cannot chain a third 20 km day eastwards without a valley night.
- **Water sources:** Comfortable by the standards of this shortlist, because the camps ARE the water points. RELIABLE: every PTTK shelter and bacówka on the plan (Wielka Racza, Przegibek, Rycerzowa, Krawców Wierch, Lipowska, Rysianka, Krupowa) sells drinks and has running or well water; Hala Rysianka explicitly offers hot showers and boiling water to tent campers. Spacing between them on the recommended Saturday is 3-5 h, which is manageable. UNRELIABLE: the border ridge itself between Przełęcz Glinka and Krawców Wierch is a dry crest — the springs shown on maps below the ridge are seasonal and are the first casualties of the 2026 drought; do not plan on them. Valley streams in the Ujsoła, Bystra, Glinka and Złatna catchments are permanent and drinkable after filtering/boiling, which is a further argument for the Nadl. Ujsoły free-bivouac compartments if you go that route. PLANNING RULE: fill 2.5-3 L at the Camp 1 bacówka before starting the 21 km ridge day, top up at Krawców Wierch on arrival, and carry a filter for the descents. Unlike Veľká Fatra, running dry here is a discomfort rather than an emergency, because a staffed shelter is rarely more than 3 h away.
- **Fire / cooking ⚠️:** Better than most candidates, but drought-dependent. AT THE SHELTERS: Hala Rysianka provides two fire pits for tent campers, on condition you bring or gather dry branches yourself; other bacówki generally tolerate a stove and may have a fire ring [uncertain per shelter — ask on arrival]. IN THE 'ZANOCUJ W LESIE' ZONE: unusually, Nadleśnictwo Ujsoły designates fire sites within its programme area and asks you to bring your own firewood — one of the few places on this shortlist where a legal campfire is even theoretically available. EVERYWHERE ELSE: no open fire; fire in a forest or within 100 m of one is a serious offence. GAS STOVE: permitted, but forbidden at fire-danger degree 3 (red), and never on peat, in young stands under 5 m canopy, or in tall grass/heather. 2026 REALITY CHECK: this has been a drought year severe enough to suspend the neighbouring Ustroń bivouac programme outright, and neighbouring districts have declared absolute fire prohibitions — plan the menu so it survives a total no-flame ruling, and check the daily fire-danger degree on the morning of departure.

**Safety & Costs**

- **Rescue / insurance ⚠️:** Free on the Polish side, expensive one ridge-crest to the south — and this range puts you on that crest all Saturday. POLAND: GOPR Grupa Beskidzka covers the whole area and rescue is free at the point of use under the 2011 Act on safety and rescue in the mountains; call 601 100 300, 985 or 112, and install the 'Ratunek' app for GPS transmission. No insurance is required. SLOVAKIA: the moment you are on the southern slope, Horská záchranná služba (HZS) applies and it INVOICES — commonly quoted at roughly 400 EUR for a ground callout and 2,000-4,000 EUR with a helicopter. Because the marked border trail weaves back and forth across the line between Wielka Racza and Przełęcz Glinka, a travel/mountain policy covering Slovakia is cheap insurance for this specific itinerary: a standard Polish NNW+KL mountain rider with a rescue-cost limit of at least 30,000-50,000 PLN (ideally 100,000 PLN if a helicopter is in scope) costs a few złoty per day [uncertain — no 2026 quotes verified]. Also carry an EHIC card for Slovak/Czech treatment. Practical rule if something happens near the crest: if you can move at all, move NORTH before calling.
- **Bears / ticks:** THE BEAR RANGE ON THIS SHORTLIST — treat this as a planning input, not a footnote. Beskid Żywiecki is one of only three areas in Poland with confirmed PERMANENT brown-bear reproduction (alongside the Bieszczady and the Tatras); camera traps regularly record females, males and cubs across the range, and 2025 produced repeated trail sightings, including a hiker's encounter with an agitated bear on the Jałowiec trail on 4 May 2025 and public appeals for caution on the Żywiec Beskids trails. The Racza/Rycerzowa/Oszast forests are exactly the quiet, low-traffic habitat bears use. PRACTICAL PROTOCOL: camping at a staffed bacówka (the recommended plan) largely removes the problem — human presence, lighting and proper waste disposal. If wild-camping in the Ujsoły zone: cook and eat at least 50-100 m downwind of the tent, never store food or scented items in the tent, bag or hang food, make noise on overgrown trails and in dense stands, never walk in silence at dusk, and never approach or photograph a bear. If you meet one: do not run, do not turn your back, back away slowly with your head lowered while talking calmly. Wolves and lynx are also present and are not a threat to people. TICKS: śląskie is a historically endemic TBE (KZM) region and incidence is rising nationally — 793 registered cases in 2024 versus 659 in 2023, with śląskie among the growing voivodeships. Late July is peak season and meadow camping on hale is prime habitat. Use permethrin-treated clothing plus DEET/icaridin, tuck trousers into socks, do a full body check every evening, carry a tick tool, and remember that TBE vaccination is the only genuine protection and must be started long before the trip. Lyme risk is high and cannot be vaccinated against.
- **Weather / storms:** Classic Carpathian July-August convection: clear humid mornings, cumulus building from late morning, thunderstorms clustered roughly 14:00-19:00, clearing by evening. This range is more exposed than Beskid Śląski because Saturday's route spends hours on open border ridge and hale (Wielka Rycerzowa, Bendoszka, the Krawcula and Lipowska meadows) where you are the highest object around — apply the discipline of being off the crest and into forest by about 15:00, which the 21 km day demands anyway (start 07:00, at Krawców Wierch by 15:00-16:00). Wind on the crest is stronger and colder than the valleys suggest; carry a proper shell even in a heatwave. 2026-SPECIFIC: a severe-drought season punctuated by damaging squall lines (28 June-1 July, 7 July, 19 July 2026) that produced enough windthrow to trigger entry bans in dozens of forest districts, on top of ongoing spruce dieback in Nadl. Ujsoły — so standing dead and wind-damaged spruce is a real overnight hazard. Never pitch under a dead or leaning spruce; the bacówka meadows are open ground and therefore safer than any forest pitch in this respect. Night temperatures at 1,000-1,250 m in late July typically 8-13 °C.
- **Bail-out plan ⚠️:** Excellent — the shelter density IS the bailout plan, which is the strongest safety argument for this range. Staffed refuges on or beside the route, each with a roof, hot food, beds and a landline/phone: Schronisko PTTK na Wielkiej Raczy (1,236 m), Schronisko PTTK na Przełęczy Przegibek, Bacówka PTTK na Rycerzowej (1,120 m; beds from ~38 PLN, floor ~23 PLN), Bacówka PTTK na Krawcowym Wierchu (1,038 m; beds 40-50 PLN), Schronisko PTTK na Hali Lipowskiej (1,290 m), Schronisko PTTK na Hali Rysiance, Schronisko PTTK Hala Miziowa (1,330 m, the highest in the Polish Beskids). ROADS: Przełęcz Przegibek is reachable by vehicle from Rycerka Górna; valley roads run up Soblówka, Glinka, Złatna, Ujsoły and Żabnica, so a descent of 1-2 h from almost anywhere on the ridge reaches tarmac. RAIL: Koleje Śląskie line 139 at Rajcza, Rajcza Centrum, Węgierska Górka and Zwardoń — an aborted trip is cheap to escape. GSM: generally usable on the ridge and at the shelters, patchy-to-absent in the deep Ujsoła/Glinka side valleys and on north-facing slopes [uncertain — no coverage-map verification]. Border note: on the crest your phone may register onto a Slovak network — check you are not roaming before dialling, and prefer 112 (works on any network). Emergency: GOPR 601 100 300 / 985 / 112, 'Ratunek' app.
- **Total fees ⚠️:** Cheap and predictable. NO park entry ticket in the planned area (Żywiecki Park Krajobrazowy is free; Babia Góra with its 10 PLN / 5 PLN reduced 2026 ticket is deliberately excluded). Realistic totals per person: PLAN A (Rycerzowa 18 PLN + Krawców Wierch 15 PLN) = 33 PLN for two nights; PLAN B (Wielka Racza ~20 PLN + Rycerzowa 18 PLN) = ~38 PLN; cheapest configuration (Hala Lipowska 10 PLN + a free 'Zanocuj w lesie' night) = 10 PLN; most expensive (Hala Rysianka 30 PLN + a bacówka) = ~45-48 PLN. Add parking 0-20 PLN [uncertain] and fuel for ~150-180 km round trip (~90-120 PLN per car). Optional bad-weather upgrade to a bunk: 23-50 PLN/person. Optional Slovak-side rescue insurance a few złoty per day. BUDGET LINE: 35-60 PLN per person for the weekend excluding fuel and food — the cheapest 'serviced' option on the whole shortlist, and roughly a tenth of what a Slovak variant with mandatory rescue cover would cost if anything went wrong.

**Season, Crowds & Logistics**

- **Season fit:** Very good for late July-August. Altitudes 1,000-1,300 m mean warm days, mild nights (8-13 °C), no snow and no altitude issue, while the extensive hale (Rycerzowa, Krawcula, Lipowska, Rysianka) give open views and drying breezes that the fully-forested Beskid Śląski lacks. Trails are firm and dry in summer — this range is notoriously muddy in spring and autumn, so July is arguably its best window. Blueberries and raspberries are abundant on the hale in late July. Shelter service is at full summer staffing and the Rysianka tent field is open (it closes end of September). Season-specific negatives: peak fire danger and the drought-related restrictions that come with it, peak tick activity, peak bear activity on the berry crop (bears feed heavily on bilberry in July-August, which puts them on exactly the open hale you are walking across), and afternoon storms on exposed crests. Net: seasonally the right choice, with bears and ticks as the two things to actively manage.
- **Crowd level:** The quietest well-served range on the shortlist — a genuine differentiator. The Worek Raczański (Zwardoń – Wielka Racza – Przegibek – Rycerzowa – Krawców Wierch) is one of the least-visited corners of the Polish Beskids: peripheral location, no gondola, no big resort, long valley approaches, and no summit that draws mass day-tripping. Expect to have most of Saturday's 21 km ridge largely to yourself even in high season, and to find space on the bacówka meadows without booking. The exceptions are concentrated and easy to avoid: Hala Rysianka and Hala Lipowska pull steady weekend traffic from the popular Złatna Huta loop, Hala Miziowa/Pilsko is busy, and Babia Góra (excluded) is the single most crowded mountain in the region. A Friday-evening start compounds the advantage — you climb while the day crowd descends, and you are already at Camp 1 when Saturday's trickle starts. Shelter beds do fill on peak weekends, so treat a bunk as a fallback to phone about rather than assume.
- **Navigation / maps:** Marking: Polish system, white-colour-white stripes with signposted times and distances; the border ridge trail is RED, the Rycerzowa <-> Krawców Wierch link is BLUE finishing on YELLOW, and the descents to Glinka/Złatna/Soblówka are yellow and blue. Trail density is lower than in Beskid Śląski and some junctions on the ridge are sparse, so this is one place where a downloaded track genuinely helps. RECOMMENDED STACK: mapa-turystyczna.pl (best Polish trail layer and the source of the 21.1 km and 14.5 km figures used above — plan the route there and export GPX), mapy.cz (renders both sides of the border, essential because half the ridge is Slovak), OsmAnd or Locus Map with offline OSM for the field, and mBDL / bdl.lasy.gov.pl to confirm any 'Zanocuj w lesie' compartment before pitching. Also bookmark zakazywstepu.bdl.lasy.gov.pl for live entry bans. PL/SK DIFFERENCE: Slovak KČT signposts give walking times rather than kilometres and use a different post design; the colour semantics are similar but the numbering is not, so do not mix a Slovak time estimate with a Polish distance when planning the long Saturday. Paper backup: Compass or Sygnatura sheet for Beskid Żywiecki, scale 1:50,000.

> ⚠️ _Fields above contain self-flagged agent estimates/caveats (verify before departure): Distance / time from Ustroń, Parking, Public transport, Vignettes, Camping rules, Hourly / seasonal restrictions, Camp 1, Actual spacing vs 15-25 km target, Fire / cooking, Rescue / insurance, Bail-out plan, Total fees_

---

### 3. Beskid Mały (Little Beskids, PL — Magurka Wilkowicka / Żar / Kocierz / Leskowiec groups)

**Access & Travel**

- **Distance / time from Ustroń ⚠️:** The fastest approach of any candidate with a workable legal camp, and the reason this range owns the 'leave at 18:00, tent up at 21:00' scenario. Estimated road distances/times from Ustroń (map estimates, NOT verified in a routing engine — DATA SOURCE FLAG): Bielsko-Biała Straconka, the western terminus of the Mały Szlak Beskidzki, ~30-35 km, ~35-40 min (DW941 Ustroń – Skoczów then DK52/S52 through Bielsko); Wilkowice/Przełęcz Przegibek ~35 km, ~40 min; Międzybrodzie Bialskie and the Żar area ~45-50 km, ~50-60 min (via Bielsko and Porąbka, or via Żywiec); Przełęcz Kocierska ~55 km, ~1 h 05; Rzyki-Praciaki / Andrychów (Leskowiec trailheads) ~65-70 km, ~1 h 10-1 h 20; Zembrzyce, the eastern edge of the Beskid Mały section, ~85 km, ~1 h 30. Practical read: the western half (Straconka, Magurka, Hrobacza Łąka) is genuinely 35-40 minutes away, but the nearest ACTIVE legal wild-bivouac ground sits in the Międzybrodzie/Andrychów half, ~50-60 min out — so budget an hour, not forty minutes, if you want to camp legally.
- **PL/SK/CZ border:** Not applicable — Beskid Mały lies entirely within Poland, in the Śląskie and Małopolskie voivodeships, and no part of the range or of any sensible route touches a state border. The nearest border (PL/SK at Zwardoń, PL/CZ at Cieszyn) is 30-50 km away and irrelevant to this plan. Consequences, all positive: one legal regime throughout, one set of trail markings, one currency, GOPR's free rescue with no HZS exposure anywhere on the route, and no vignette or crossing logistics. This is the only candidate on the shortlist with zero cross-border complexity — a real simplification for a short after-work trip.
- **Parking ⚠️:** Cheap, plentiful and never a constraint. Bielsko-Biała Straconka (MSB kilometre 0): free street parking by the church and the trailhead, in a residential district. Wilkowice / Przełęcz Przegibek: free trailhead lay-bys. Międzybrodzie Bialskie: lakeside parking, partly paid in summer (~10-15 PLN/day at the busiest lots near the water) [uncertain — no verified 2026 tariffs], with free roadside space further from the shore. Góra Żar lower station of the funicular: paid lot [uncertain]. Przełęcz Kocierska (DW781): free lay-by on the pass, the single most useful mid-range trailhead. Rzyki-Praciaki (Leskowiec): ski-station lot, free or nominal in summer. Zembrzyce and Sucha Beskidzka: free station parking. Overnight standing is tolerated at village and pass lay-bys but not on Lasy Państwowe forest roads or behind barriers. Fill-time is not a real issue anywhere — the only lots that get busy are the Międzybrodzie lakeshore on a hot Saturday and the Rzyki ski lot during the Leskowiec pilgrimage traffic; both are irrelevant to a Friday-evening arrival.
- **Public transport ⚠️:** Good, and better than Beskid Żywiecki because Bielsko-Biała is the hub for both ends. Western end: Koleje Śląskie / Polregio trains Ustroń -> Bielsko-Biała Główna in roughly 25-35 min [uncertain — 2026 timetable not verified], then a city bus to Straconka or Wilkowice; this makes a car-free start realistic. Eastern end: Andrychów and Wadowice sit on rail line 117 (Bielsko-Biała – Kalwaria Zebrzydowska – Kraków), and Zembrzyce / Sucha Beskidzka on the Sucha line, so an east-bound linear MSB walk can be closed by train back through Bielsko. Local buses run Bielsko – Porąbka – Międzybrodzie – Żywiec and Andrychów – Rzyki, but weekend service is thin and evening departures are scarce [uncertain]. VERDICT: a true linear A->B Straconka -> Leskowiec -> Andrychów weekend is doable entirely on public transport with one change in Bielsko, which no other candidate except Beskid Śląski offers. For a Friday-evening start, however, driving is faster and more reliable, and the range is close enough that the car is not a burden.
- **Vignettes:** None. Every approach is within Poland on toll-free roads — DW941, DK52, the S1/S52 Bielsko expressway ring and DW781 over Przełęcz Kocierska are all free for passenger cars, and Polish e-TOLL applies only to vehicles above 3.5 t. No Slovak e-známka, no Czech e-vignette, no border tolls. Total road-charge cost for the weekend: 0 PLN. Fuel for a 100-140 km round trip is the only vehicle cost.

**Camping Legality**

- **Protection status:** NO national park, and the most permissive protection regime on the whole shortlist — which is exactly why this range is the low-friction option. The area is covered by Park Krajobrazowy Beskidu Małego (Little Beskids Landscape Park, established 1998, roughly 25-26 thousand ha plus buffer zone), a LANDSCAPE park that constrains land use and development but does not prohibit hiking or pitching a tent. Overlaid are Natura 2000 sites and a small number of strict reserves where you must stay on the marked trail and may not camp: MADOHORA (over 70 ha, strict protection / ochrona ścisła, covering the summit and slopes of Łamana Skała, 929 m — 150-year-old spruce, mature beech and rock outcrops, established 1960), plus Szeroka w Beskidzie Małym, Zasolnica and a few smaller ones. The binding constraint everywhere else is ordinary forestry law under Nadleśnictwo Bielsko (western part) and Nadleśnictwo Andrychów (central and eastern part). Net effect: no entry ticket, no time window, no park ranger regime — only the Forest Act and the reserve boundaries.
- **Camping rules ⚠️:** DEFAULT: Ustawa o lasach art. 30 forbids camping in State Forests outside designated places. THE LEGAL CHANNEL here is 'Zanocuj w lesie', and Beskid Mały is served by TWO districts. (A) NADLEŚNICTWO ANDRYCHÓW — the one that actually matters, covering the central and eastern range. Zone spans three leśnictwa: MIĘDZYBRODZIE (the Żar / Międzybrodzie Bialskie–Żywieckie block, right on the Mały Szlak Beskidzki), ROZTOKA, and ŁĘKAWICA (with the Beskid Mały portion reported around Jaroszowicka Góra and Mucharz). Distinctive and useful: the district states that GAS COOKERS ARE PERMITTED YEAR-ROUND across its area under the programme rules — an explicit permission most districts do not spell out. Standard programme limits apply: max 9 people at one spot, max 2 consecutive nights, no fee and no notification below those limits; beyond them submit the registration form by e-mail at least 2 working days ahead and get written confirmation (programme coordinator Patryk Chlebek, tel. 666 663 169). (B) NADLEŚNICTWO BIELSKO — its zone is in Dolina Wapienicy and Jaworze Nałęże, with TWO DESIGNATED CAMPFIRE SITES (Jaworze Nałęże by the shelter near the chapel, and Dębowiec behind the hut). Note carefully: those two areas are geographically in the BESKID ŚLĄSKI, not Beskid Mały, so the western Beskid Mały (Magurka Wilkowicka, Hrobacza Łąka, Czupel) appears to have NO active wild-bivouac zone [uncertain — not positively confirmed]. Excluded everywhere: nature reserves (Madohora above all), signposted no-entry areas, and active logging blocks. Second channel: staffed huts — Chatka pod Potrójną (884 m, private, year-round, kitchen and fireplace room) and Schronisko PTTK na Leskowcu (32 beds); neither advertises a formal tent meadow [uncertain — phone ahead], so treat them as roofs and water points rather than camp spots.
- **2026 status (live blockers) ⚠️:** CHECKED 2026-07-28 — the healthiest legal picture of the four Polish near-range candidates. (1) Nadleśnictwo Andrychów's 'Zanocuj w lesie' programme page is dated 12 March 2026 and shows the programme as ACTIVE, with NO suspension notice and no drought or fire-hazard alert — a direct contrast with neighbouring Nadleśnictwo Ustroń, which suspended its programme on 8 May 2026 over extreme fire risk. (2) Nadleśnictwo Bielsko also lists an active zone (Wapienica / Jaworze Nałęże) [uncertain — suspension status not positively re-verified for July 2026]. (3) National context: around 19 July 2026 roughly 29 of 429 forest districts had temporary forest-entry bans in force, mostly windthrow damage from the storm episodes of 28 June-1 July, 7 July and 19 July 2026; Andrychów was not confirmed on that list. (4) The districts themselves direct users to check zakazywstepu.bdl.lasy.gov.pl before every trip and to verify the daily fire-danger degree, since level 3 (red) suspends even gas stoves under the national programme rules. MANDATORY RE-CHECK IN THE DEPARTURE WEEK, in order: zakazywstepu.bdl.lasy.gov.pl; andrychow.katowice.lasy.gov.pl (and bielsko.katowice.lasy.gov.pl if using Wapienica); the fire-danger forecast; mBDL to confirm your pitch sits inside a listed compartment. Because this range is a 40-60 minute drive, a same-week or even same-day status check is cheap and the trip can be re-pointed at short notice.
- **Hourly / seasonal restrictions:** Essentially none — the most unconstrained schedule on the shortlist. No national park means no entry window (nothing like the NP Malá Fatra 06:00-21:00 limit), no ticket office hours, and no Czech-style 'pitch after 18:00, strike before 09:00' rule. You may walk in after dark and pitch inside a 'Zanocuj w lesie' compartment at any hour, which is precisely what makes the Friday-evening scenario work here. Real constraints, all minor: nature reserves (Madohora, Szeroka, Zasolnica) are trail-only year-round with no bivouac at any time; collective driven hunts run roughly October-January with signposted temporary closures (irrelevant in July); active logging blocks are signposted and move; the Góra Żar funicular and the Rzyki ski lifts run to published seasonal hours if you intend to use them; and the fire-danger degree can suspend gas stoves on any given day. Chatka pod Potrójną is open year-round with permanent staff, so the bad-weather roof is not seasonal.
- **Fine risk:** Standard Polish regime, low absolute exposure. Camping in State Forests outside a designated place is a wykroczenie normally handled by a mandat karny of up to 500 PLN issued by Straż Leśna or Policja, with stacking offences (littering, damaging the litter layer, taking deadwood, disturbing wildlife, driving into the forest) pushing a combined mandate toward ~1,000 PLN and court referral available for aggravated cases (sources cite up to 5,000 PLN for the heavier forest offences). Open fire in a forest or within 100 m of one is a separate and much more serious matter. Two range-specific notes: (a) Beskid Mały sits between Bielsko-Biała, Andrychów and Wadowice, so forest roads see regular Straż Leśna and municipal traffic — this is not a place where an illegal pitch goes unnoticed; (b) camping inside the Madohora reserve carries nature-conservation penalties on top of the forestry fine. RISK IN PRACTICE IS NEAR ZERO if you stay inside a listed Nadleśnictwo Andrychów compartment, which is free, verifiable in mBDL and explicitly permits a gas stove.

**Route & Camps**

- **Suggested route:** PLAN A — 'legal anchor first', the recommended version. FRIDAY: drive Ustroń -> Międzybrodzie Bialskie / Zarzecze (~50-60 min), walk up toward Żar (761 m) into the Leśnictwo Międzybrodzie 'Zanocuj w lesie' block; ~3-5 km, +300-400 m, 1-1.5 h -> Camp 1, free, legal, gas stove allowed. SATURDAY: pick up the Mały Szlak Beskidzki (red) eastwards — Żar -> Kiczera -> Kocierz -> Przełęcz Kocierska (DW781) -> Potrójna -> Chatka pod Potrójną (884 m, water, kitchen, coffee) -> Łamana Skała / Madohora (929 m — the trail crosses the reserve, so stay on it and do not camp) -> Groń Jana Pawła II (890 m) -> Schronisko PTTK na Leskowcu; roughly 23-27 km, ~900-1,100 m ascent, 8-9 h with a pack -> Camp 2 in the Leśnictwo Łękawica / Roztoka bivouac ground below Leskowiec (the shelter itself has no confirmed tent meadow). SUNDAY: descend to Rzyki and Andrychów (~8-10 km, 2.5-3 h) and take the train back via Bielsko, or arrange a pickup. Totals ~35-42 km. PLAN B — 'the 18:00 departure, short and certain'. FRIDAY as above but stop at the first legal compartment (~45-60 min from the car); SATURDAY only as far as Przełęcz Kocierska / Chatka pod Potrójną, ~15-18 km; SUNDAY descend to Kocierz or Targanice. Less mileage, far more slack, and the whole thing collapses gracefully if the weather turns. PLAN C — 'the classic west-end MSB', Straconka -> Magurka Wilkowicka (912 m) -> Hrobacza Łąka (828 m) -> Żar -> Kiczera -> Kocierz -> Przełęcz Kocierska, the published 24.7 km / ~8 h MSB segment: scenically the best introduction to the range and the shortest drive (35-40 min), BUT its first half runs through Nadleśnictwo Bielsko territory where no Beskid Mały bivouac zone could be confirmed — treat Plan C as a day-walk or accept a hut night. Reference figures for the whole trail: MSB is 135.3 km of red waymarking from Bielsko-Biała Straconka to Luboń Wielki, and the published Zembrzyce – Gołuszkowa Góra – Leskowiec – Łamana Skała – Potrójna – Przełęcz Kocierska segment is 29.6 km / 9.5 h.
- **Friday-evening feasibility:** THE BEST FRIDAY-EVENING PROFILE ON THE SHORTLIST, and the scenario in the brief ('leave 18:00, tent up by 21:00') is not optimistic here — it is the normal case. Sunset in the Bielsko area at the end of July is around 20:25-20:40 CEST with usable civil twilight to roughly 21:00-21:10 [approximate; verify for the exact date]. Plan A budget: leave Ustroń 18:00, drive 50-60 min -> at the Międzybrodzie trailhead 18:50-19:05, walk 1-1.5 h on forest roads -> tent up 20:00-20:35, in daylight, with time to cook before dark. Even a 19:00 departure lands you in camp by ~21:15 with at most 30 minutes of head-torch walking on an easy track. Plan C's western trailhead (Straconka) is only 35-40 min away, buying another 20 minutes of margin, but at the cost of legal certainty. Three structural advantages compound this: (1) the approach is short enough that a late finish at work does not kill the trip; (2) the range is low (max 933 m) so the climb to a camp is 300-450 m rather than 700-900 m; (3) if everything goes wrong you are 50 minutes from home and can simply drive back — the cheapest abort of any candidate. This is the only option on the list that tolerates a genuinely late Friday.
- **Camp 1:** LEŚNICTWO MIĘDZYBRODZIE 'ZANOCUJ W LESIE' BLOCK, Nadleśnictwo Andrychów — the forested slopes above Międzybrodzie Bialskie and Międzybrodzie Żywieckie on the Żar (761 m) / Kiczera side, directly beneath the Mały Szlak Beskidzki. Legal basis: one of the three leśnictwa (Międzybrodzie, Roztoka, Łękawica) named in the district's active 'Zanocuj w lesie' designation — free, no booking, no fee, max 9 people, max 2 consecutive nights, and gas cookers explicitly permitted year-round across this district. Character: mixed beech-spruce stands with a dense network of forest roads and flat shelves; quiet, because the day-trippers are down at the Międzybrodzkie lake or riding the Żar funicular. Approach 1-1.5 h from the village, so it is reachable in daylight after an 18:00 departure. VERIFY IN mBDL before pitching — the designation is by leśnictwo and compartment, and the adjacent Nadleśnictwo Bielsko ground is NOT covered. CARRY WATER UP: these are dry slopes, fill in the village. FASTEST-POSSIBLE ALTERNATIVE if you leave very late: the Nadleśnictwo Bielsko zone in Dolina Wapienicy is only ~30-35 min from Ustroń and has a legal pitch within an hour of the car — geographically it is Beskid Śląski rather than Beskid Mały, but it is the single quickest legal tent on the whole shortlist.
- **Camp 2 ⚠️:** LEŚNICTWO ŁĘKAWICA / ROZTOKA 'ZANOCUJ W LESIE' GROUND beneath the Leskowiec – Groń Jana Pawła II – Jaroszowicka Góra ridge, Nadleśnictwo Andrychów — the eastern end of Saturday's walk, below Schronisko PTTK na Leskowcu and within a few kilometres of the summit. Legal basis: same active district designation (Łękawica is named explicitly, with the Beskid Mały portion reported around Jaroszowicka Góra and Mucharz), free, max 9 people / 2 nights, gas stove permitted. Water and hot food at Schronisko PTTK na Leskowcu (32 beds, home cooking, tel. 33 872 16 94 / 606 743 880) a short walk above; the shelter does NOT advertise a tent meadow, so use it as a kitchen and a foul-weather fallback, not as a pitch [uncertain — worth phoning to ask]. HARD EXCLUSION: do not camp anywhere inside the MADOHORA reserve around Łamana Skała (929 m) — strict protection, trail-only; drop clearly off the reserve before looking for ground. MID-ROUTE ALTERNATIVE that shortens Saturday to ~15-18 km: the Leśnictwo Roztoka block near Przełęcz Kocierska / Potrójna, with CHATKA POD POTRÓJNĄ (884 m, ul. Turystyczna 4, Zakocierz, tel. 501 694 158) 200 m below the red trail as the water point and bad-weather roof — privately run, staffed, open year-round, guest kitchen with stove and fridge, fireplace room, ~45 beds. This is the single most useful building on the route.
- **Actual spacing vs 15-25 km target ⚠️:** This range can reach the 15-25 km band, but only in the Plan A configuration, and it does so by exploiting a long ridge rather than by having well-spaced anchors. Estimated spacings (map/published-segment estimates, NOT GPS-measured — treat as ±2-3 km): Międzybrodzie/Żar block -> Leskowiec/Łękawica block along the MSB = roughly 23-27 km, IN BAND at its upper end and arguably a hard day with a full pack; Międzybrodzie/Żar block -> Roztoka block near Przełęcz Kocierska / Potrójna = roughly 15-18 km, IN BAND at its lower end and the more comfortable choice. Published reference segments used for the interpolation: Przełęcz Kocierska – Kocierz – Kiczera – Żar – Hrobacza Łąka – Bielsko-Biała Straconka = 24.7 km / ~8 h; Zembrzyce – Gołuszkowa Góra – Leskowiec – Łamana Skała – Potrójna – Przełęcz Kocierska = 29.6 km / 9.5 h; whole MSB = 135.3 km. HONEST CAVEAT, in the spirit of the brief: unlike Beskid Żywiecki, where two shelters happen to sit 21.1 km apart on a published trail, Beskid Mały has NO fixed legal anchors at a measured spacing — the 'Zanocuj w lesie' permission is granted over whole leśnictwa, so the spacing is whatever you choose to walk between two compartments. That is a feature (total flexibility, you can dial the day to exactly 20 km) and a weakness (nothing verifies your pitch for you, and you must confirm every compartment in mBDL). The range is only ~35 km long end to end, so the ceiling for a two-camp trip is roughly 30 km between camps.
- **Water sources ⚠️:** THE WEAKEST POINT OF THIS RANGE — plan for it. The Mały Szlak Beskidzki runs along ridge crests where water occurs rarely; through-hikers report carrying 2 L as the working reserve for a several-hour stage, and the ridges between Magurka Wilkowicka, Żar, Kocierz, Potrójna and Leskowiec have essentially no reliable natural source. RELIABLE POINTS, and they are buildings rather than springs: Chatka pod Potrójną (884 m, staffed year-round, guest kitchen, roughly mid-route — the single most important water stop on Saturday), Schronisko PTTK na Leskowcu, Dom Turystyczny on Hrobacza Łąka [uncertain — service hours not verified], and the villages of Międzybrodzie, Kocierz, Zakocierz, Rzyki and Targanice. Jezioro Międzybrodzkie is a reservoir and NOT a drinking source. UNRELIABLE: every stream shown on the map high on the ridge; the Beskids have had repeated drought and municipal tap-water shortages in recent years, and 2026 has been a severe drought season (severe enough to suspend the neighbouring Ustroń bivouac programme). PLANNING RULE: carry 3 L per person out of the village on Friday, plan Saturday explicitly around Chatka pod Potrójną as the refill, and carry a filter for the valley streams on the descents. Running dry here is an inconvenience rather than an emergency — you are never more than 1-2 h from a village — but it will wreck a hot day if unplanned.
- **Fire / cooking ⚠️:** GAS STOVE — and this range is unusually explicit about it.
