Nadleśnictwo Andrychów states that gas cookers are PERMITTED YEAR-ROUND throughout its area under the 'Zanocuj w lesie' rules, which removes the usual ambiguity; the national programme caveat still applies, so a stove is forbidden when the daily fire-danger degree reaches level 3 (red), and never on peat, in young stands under 5 m canopy, or in tall grass and heather. OPEN FIRE: not permitted in the Beskid Mały bivouac blocks — the only designated campfire sites in the neighbourhood belong to Nadleśnictwo Bielsko and are at Jaworze Nałęże (by the shelter near the chapel) and Dębowiec (behind the hut), both geographically in the Beskid Śląski, and both requiring you to bring your own firewood [uncertain whether Nadl. Andrychów designates any fire site in Beskid Mały]. Fire in a forest or within 100 m of one is a separate serious offence, and the 2026 drought has produced absolute fire prohibitions in neighbouring districts. If you want a legal campfire this weekend, Chatka pod Potrójną's indoor fireplace room is the realistic answer.

**Safety & Costs**

- **Rescue / insurance:** Free, and with the shortest evacuation chain of any candidate. GOPR Grupa Beskidzka covers the whole range and mountain rescue in Poland is free at the point of use under the 2011 Act on safety and rescue in the mountains — no insurance required, no HZS-style invoice, no exposure to the ~400 EUR ground / 2,000-4,000 EUR helicopter charges that apply one ridge south in Slovakia. Call 601 100 300, 985 or 112, and install the 'Ratunek' app for GPS transmission. Because the range is low (max 933 m), narrow, crossed by roads and ringed by Bielsko-Biała, Żywiec, Andrychów and Wadowice, a ground evacuation is short and a road head is rarely more than 1-2 h away — meaning most incidents here resolve as a walk-out or a car pickup rather than a rescue operation. Optional private NNW/KL cover is worth having for medical transport and equipment, but nothing about this range requires it. No EHIC needed (no border exposure).
- **Bears / ticks ⚠️:** BEARS: effectively a non-issue, and this is a genuine differentiator from Beskid Żywiecki. Beskid Mały lies OUTSIDE Poland's permanent brown-bear range — confirmed permanent bear reproduction in Poland is limited to the Bieszczady, the Tatras and the Beskid Żywiecki — and the range is small, low, road-crossed and hemmed in by three cities, which makes it poor bear habitat. A wandering individual cannot be excluded but is rare enough that it should not drive camp choice [uncertain — no 2025/2026 Beskid Mały sighting records found]. Deer, boar and foxes are the realistic nocturnal visitors; standard food hygiene (cook away from the tent, bag food, no scraps) is enough. TICKS ARE THE DOMINANT RISK AND ARGUABLY THE HIGHEST OF ALL FOUR CANDIDATES, precisely because the range is low, warm and dominated by beech and mixed deciduous forest with grassy clearings — optimal Ixodes habitat, and you will be sleeping in it. Both Śląskie and Małopolskie are TBE-relevant regions and national incidence is climbing (793 registered KZM cases in 2024 versus 659 in 2023, with śląskie among the growing voivodeships). Mitigation: permethrin-treated clothing, DEET or icaridin on skin, trousers tucked into socks, a full body check every evening and again after the trip, a tick removal tool in the kit, and awareness that TBE vaccination is the only real protection and must be started well before the season. Lyme borreliosis is the other, unvaccinable, risk — watch for erythema migrans for several weeks afterwards.
- **Weather / storms:** Typical July-August convection: clear humid mornings, build-up from late morning, thunderstorms concentrated roughly 14:00-19:00, clearing in the evening. Exposure is LOW compared with every other candidate — the range tops out at 933 m (Czupel) and the MSB spends most of its length in forest, so lightning exposure is limited to a handful of open points: Magurka Wilkowicka (912 m), Góra Żar and its reservoir plateau, Łamana Skała (929 m) and the Leskowiec / Groń Jana Pawła II meadow. Applying the usual 'off the tops by 15:00' rule costs almost nothing here because the crossings are short. Heat is a bigger practical problem than storms: at 600-900 m in a July heatwave the valleys and lower forest get genuinely hot and airless, which compounds the water scarcity. 2026-SPECIFIC: a severe drought season interrupted by damaging squall lines (28 June-1 July, 7 July and 19 July 2026) that produced widespread windthrow and triggered entry bans in dozens of forest districts — so deadfall and leaning trees are the main overnight hazard for a forest pitch. Never camp under a damaged crown or in a fresh windthrow patch; prefer clearings and forest-road edges. Night temperatures in late July typically 12-16 °C, warmer than the higher ranges.
- **Bail-out plan ⚠️:** The best-connected escape network on the shortlist — you are never remote. STAFFED BUILDINGS on or beside the route: Chatka pod Potrójną (884 m, private, year-round staff, ~45 beds, kitchen, fireplace, tel. 501 694 158), Schronisko PTTK na Leskowcu (32 beds, meals, tel. 33 872 16 94 / 606 743 880), Dom Turystyczny on Hrobacza Łąka [uncertain — hours], and the Góra Żar upper-station facilities. ROADS: DW781 crosses the range at Przełęcz Kocierska; valley roads reach Międzybrodzie, Porąbka, Kocierz, Zakocierz, Rzyki, Targanice and Zembrzyce; from almost any point on the MSB a descent of 45-90 min reaches tarmac with traffic. MECHANISED: the Góra Żar funicular and the Rzyki ski lifts (seasonal hours). RAIL: Bielsko-Biała, Andrychów, Wadowice, Zembrzyce and Sucha Beskidzka all have stations, so an aborted trip is cheap to escape by train. HOSPITALS: Bielsko-Biała, Żywiec, Andrychów, Wadowice — all within 30-45 min by road. GSM: coverage is good to excellent throughout because the range is surrounded by dense settlement and city transmitters; expect only brief shadows in the deepest valleys [uncertain — no coverage-map verification]. Emergency: GOPR 601 100 300 / 985 / 112, 'Ratunek' app. Realistically, the strongest bailout of all is that home is 50 minutes away by car.
- **Total fees ⚠️:** The cheapest option on the shortlist, alongside Beskid Śląski. 'Zanocuj w lesie' costs 0 PLN — no fee, no permit, no booking — so both nights can be free. There is no national-park ticket (the range has no NP; contrast Babiogórski PN's 10 PLN / 5 PLN reduced or TPN's 11 PLN). Realistic weekend total per person: 0 PLN for camping, 0-15 PLN parking [uncertain], plus a share of fuel for a 100-140 km round trip (~60-90 PLN per car), plus whatever you spend voluntarily on soup and tea at Chatka pod Potrójną or Schronisko Leskowiec. Optional bad-weather bunk at Chatka pod Potrójną or Leskowiec: roughly 50-70 PLN/person [uncertain — 2026 rates not verified]. Train fares if going car-free: Ustroń <-> Bielsko and Andrychów <-> Bielsko, low teens of PLN each [uncertain]. BUDGET LINE: 0-20 PLN per person in fees; under 100 PLN all-in including fuel for two people.

**Season, Crowds & Logistics**

- **Season fit:** Well suited to late July-August, with heat and water as the caveats rather than terrain. Maximum altitude 933 m means no snow, no altitude effect, mild nights (12-16 °C) and a light sleeping bag; the trails are firm and dry in summer, which matters because this range is notably muddy in spring and autumn. Extensive beech and mixed forest gives shade for most of the walking day — a real advantage over open ridges in a heatwave — and the few open points (Magurka, Żar, Łamana Skała, Leskowiec) are short crossings. Blueberries and raspberries are out in late July. Season-specific negatives: it is the hottest and lowest of the four candidates, so valley sections are sweaty; natural water is at its scarcest exactly when you need most; tick activity is at its annual peak in precisely this habitat; and fire-danger restrictions are live. Verdict: seasonally fine and legally the most robust of the four right now — the reasons to hesitate are heat, water and ticks, not the calendar.
- **Crowd level:** Moderate, and structurally easy to avoid — much quieter than Beskid Śląski, busier than the Worek Raczański. The range is the local backyard for Bielsko-Biała, Andrychów, Wadowice and Żywiec, so on a sunny Saturday there is steady day traffic on the honeypots: Magurka Wilkowicka, Góra Żar and its reservoir viewpoint, Łamana Skała/Madohora, and above all Groń Jana Pawła II with Leskowiec, which draws pilgrimage as well as hiking traffic. But that traffic is out-and-back from valley car parks between roughly 10:00 and 17:00 and concentrated on a handful of approach trails; the MSB itself sees very little through-hiking, and the forest compartments where you will actually sleep are ordinary working forest that nobody visits. A Friday-evening start converts this from a mild negative into a positive: you walk up as the day crowd drives home, camp in an empty forest, and are already deep on the ridge before Saturday's wave arrives. Sunday mornings before ~09:00 are effectively private.
- **Navigation / maps:** Marking: Polish system, white-colour-white stripes with signposted times and distances. The spine is the MAŁY SZLAK BESKIDZKI (red), 135.3 km from Bielsko-Biała Straconka east to Luboń Wielki in the Beskid Wyspowy — well waymarked but with occasional road and village sections and a denser junction network than the big ridge trails, so a GPX track is genuinely worth having. RECOMMENDED STACK: mapa-turystyczna.pl for planning and GPX export (the source of the segment figures used above), OsmAnd or Locus Map with offline OSM in the field, mapy.cz as a cross-check, and — most importantly here — mBDL / bdl.lasy.gov.pl/portal/mapy to confirm that a prospective pitch lies inside a listed Nadleśnictwo Andrychów leśnictwo/compartment rather than in neighbouring Nadleśnictwo Bielsko ground where no Beskid Mały zone was confirmed. Also bookmark zakazywstepu.bdl.lasy.gov.pl for live entry bans. No PL/SK/CZ marking differences apply — the range is entirely Polish, so one convention throughout, which is one less thing to get wrong on a fast after-work trip. Paper backup: Compass or Sygnatura sheet for Beskid Mały, 1:50,000.

> ⚠️ _Fields above contain self-flagged agent estimates/caveats (verify before departure): Distance / time from Ustroń, Parking, Public transport, Camping rules, 2026 status (live blockers), Camp 2, Actual spacing vs 15-25 km target, Water sources, Fire / cooking, Bears / ticks, Bail-out plan, Total fees_

---

### 4. Beskid Makowski + Beskid Wyspowy (PL — the eastern Mały Szlak Beskidzki corridor: Sucha/Myślenice – Kudłacze – Lubogoszcz – Luboń Wielki)

**Access & Travel**

- **Distance / time from Ustroń ⚠️:** The most distant of the four near-range candidates, and the corridor stretches over a wide band, so pick the trailhead deliberately. Estimated road distances/times from Ustroń (map estimates, NOT verified in a routing engine — DATA SOURCE FLAG): Sucha Beskidzka / Zembrzyce (western Beskid Makowski) ~85-90 km, ~1 h 20-1 h 30 via Bielsko-Biała and Wadowice on DK52; Myślenice ~110-120 km, ~1 h 40-1 h 55 (DK52 to Głogoczów, then S7); Pcim / Trzebunia (the Kudłacze trailhead) ~115-125 km, ~1 h 45-2 h; Kasina Wielka and Mszana Dolna ~130-140 km, ~1 h 55-2 h 10; Rabka-Zdrój / Zaryte (Luboń Wielki trailhead) ~130 km, ~1 h 50-2 h. IMPORTANT CORRECTION TO THE BRIEF: the '1 h – 1 h 40' figure holds only for the WESTERN end (Sucha Beskidzka, Zembrzyce, Mucharz, Lachowice); the Kudłacze–Lubogoszcz–Luboń core of the corridor is realistically 1 h 45-2 h, because DK52 Bielsko–Wadowice–Głogoczów is a single-carriageway road with heavy weekend traffic and no motorway alternative. That extra 30-45 minutes is the single biggest argument against this range for a Friday-evening start.
- **PL/SK/CZ border:** Not applicable — both ranges lie entirely within Poland, in the Małopolskie voivodeship, well inside the country. The nearest state border (PL/SK at Chyżne or Korbielów) is 40-60 km south and no plausible route touches it. Consequences, all simplifying: one legal regime end to end, one trail-marking convention, one currency, GOPR's free rescue everywhere with zero HZS exposure, no vignette and no border logistics. Alongside Beskid Mały, this is one of the two candidates with no cross-border complexity at all — worth noting because the corridor's whole selling point is administrative simplicity: no national park, no border, no permit.
- **Parking ⚠️:** Cheap, informal and never a bottleneck — this is the quietest tourist corridor on the shortlist. Zembrzyce / Sucha Beskidzka: free station and market-square parking. Myślenice: paid town parking, free lots at the Zarabie recreation area on the Raba. Pcim and Trzebunia (Kudłacze trailheads): roadside and small trailhead parking in the villages, free; the forest road up to the shelter is closed to vehicles. Kasina Wielka: free lots by the church and at the ski station. Mszana Dolna: free station parking. Rabka-Zaryte (Luboń Wielki, red trail and Perć Borkowskiego): small free/nominal trailhead lot [uncertain — no verified 2026 tariffs for any of these]. Overnight standing is tolerated at village and station lay-bys, forbidden on Lasy Państwowe forest roads and behind barriers. Fill time is not a concern: none of these trailheads sees mass day-tripping, with the single exception of the 'Odkryj Beskid Wyspowy' event Sundays, when one designated peak per weekend draws organised crowds and its trailhead can fill (2026 dates include Mogielica on 30 August and Lubogoszcz on 13 September — outside a July trip).
- **Public transport ⚠️:** Good WITHIN the corridor, poor for getting to it from Ustroń. Local rail is genuinely useful: line 104 (Chabówka – Rabka – Mszana Dolna – Kasina Wielka – Limanowa – Nowy Sącz) has stations at MSZANA DOLNA and KASINA WIELKA, both directly on the Mały Szlak Beskidzki, and the Kraków–Zakopane line serves Rabka-Zdrój and Chabówka within a few km of Luboń Wielki — so an east-bound linear A->B walk finishing at Mszana Dolna, Kasina Wielka or Rabka can be closed by train without a car shuttle. Buses run the Zakopianka corridor (Myślenice, Pcim, Lubień) frequently. THE PROBLEM is the Ustroń end: there is no direct link, and a rail journey means Ustroń -> Bielsko-Biała -> Kraków -> Rabka/Mszana Dolna, realistically 4-5 h each way with changes [uncertain — 2026 timetables not verified]. VERDICT: drive in, and if you want a linear route, either park at the finish and take a train/bus to the start on Friday evening, or plan a loop. Unlike Beskid Śląski, public transport cannot replace the car here.
- **Vignettes ⚠️:** None. The entire approach — DW941, DK52 Bielsko-Biała – Wadowice – Głogoczów, and S7 Zakopianka south of Kraków — is toll-free for passenger cars, and Polish e-TOLL applies only to vehicles over 3.5 t. No Slovak or Czech vignette is involved. Total road-charge cost: 0 PLN. Note that the A4 motorway around Kraków IS tolled on some sections, but no sensible route from Ustroń to this corridor uses it — the DK52/S7 line is both shorter and free. Fuel for a 230-280 km round trip is the real vehicle cost, roughly 130-180 PLN [uncertain — depends on car and 2026 fuel prices].

**Camping Legality**

- **Protection status ⚠️:** NO national park anywhere in the corridor — the strongest structural feature of this option, and the reason it 'fills the gap' between the near ranges and the Gorce. Neither Beskid Makowski nor Beskid Wyspowy contains a national park, and large parts sit outside landscape parks too, falling instead under looser Obszar Chronionego Krajobrazu designations (including the Południowomałopolski OChK) plus Natura 2000 sites. There are scattered small strict reserves where you must stay on the trail and may not camp — notably REZERWAT LUBOŃ WIELKI on the summit block (protecting the sandstone rock-rubble field and the Perć Borkowskiego route) and a handful of others such as Kostrza and the Mogielica ecological area [uncertain — full reserve inventory not verified]. Everything else is ordinary State Forest under Nadleśnictwa Sucha, Myślenice and Limanowa, so the binding constraint is forestry law, not conservation law. Net: no entry ticket, no ranger regime, no time window — administratively the most permissive corridor on the shortlist alongside Beskid Mały.
- **Camping rules ⚠️:** TWO CHANNELS, both unusually generous here. (A) 'ZANOCUJ W LESIE' — three participating districts cover the whole corridor. NADLEŚNICTWO SUCHA (western Beskid Makowski): compartment-precise zone active since 1 May 2021 across four blocks — Leśnictwo Mucharz oddz. 89-90 (Mucharski Las), Leśnictwo Lachowice oddz. 249-256 (Gajka), Leśnictwo Mosorne oddz. 392-394, 403-404, 409-414, and Leśnictwo Wełcza oddz. 424-432 and 434-454 (the whole leśnictwo); designated fire spots exist but you must bring your own firewood; gas stoves permitted under §18-19 of the regulations; JULY-SPECIFIC: compartment 453a is closed for forest management work. Contact: Małgorzata Murańska, sucha@katowice.lasy.gov.pl, tel. 530 966 748. NADLEŚNICTWO MYŚLENICE (central corridor, Uklejna – Działek – Kudłacze area): zone active, mapped only via BDL/mBDL rather than a published compartment list; campfires only at designated spots and not all of them supply wood; entry banned during collective hunts. Contact: Wiktor Siłuszyk, wiktor.siluszyk@krakow.lasy.gov.pl, tel. 12 272 00 69. NADLEŚNICTWO LIMANOWA (Beskid Wyspowy): the richest list of named areas of any district on this shortlist — Kostrza-Szyk plus LUBOGOSZCZ, ŚNIEŻNICA, ĆWILIN, Zęzów, Szyk, Słupia, Jaworz, Ostra, Bulandy, Bystra, Okręgi and Klenina, i.e. most of the range's signature 'island' peaks. Standard programme limits apply throughout: max 9 people, max 2 consecutive nights, free and without notification below those limits; beyond them, e-mail the district's form at least 2 working days ahead and obtain written confirmation. (B) PAID SHELTER PITCHES — SCHRONISKO PTTK NA KUDŁACZACH (730 m) has a proper camping field plus two covered wiaty, one of them designated for campfires; the published rate is 10.00 PLN per tent plus 2.00 PLN per person, with a bed at 15.00 PLN and bedding 7.00 PLN [uncertain — the price list carries no date and these figures look stale; phone before relying on them]. Schronisko PTTK na Luboniu Wielkim (1,022 m, 25 beds) publishes beds at 30.00 PLN and bedding 15.00 PLN but the page is copyright-dated 2015 and lists NO tent pitch [uncertain]. Reserves and signposted no-entry/logging areas are excluded from everything above.
- **2026 status (live blockers) ⚠️:** CHECKED 2026-07-28 — clean, with one concrete July restriction. (1) Nadleśnictwo Sucha's programme is ACTIVE (running since 1 May 2021) with no suspension notice; it does, however, publish a live restriction that in JULY forest management work closes compartment 453a in Leśnictwo Wełcza — a reminder that this district publishes real, dated, moving exclusions and must be re-read rather than assumed. (2) Nadleśnictwo Myślenice's programme is ACTIVE, with the caveat that it publishes no compartment list, so mBDL is the only way to know where the zone is. (3) Nadleśnictwo Limanowa's programme is ACTIVE across the named Beskid Wyspowy peaks. (4) None of the three shows a drought suspension of the kind that closed Nadleśnictwo Ustroń on 8 May 2026 — so this corridor is currently a MORE RELIABLE legal-camping proposition than the home range, which is a genuine argument for the extra driving. (5) National context: around 19 July 2026 roughly 29 of 429 forest districts had temporary forest-entry bans in force, mostly windthrow damage from the storm episodes of 28 June-1 July, 7 July and 19 July 2026; none of the three districts was confirmed on that list [uncertain]. MANDATORY RE-CHECKS IN THE DEPARTURE WEEK: zakazywstepu.bdl.lasy.gov.pl; each district's programme page for new work exclusions; the daily fire-danger degree (level 3 suspends gas stoves); and a phone call to Kudłacze to confirm the camping field is open and the price is current.
- **Hourly / seasonal restrictions ⚠️:** No time-of-day limits whatsoever — no national park, so no entry window, no ticket hours, and no Czech-style pitch/strike times. You may walk in after dark and pitch at any hour inside a 'Zanocuj w lesie' block, and the Kudłacze camping field has no stated curfew [uncertain]. Real constraints: (a) the DATED, MOVING work exclusions the districts publish — Nadleśnictwo Sucha explicitly closes compartment 453a in July, and Myślenice bans entry during collective hunts, which run roughly October-January and must be checked with the relevant gminas; (b) nature reserves, above all Rezerwat Luboń Wielki on the summit block, are trail-only year-round with no bivouac; (c) the exposed scramble variant on Luboń Wielki (Perć Borkowskiego) is unsuitable in wet or icy conditions; (d) shelter service hours — both PTTK huts are small and staffing can be irregular out of peak season [uncertain]; (e) fire-danger degree 3 suspends gas stoves. Also note the 'Odkryj Beskid Wyspowy' campaign: on specific announced Sundays a named peak hosts an organised mass walk (2026 includes Mogielica on 30 August and Lubogoszcz on 13 September) — not a restriction, but it turns one quiet summit per weekend into a crowded one.
- **Fine risk:** Standard Polish regime and low practical exposure. Camping in State Forests outside a designated place is a wykroczenie normally handled by a mandat karny of up to 500 PLN from Straż Leśna or Policja, with stacking offences (littering, damaging the litter layer, taking deadwood, disturbing wildlife, driving into the forest) pushing a combined mandate toward ~1,000 PLN, and court referral available in aggravated cases (sources cite up to 5,000 PLN). Open fire in a forest or within 100 m of one is a separate, far more serious offence. Camping inside Rezerwat Luboń Wielki would additionally breach nature-conservation law. TWO CORRIDOR-SPECIFIC NOTES: (1) enforcement pressure is LOWER here than in the resort ranges simply because there is less traffic and fewer patrols — which is exactly why the discipline of verifying compartments in mBDL matters, since 'nobody will notice' is not a legal defence; (2) Nadleśnictwo Myślenice publishes no compartment list, so a pitch there is only defensible if you can show it was inside the mapped zone — screenshot the mBDL position. Using the Kudłacze camping field for one of the two nights removes all doubt for that night at a cost of a few złoty.

**Route & Camps**

- **Suggested route:** RECOMMENDED — MSB eastbound, Kudłacze then Lubogoszcz, finishing at Luboń Wielki. FRIDAY: drive Ustroń -> Pcim or Trzebunia (~1 h 45-2 h), walk the red Mały Szlak Beskidzki up to SCHRONISKO PTTK NA KUDŁACZACH (730 m, Pasmo Lubomira i Łysiny, ~8 km SE of Myślenice); roughly 4-6 km, +300-400 m, 1 h 15-1 h 45 -> Camp 1 on the shelter's camping field (water, two covered wiaty, one with a legal fire spot). SATURDAY: MSB east — Kudłacze -> Wierzbanowska Góra -> Kasina Wielka -> up onto LUBOGOSZCZ (869 m); roughly 17-19 km, 6-7 h with a pack -> Camp 2 in the Nadleśnictwo Limanowa 'Zanocuj w lesie' block on Lubogoszcz (free). SUNDAY: descend to Mszana Dolna, then either finish there at the line-104 station (~5-6 km, short day) or continue the MSB over Przełęcz Glisne to LUBOŃ WIELKI (1,022 m, PTTK shelter, panorama over the Wyspowy, Makowski, Żywiecki, Gorce and the Tatras) and drop to Rabka-Zaryte (~12-14 km, 5-6 h). Totals ~35-40 km with the full Sunday, ~28 km with the short one. SHORTER/CLOSER VARIANT — western Beskid Makowski from Zembrzyce/Sucha Beskidzka (only ~1 h 20-1 h 30 from Ustroń): use the Nadleśnictwo Sucha compartment blocks (Lachowice/Gajka oddz. 249-256, Mucharz oddz. 89-90) as free camps on either side of the MSB stretch Zembrzyce – Chełm – Palcza – Babica; this trades the best scenery for a materially better Friday-evening budget. REFERENCE FIGURES for planning: the whole MSB is 137 km / ~42 h 30 of walking time, Straconka to Luboń Wielki, with published cumulative times Myślenice 25 h 45, Działek 29 h, Kasina Wielka 35 h 15, Mszana Dolna 39 h 30, Przełęcz Glisne 41 h, Luboń Wielki 42 h 30 — Kudłacze interpolates at roughly 31-32 h.
- **Friday-evening feasibility:** THE WEAK POINT OF THIS OPTION, and the honest reason it ranks below Beskid Mały for a Friday-night start. Sunset in Małopolska at the end of July is around 20:20-20:35 CEST with usable civil twilight to roughly 20:55-21:10 [approximate; verify for the exact date]. Recommended-route budget: leave Ustroń 17:00-17:30, drive 1 h 45-2 h on DK52 (single carriageway, Friday-afternoon traffic through Wadowice and Sucha is the main variable) -> Pcim/Trzebunia at 18:45-19:30, walk 1 h 15-1 h 45 -> tent up 20:00-21:15. That is right at the edge: a 17:00 departure works comfortably, an 18:00 departure means arriving in the dark. MITIGATION, and it is a good one: Camp 1 is a STAFFED PTTK SHELTER with a marked camping field, covered wiaty and water — a lit, known destination on a clear waymarked forest track, so a head-torch finish is low-consequence, and you can phone ahead. Better mitigations: (1) use the WESTERN variant from Zembrzyce/Sucha Beskidzka, which is 25-40 minutes closer and puts a legal Nadleśnictwo Sucha compartment within an hour of the car; (2) treat this range as a SATURDAY-MORNING-START option rather than a Friday-evening one, which is what the extra distance really argues for; (3) if departure slips past 18:30, switch destination to Beskid Mały and keep this corridor for a weekend with an early finish. Traffic risk is the dominant uncertainty: DK52 has no bypass at several towns and can add 20-30 minutes on a summer Friday.
- **Camp 1 ⚠️:** SCHRONISKO PTTK NA KUDŁACZACH camping field, 730 m, on the Kudłacze clearing in Pasmo Lubomira i Łysiny (Beskid Makowski / Średni), about 8 km south-east of Myślenice and directly on the Mały Szlak Beskidzki. Legal basis: a formally operated PTTK camping field — published rate 10.00 PLN per tent plus 2.00 PLN per person [uncertain — undated price list, verify by phone]. Why this is the best Camp 1 on the corridor: it combines a legal pitch, running water, a 37-bed shelter as a weather fallback (bed 15.00 PLN, bedding 7.00 PLN [uncertain]), TWO COVERED WIATY — one of them designated for campfires, which makes this one of very few places on the whole shortlist with a legal fire — and a position that leaves a clean 17-19 km Saturday. Access ~1-1.5 h on the red trail from Pcim/Trzebunia; the forest road is closed to cars, which keeps it quiet. Contact via kudlacze.pttk.pl. FREE ALTERNATIVE nearby: the Nadleśnictwo Myślenice 'Zanocuj w lesie' zone around Uklejna – Śliwnik – Działek, west of Kudłacze on the MSB — 0 PLN, but the district publishes no compartment list, so you must locate the zone in mBDL and screenshot your position. WESTERN ALTERNATIVE (shorter drive): Nadleśnictwo Sucha's Leśnictwo Lachowice block, oddz. 249-256 ('Gajka'), or Leśnictwo Mucharz oddz. 89-90 — compartment-precise, free, with designated fire spots if you bring your own wood.
- **Camp 2 ⚠️:** LUBOGOSZCZ (869 m) 'ZANOCUJ W LESIE' BLOCK, Nadleśnictwo Limanowa — the forested dome above Mszana Dolna and Kasina Wielka, explicitly named in the district's list of designated bushcraft areas (alongside Śnieżnica, Ćwilin, Ostra, Bulandy, Kostrza-Szyk, Zęzów, Słupia, Jaworz, Bystra, Okręgi and Klenina). Legal basis: active district 'Zanocuj w lesie' designation — free, no booking, max 9 people, max 2 consecutive nights. Why here: it sits at ~17-19 km from Kudłacze along the MSB, which is the only pairing in this corridor that lands inside the 15-25 km band, and it leaves a short, flexible Sunday with Mszana Dolna's railway station 5-6 km below. Character: quiet beech and fir forest on a classic Beskid Wyspowy 'island' massif with almost no weekend traffic. CARRY WATER UP — Lubogoszcz is a dry dome; fill at Kasina Wielka on the way. VERIFY IN mBDL, since the designation is by area name rather than by a drawn boundary you can see on the ground. ALTERNATIVE (serviced, but too far for a single day from Kudłacze at ~34 km): SCHRONISKO PTTK NA LUBONIU WIELKIM, 1,022 m, 25 beds in the main building and the summer bacówka, published bed rate 30.00 PLN and bedding 15.00 PLN [uncertain — price page is copyright-dated 2015], with NO advertised tent field; treat it as a Sunday destination and water/food stop rather than as Camp 2, and note its documented water problem (see water_sources). FURTHER ALTERNATIVES on the Limanowa list if you reshape the route: Śnieżnica or Ćwilin, both free and both on marked trails.
- **Actual spacing vs 15-25 km target ⚠️:** This corridor CAN meet the 15-25 km criterion, and it does so more naturally than Beskid Śląski or the Racza group — but only on specific pairings, and the anchors are areas rather than fixed points. Estimated spacings, derived from the published MSB cumulative walking times (Myślenice 25 h 45, Działek 29 h, Kasina Wielka 35 h 15, Mszana Dolna 39 h 30, Przełęcz Glisne 41 h, Luboń Wielki 42 h 30 for 137 km total, i.e. ~3.2 km/h) and interpolation for Kudłacze at ~31-32 h — NOT GPS-measured, treat as ±2-3 km: Kudłacze -> Lubogoszcz block ≈ 17-19 km, IN BAND and the recommended pairing; Kudłacze -> Mszana Dolna ≈ 25 km, at the very TOP of the band; Kasina Wielka -> Luboń Wielki ≈ 22-23 km, IN BAND if you shift both camps east; Myślenice -> Kudłacze ≈ 18 km, IN BAND for a western variant; Kudłacze -> Kasina Wielka ≈ 12 km, BELOW band; Kudłacze -> Luboń Wielki ≈ 34-35 km, ABOVE band and not a realistic single day with camp gear; Działek -> Kudłacze ≈ 8 km, far below band. STRUCTURAL ADVANTAGE over the other three Polish candidates: because Nadleśnictwo Limanowa designates whole named massifs (Lubogoszcz, Śnieżnica, Ćwilin, Ostra...) rather than isolated points, you can dial the day's distance to almost exactly what you want and still be legal — the corridor is ~60 km of continuous MSB between Sucha Beskidzka and Luboń Wielki with legal ground scattered along most of it. STRUCTURAL WEAKNESS: nothing verifies the pitch for you, so every free camp needs an mBDL check, and Nadleśnictwo Myślenice does not even publish a compartment list.
- **Water sources ⚠️:** Adequate but requires planning, and one documented failure point. RELIABLE: Schronisko PTTK na Kudłaczach (running water, buffet, showers [uncertain]); the villages the MSB passes or skirts — Pcim, Trzebunia, Myślenice, Kasina Wielka, Mszana Dolna, Glisne, Rabka-Zaryte — which is the corridor's real advantage, since it dips to valley level repeatedly and you are rarely more than 2-3 h from a tap; and the Raba and its tributaries in the valleys (filter/boil). DOCUMENTED PROBLEM: SCHRONISKO NA LUBONIU WIELKIM RUNS SHORT OF WATER IN DRY SPELLS — the shelter itself states that during rainless periods the shower stops working and even cold water can run short, and there are no indoor toilets. In a severe drought year like 2026 you must NOT plan on refilling at Luboń Wielki; arrive with what you need. UNRELIABLE: the ridge and dome tops generally — Lubogoszcz, Śnieżnica, Ćwilin, Koskowa Góra and Luboń Wielki are all dry summits with no springs on the crest, and the small streams shown low on their flanks are seasonal and are the first casualties of drought. PLANNING RULE: fill 2.5-3 L at Kudłacze on Saturday morning, top up in Kasina Wielka mid-day, carry it up to the Lubogoszcz camp, and carry a filter for the valley crossings. Running dry here is an inconvenience rather than an emergency because villages are close, but the Luboń Wielki caveat is a genuine trap for anyone who assumes a staffed shelter means water.
- **Fire / cooking ⚠️:** The most fire-friendly corridor on the shortlist, though still drought-dependent. LEGAL CAMPFIRE IS ACTUALLY AVAILABLE HERE: Schronisko PTTK na Kudłaczach maintains two covered wiaty, one of them designated for campfires, on the same site as its camping field — so a fire at Camp 1 is legitimate and sheltered from rain. Within the 'Zanocuj w lesie' zones, Nadleśnictwo Sucha and Nadleśnictwo Myślenice both designate fire spots but require you to BRING YOUR OWN FIREWOOD (Myślenice notes that not all sites supply wood, so verify in advance); Nadleśnictwo Limanowa's fire provision is not confirmed [uncertain]. GAS STOVE: permitted — Nadleśnictwo Sucha references §18-19 of the programme regulations — subject to the national caveats: forbidden at fire-danger degree 3 (red), and never on peat, in young stands under 5 m canopy, or in tall grass and heather. EVERYWHERE ELSE: no open fire; fire in a forest or within 100 m of one is a serious offence, and 2026 is a severe drought season in which neighbouring districts have declared absolute fire prohibitions. Check the daily fire-danger degree on the morning of departure and have a cold-food fallback.

**Safety & Costs**

- **Rescue / insurance:** Free, with no foreign exposure. Poland's Beskid Wyspowy, Beskid Makowski and the Rabka area are covered by GOPR (Grupa Podhalańska, based in Rabka-Zdrój, with the Krynicka group to the east), and mountain rescue is free at the point of use under the 2011 Act on safety and rescue in the mountains — no insurance required, no HZS-style invoice, no exposure to the ~400 EUR ground / 2,000-4,000 EUR helicopter charges that apply across the Slovak border. Call 601 100 300, 985 or 112, and install the 'Ratunek' app for GPS transmission. The terrain is forgiving: maximum altitude 1,022 m (Luboń Wielki), no exposure except the optional Perć Borkowskiego scramble on Luboń's northern rock-rubble slope, and villages and roads within 1-2 h of almost any point, so most incidents resolve as a walk-out. Optional private NNW/KL cover is worth having for medical transport and gear, but nothing here requires it. No EHIC needed.
- **Bears / ticks ⚠️:** BEARS: intermediate risk, lower than Beskid Żywiecki and higher than Beskid Mały. Beskid Wyspowy is not one of Poland's three confirmed permanent bear-breeding areas (Bieszczady, Tatras, Beskid Żywiecki), but it sits on the migration corridor linking the Gorce, Beskid Sądecki and the Tatras with the western ranges, and transient individuals do pass through — treat a bear encounter as unlikely but not absurd [uncertain — no specific 2025/2026 Beskid Wyspowy sighting records found in this research]. Wolves and lynx are present across both ranges and are not a threat to people. PROTOCOL if wild-camping on Lubogoszcz or the other Limanowa massifs: cook and eat 50-100 m downwind of the tent, never store food or scented items inside it, bag or hang food, make noise on overgrown trails, avoid moving silently at dusk. Camping at Kudłacze largely removes the question. TICKS ARE THE DOMINANT AND CERTAIN RISK: Małopolskie is a TBE-relevant region and national incidence is rising sharply (793 registered KZM cases in 2024 versus 659 in 2023, with małopolskie among the growing voivodeships), and these low, warm, beech-and-fir massifs with grassy clearings are prime Ixodes habitat in late July — precisely where you will sleep. Mitigation: permethrin-treated clothing, DEET or icaridin on exposed skin, trousers tucked into socks, a full body check every evening and again after the trip, a tick removal tool in the kit. TBE vaccination is the only genuine protection and must be started well before the season; Lyme borreliosis cannot be vaccinated against, so watch for erythema migrans for several weeks afterwards.
- **Weather / storms:** Standard Carpathian July-August convection: humid clear mornings, build-up from late morning, thunderstorms clustered roughly 14:00-19:00, clearing by evening. Exposure is LOW-TO-MODERATE and unusual in shape: Beskid Wyspowy consists of isolated conical massifs separated by deep valleys, so instead of a long exposed ridge you get a series of short summit crossings (Lubogoszcz 869 m, Śnieżnica, Ćwilin, Luboń Wielki 1,022 m) with forested approaches — easy to time around storms, but the repeated ascents and descents make the day feel longer than the kilometres suggest and leave you fully exposed for 20-40 minutes at a time on each top. Apply the usual 'off the summits by about 15:00' rule and note that the Perć Borkowskiego rock-rubble route on Luboń Wielki becomes genuinely slippery and unwise in rain. 2026-SPECIFIC: a severe drought season punctuated by damaging squall lines (28 June-1 July, 7 July and 19 July 2026) that produced widespread windthrow and triggered forest-entry bans in dozens of districts — deadfall and leaning trees are the main overnight hazard for a forest pitch, so avoid damaged crowns and fresh windthrow and prefer clearings or the Kudłacze field. Night temperatures at 700-900 m in late July typically 10-15 °C; valley inversions can make the Raba and Mszana valleys foggy and cold at dawn.
- **Bail-out plan ⚠️:** Solid, thanks to the corridor's repeated descents to inhabited valleys. STAFFED BUILDINGS on or beside the route: SCHRONISKO PTTK NA KUDŁACZACH (730 m, 37 beds, buffet, two wiaty — the best refuge on the western half) and SCHRONISKO PTTK NA LUBONIU WIELKIM (1,022 m, 25 beds in the main building plus a summer bacówka, buffet, shower when water allows, outdoor toilets, telephone). ROADS AND SETTLEMENTS: the MSB touches or passes close to Myślenice, Pcim, Trzebunia, Kasina Wielka, Mszana Dolna, Glisne and Rabka-Zaryte, and the S7 Zakopianka runs parallel through the Raba valley with frequent buses — so a descent of 45-90 min from anywhere on the route reaches a road with traffic. RAIL: Mszana Dolna and Kasina Wielka on line 104, Rabka-Zdrój and Chabówka on the Kraków–Zakopane line; an aborted trip escapes by train. HOSPITALS: Myślenice, Mszana Dolna, Rabka-Zdrój, Limanowa and Nowy Targ, all within 30-50 min by road. GSM: generally usable on the summits and in the valleys because of the dense settlement and the Zakopianka corridor, patchier on north-facing forested slopes and in the deeper side valleys [uncertain — no coverage-map verification]. Emergency: GOPR 601 100 300 / 985 / 112, 'Ratunek' app. The one weakness relative to the near ranges: home is ~2 h away, so 'just drive back' is a real decision rather than a shrug.
- **Total fees ⚠️:** Very cheap, with one price-currency caveat. No national-park ticket anywhere in the corridor and no landscape-park fee. 'Zanocuj w lesie' nights cost 0 PLN. Kudłacze camping field: published 10.00 PLN per tent plus 2.00 PLN per person — i.e. about 12-14 PLN for one or two people for the night — with a bed at 15.00 PLN and bedding 7.00 PLN [uncertain — the price list is undated and these rates look considerably below 2026 market levels; budget 25-40 PLN and phone ahead]. Luboń Wielki beds published at 30.00 PLN plus 15.00 PLN bedding, but from a page copyright-dated 2015 [uncertain — budget 50-70 PLN]. REALISTIC WEEKEND TOTAL PER PERSON: roughly 15-30 PLN in fees for the recommended Kudłacze + free Lubogoszcz configuration, or 0 PLN if both nights are in 'Zanocuj w lesie' blocks. Parking 0-15 PLN [uncertain]. Fuel dominates: a 230-280 km round trip is roughly 130-180 PLN per car, which is double the Beskid Mały figure and is the real cost difference between this option and the near ranges. Optional train fares if finishing at Mszana Dolna or Rabka: low tens of PLN [uncertain].

**Season, Crowds & Logistics**

- **Season fit:** Well suited to late July-August. Altitudes of 700-1,022 m mean no snow, no altitude effect, mild nights (10-15 °C) and a light bag; the massifs are heavily forested with beech and fir, giving shade on hot days, and the summit clearings on Lubogoszcz, Śnieżnica, Ćwilin and Luboń Wielki open out to what is arguably the best panorama-to-effort ratio in the Polish Beskids — from Luboń Wielki the view spans the Wyspowy, Makowski, Żywiecki, Gorce and the Tatras. Trails are firm and dry in summer, which matters because these clay-and-flysch slopes are notoriously muddy in spring and autumn. Season-specific negatives: peak tick activity in exactly this habitat; scarce water on the domes and the documented Luboń Wielki shortage in dry spells; fire-danger restrictions; and afternoon storms on the short summit crossings. Also worth knowing: the 'Odkryj Beskid Wyspowy' campaign runs organised mass walks on announced Sundays from late spring to autumn (2026 includes Mogielica 30 August and Lubogoszcz 13 September) — outside a July trip, but check the schedule if the plan slips.
- **Crowd level:** THE QUIETEST CORRIDOR ON THE SHORTLIST — this is its defining advantage and the reason it is worth the extra hour of driving. Beskid Makowski is a transit range that almost nobody visits on foot, and Beskid Wyspowy, despite excellent views, remains one of the least-walked parts of the Polish Carpathians: no cable cars, no resort, isolated massifs that each require a full ascent from the valley, and a through-trail (the MSB) that sees very little end-to-end traffic. Expect to meet a handful of people at most on a July Saturday between Kudłacze and Lubogoszcz, and to have the Kudłacze camping field largely to yourself. The exceptions are narrow and predictable: Myślenice-Zarabie and the Uklejna approaches get local day traffic; Luboń Wielki draws steady weekend walkers from Rabka because of the panorama and the Perć Borkowskiego; and the 'Odkryj Beskid Wyspowy' campaign concentrates organised crowds on one announced peak per weekend (Mogielica 30 August 2026, Lubogoszcz 13 September 2026 — check the schedule and simply pick a different massif that day). A Friday-evening start makes an already empty range emptier.
- **Navigation / maps:** Marking: Polish system, white-colour-white stripes, signposted with times and distances. The spine is the MAŁY SZLAK BESKIDZKI (red), 137 km from Bielsko-Biała Straconka to Luboń Wielki, with published cumulative walking times that are the best planning currency for this corridor (Myślenice 25 h 45, Działek 29 h, Kasina Wielka 35 h 15, Mszana Dolna 39 h 30, Przełęcz Glisne 41 h, Luboń Wielki 42 h 30). NAVIGATION IS HARDER HERE THAN IN THE NEAR RANGES: trail density is lower, the route drops into villages and follows tarmac and field tracks between massifs, and junction signage is sparser — so a downloaded GPX is close to essential rather than merely helpful. RECOMMENDED STACK: mapa-turystyczna.pl for planning and GPX export, OsmAnd or Locus Map with offline OSM in the field, mapy.cz as a cross-check, and mBDL / bdl.lasy.gov.pl/portal/mapy as the ONLY way to confirm a legal pitch — critically so for Nadleśnictwo Myślenice, which publishes no compartment list and refers users to BDL and the mBDL app, and useful for Nadleśnictwo Sucha, whose zone is defined by explicit compartment numbers (Mucharz 89-90; Lachowice 249-256; Mosorne 392-394, 403-404, 409-414; Wełcza 424-432, 434-454). Also bookmark zakazywstepu.bdl.lasy.gov.pl. Useful regional resource: odkryjbeskidwyspowy.pl publishes route descriptions for the Wyspowy massifs. No PL/SK/CZ marking differences apply — one convention throughout. Paper backup: Compass sheets for Beskid Makowski/Wyspowy, 1:50,000.

> ⚠️ _Fields above contain self-flagged agent estimates/caveats (verify before departure): Distance / time from Ustroń, Parking, Public transport, Vignettes, Protection status, Camping rules, 2026 status (live blockers), Hourly / seasonal restrictions, Camp 1, Camp 2, Actual spacing vs 15-25 km target, Water sources, Fire / cooking, Bears / ticks, Bail-out plan, Total fees_

---

### 5. Moravskoslezske Beskydy / CHKO Beskydy (CZ)

**Access & Travel**

- **Distance / time from Ustroń ⚠️:** ESTIMATED BY MAP READING, NOT VERIFIED IN A ROUTING ENGINE - treat every figure below as +/- 15%. Ustron sits ~15 km from the Czech border at Cieszyn, so the eastern (Jablunkov) end of this range is the closest foreign massif to the flat. (a) Mosty u Jablunkova / Chata Skalka trailhead - the main recommended start: ~40-50 km, ~45-60 min, either via Wisla - Istebna - Jasnowice/Bukovec border or (faster in traffic) Cieszyn - Cesky Tesin - Trinec on I/11. This is the only part of the range that genuinely fits the '40-70 min from Ustron' bracket in the task scope. (b) Ostravice / Visalaje (Lysa hora southern approach): ~65-80 km, ~1 h 10 - 1 h 25 via Trinec - Jablunkov - Bila - Stare Hamry, or via D48 Frydek-Mistek. (c) Malenovice (Lysa hora northern approach): ~75 km, ~1 h 15. (d) Pustevny / Trojanovice: ~95-100 km, ~1 h 30. (e) Utulna Uvez / Velky Javornik near Roznov pod Radhostem: ~90-100 km, ~1 h 30. (f) Kemp Pulcin (Francova Lhota, Zlinsky kraj): ~105-115 km, ~1 h 45 - 2 h. CORRECTION TO THE SCOPE ASSUMPTION: only Skalka is a 45-60 min drive; Lysa hora is ~70-85 min and Pulcin/Uvez are roughly double the stated bracket. For a Friday-after-work start this matters a lot - see friday_evening_feasibility.
- **PL/SK/CZ border:** Poland-Czechia is an internal Schengen land border with no permanent checks; the Cieszyn/Cesky Tesin, Jasnowice/Bukovec and Zwardon-area crossings are drive-through. Czechia has not reintroduced systematic controls on the PL border (unlike the PL-SK/PL-DE pattern of recent years), but random Czech police spot-checks near Cesky Tesin and Jablunkov do happen - carry ID/passport. Currency is the Czech koruna (CZK); cards and Apple/Google Pay work almost everywhere including mountain chatas, but the emergency-camp fee at Skalka and small huts may want cash - bring ~500 CZK. Do NOT rely on PLN or EUR being accepted; roadside exchange rates in Cesky Tesin are poor. Trail marking is the KCT four-colour system that Poland copied, so it reads identically to a Polish hiker (red = long-distance ridge, blue/green/yellow = connectors); the practical difference is signpost content - Czech signposts give TIMES in hours rather than kilometres in some places, and give distances to the next chata rather than to the summit. The Skalka-Velky Polom-Bily Kriz ridge IS the CZ/SK state border, so trails are marked from both sides and your phone will hop between Czech and Slovak networks (EU roaming, no cost for a Polish SIM, but emergency 112 may route to either country's dispatch).
- **Parking ⚠️:** No central paid park-entry system - CHKO Beskydy is a Protected Landscape Area, not a national park, so there is no entrance gate or park ticket. Parking is municipal/commercial and cheap: Malenovice (Lysa hora N approach) has a paid lot by Rajska bouda, ~70 CZK/day, ~65 spaces, plus a second lot toward Hute at ~50 CZK/day (~60 spaces); Ostravice has lots by the railway station (U Pily) and at hotels Sepetna (~150 CZK/car/day) and Slunicko; the old lot 'u transformatoru' below Lysa hora has been wound down in favour of a shuttle bus. Mosty u Jablunkova (Skalka start) - free/very cheap parking at the railway station and in the village; capacity is modest but this trailhead is NOT a mass destination, so a Friday-evening arrival will find space. OVERNIGHT PARKING is legal at normal public lots - nothing in Czech law forbids leaving a car parked while you are on the hill, and unlike Vratna/Sutovo there is no reservation system or gate-closing time. [uncertain] Exact 2026 tariffs: the Malenovice/Ostravice prices come from 2021-2023 sources and may have risen; capacity/fill-time for Lysa hora lots in high season - Lysa hora is the single most visited peak in the Beskids, so the Malenovice and Ostravice lots DO fill by ~09:00-10:00 on a sunny July Saturday. Friday-evening arrival sidesteps this entirely.
- **Public transport ⚠️:** Yes - this is the range's structural advantage over the Slovak options: a genuine A->B linear route is realistic. Mosty u Jablunkova lies on the electrified Bohumin-Cadca main corridor (frequent regional trains from Cesky Tesin and Trinec), and Ostravice is the terminus of the Frydlant nad Ostravici branch line, so the classic Skalka -> ridge -> Lysa hora -> Ostravice traverse can be closed by rail: Ostravice -> Frydlant nad Ostravici -> Ostrava-Kuncice/Ostrava hl.n. -> Mosty u Jablunkova, roughly 2-2.5 h with 1-2 changes, tickets under ~200 CZK. Buses (Visalaje, Bila, Bily Kriz, Pustevny) supplement this on weekends. Practical pattern: leave the car at Mosty u Jablunkova station, walk the ridge, ride the train back on Sunday afternoon. [uncertain] Exact 2026 timetables and Sunday-evening last connections - verify on idos.cz in the week of departure.
- **Vignettes:** Only needed if you actually use a Czech motorway (D-road). The whole recommended approach - Cieszyn/Jasnowice -> Trinec -> Jablunkov -> Mosty, and the Bila/Stare Hamry road to Ostravice - runs on I. class roads (I/11, I/56, I/35) that are NOT tolled, so the trip can be done with ZERO vignette cost. You only need one if you route through Frydek-Mistek/Ostrava on the D48 or D56. Czech e-vignette (elektronicka dalnicni znamka, eznamka.cz) 2026: 10-day = 300 CZK for a normal petrol/diesel car up to 3.5 t; 1-month and annual are dearer. It is bound to the registration plate, is bought online in a minute, and the start date is chosen at purchase. CNG cars get 50% off, plug-in hybrids 75%, EVs and hydrogen are free. Recommendation: skip it, take the I/11 route.

**Camping Legality**

- **Protection status:** CHKO Beskydy (Chranena krajinna oblast - Protected Landscape Area, IUCN cat. V), declared 1973, ~1160 km2 - the LARGEST CHKO in the Czech Republic, administered by AOPK CR from Roznov pod Radhostem. It is zoned I-IV (I = strictest) and is also a Natura 2000 bird area (SPA Beskydy) and SAC. Crucially it is NOT a national park, so the blanket national-park regime does not apply - but Czech law bans camping and open fires in ALL of a CHKO outside designated places (see camping_rules), which is stricter than the Slovak 2nd-degree regime just across the ridge. Embedded inside the CHKO are National Nature Reserves (NPR) with a hard statutory off-trail entry ban: Mazak (the whole north-eastern flank of Lysa hora), Mionsi, Knehyne-Certuv mlyn, Radhost, Salajka, Razula and Pulcin-Hradisko. Practical consequence: on Lysa hora and around Pulcin you must stay on marked trails, and there is no legal way to disappear into the trees to pitch a tent.
- **Camping rules ⚠️:** BASELINE: sec. 26(1)(b) of Act 114/1992 Sb. bans 'taborit a rozdelavat ohne mimo mista vyhrazena' - camping and lighting fires outside designated places - across the ENTIRE CHKO Beskydy. Wild camping is therefore illegal by default here, with no Czech equivalent of the Polish 'Zanocuj w lesie' free-bivouac programme covering these ridges. THE EXCEPTION - 'nouzova nocoviste' (emergency overnight sites): AOPK CR / Sprava CHKO Beskydy has granted formal exemptions creating a small network of legal one-night sites, explicitly aimed at long-distance ridge hikers and explicitly NOT campgrounds. Common rules at all of them: (1) tents/tarps may be pitched only on the marked patch and only between 18:00 and 09:00 the next morning - the site must be completely cleared by 09:00; (2) ONE night only ('stan na jednu noc'), no multi-day base-camping; (3) no open fire (Skalka is the exception - it has an approved fire pit; fire anywhere outside that pit is 'prisne zakazano'); a gas/tourist stove is the intended cooking method; (4) carry out all rubbish; (5) foot access only - you may not drive or sleep in a vehicle there. THE SITES AS OF JULY 2026: (a) Chata KCT Skalka above Mosty u Jablunkova (932 m) - opened April 2024, max 20 people / 10 tents-tarps, open year-round, ADVANCE REGISTRATION REQUIRED online or at the chata reception, fee 50 CZK/person/night, prepared levelled pitches, shelter, approved fire pit; campers may use the chata's toilets, showers and charging. (b) Bezrucova chata on Lysa hora (1323 m) - grassy area just below the chata, FREE, no registration reported, 18:00-09:00, strict no-fire. (c) Pustevny (~1015 m) - added in June 2026, same 18:00-09:00 / no-fire regime. Further sites on the Javorniky ridge were under discussion in mid-2026. SEPARATE, DIFFERENT REGIME - Taboriste Pulcin (Francova Lhota, Zlinsky kraj, below the Pulcinske skaly): a genuine small campsite established WITH CHKO consent, open to multi-night short stays, with wooden shelters, fire rings and firewood provided, washing troughs with running water, latrine, ~10 CZK/person/night (children under 5 free), foot access only along marked trails, ~300 m beyond the village. This is the one place in the range where a legal campfire and a two-night stay are both possible. SEPARATE AGAIN - utulna Uvez, a free open A-frame shelter built in 2024 on the Roznov pod Radhostem - Velky Javornik trail (approx. 49.4989 N, 18.1341 E), MAX 3 PEOPLE, one night, no fire, no smoking, no booking, first-come-first-served; it is a hut, not a tent pitch. [uncertain] Whether the Bezrucova chata site also requires registration or charges a fee - the June 2026 reporting says free and unregistered, but the chata may have introduced a sign-up since; call ahead (bezrucovachata@gmail.com).
- **2026 status (live blockers) ⚠️:** CHECKED 2026-07-28 - THIS FIELD DECAYS FAST, RE-CHECK IN THE WEEK OF DEPARTURE at beskydy.aopk.gov.cz/aktualni-omezeni. (1) The legal-camping network is EXPANDING, not shrinking: Skalka live since April 2024, Lysa hora and Pustevny reported as newly opened in June 2026 (iROZHLAS 14 Jun 2026, drevostavitel.cz 24 Jun 2026), more sites mooted for the Javorniky ridge. (2) FIRE BAN - the big live blocker: Zlinsky kraj declared a 'period of adverse climatic conditions' on 6 May 2026 banning ALL open flame in the countryside region-wide 'until further notice' because of prolonged drought; a CHMI fire warning also ran 3-5 July 2026 over parts of the region. Zlinsky kraj covers Pulcin and Uvez - so the Pulcin campfire, the one selling point of that site, is very likely OFF this summer. Moravskoslezsky kraj (Skalka, Lysa hora, Pustevny) status not confirmed for late July 2026 - assume the Skalka fire pit may also be closed and plan on a gas stove regardless. (3) PERMANENT CLOSURES still in force: the summit of Knehyne is off-limits (inside NPR Knehyne-Certuv mlyn, no marked trail leads there); off-trail entry banned in NPR Mionsi, Mazak, Knehyne-Certuv mlyn, Radhost, Salajka, Razula, Pulcin-Hradisko; the marked trails across the Travny massif were CANCELLED on 1 May 2020 and have not returned - do not plan a Travny link between Bily Kriz and Lysa hora. (4) Seasonal: the Pulcin 'Barevne ledopady' route is restricted 1 Jan - 30 Mar (irrelevant in July). (5) No forest-entry ban (zakaz vstupu do lesa) found for the Beskids in July 2026, but bark-beetle salvage logging closes short forest-road sections at short notice. [uncertain] Moravskoslezsky kraj open-fire ban status for late July 2026; whether the Lysa hora and Pustevny sites are permanent or a 2026 pilot.
- **Hourly / seasonal restrictions ⚠️:** There is NO general hourly restriction on walking - unlike NP Mala Fatra there is no 6:00-21:00 movement window; you may hike the Beskid ridges at night and night ascents of Lysa hora for sunrise are a local tradition. The hours that bind are camping hours only: tent up no earlier than 18:00, camp struck and site cleared by 09:00, at every nouzove nocoviste (Skalka, Lysa hora, Pustevny). That 18:00 floor is a real planning constraint - it means arriving at camp at 17:00 buys you nothing, and it pairs badly with an early-start Saturday only if you forget you must be packed by 09:00. Seasonally: the sites are year-round (Skalka explicitly 'v provozu celorocne'); the only dated seasonal closure in the CHKO is the winter Pulcin ice-fall route (1 Jan - 30 Mar). Chata opening hours matter for resupply - Bezrucova chata and the summit huts serve food into the evening but are not 24 h; Skalka reception is your registration point, so arrive while it is staffed. [uncertain] Skalka reception's exact staffed hours.
- **Fine risk ⚠️:** Camping or lighting a fire outside a designated place in a CHKO is a przestepstwo administracyjne under Act 114/1992 Sb. Headline maxima quoted in Czech media (up to 2,000,000 CZK) apply to legal entities and to serious habitat damage - not to a hiker with a tent. For an individual the realistic exposure is a fine of a few thousand crowns: press reporting on Czech nature-sleeping enforcement cites 5,000 CZK penalties, and CHKO guidance warns of 'pokuty v radu tisicu korun'. On-the-spot fines are issued by CHKO rangers (straz prirody) and Police CR; rangers can and do check the Lysa hora area, and the 2026 press coverage explicitly says the new sites are monitored by conservation staff. Lighting a fire in a forest additionally engages Act 133/1985 Sb. (fire protection), with much steeper maxima and, during a declared regional fire ban, near-certain prosecution. Off-trail entry inside an NPR (e.g. Mazak on Lysa hora) is a separate offence. Practical read: the risk is low if you use a designated site, moderate if you bivouac quietly off-trail on the border ridge, and high anywhere near Lysa hora, Pustevny or Radhost where ranger presence and visitor density are greatest. [uncertain] The precise statutory tier (Act 114/1992 sec. 87 sub-paragraph) that camping falls under, and hence the individual maximum - reported figures range from 10,000 to 100,000 CZK depending on source.

**Route & Camps**

- **Suggested route ⚠️:** RECOMMENDED - the border-ridge traverse, Skalka to Lysa hora, linking the two legal camps. FRIDAY: drive Ustron -> Mosty u Jablunkova (~45-60 min), park at the railway station, walk the RED trail up through forest to Skalka 932 m and Chata KCT Skalka - approx. 4.5-5 km, +450-500 m, 1 h 15 - 1 h 30. SATURDAY (the long day): red ridge west/north along the CZ-SK state border - Skalka -> Murinkovy vrch -> Burkov vrch 1032 m -> Cubunov -> Pod Malym Polomem 1061 m -> Maly Polom -> Velky Polom 1067 m -> Bily Kriz (chatas Tetrev / KCT Sulov, road, buses) -> Visalaje, approx. 23 km along an undulating, mostly forested ridge with modest cumulative gain (roughly +900/-1000 m). From Visalaje climb the marked trail to Lysa hora 1323 m, a further ~6-7 km and +550-600 m. Saturday total approx. 29-30 km / +1500 m / 9-11 h moving - a big but not extreme day on good, well-marked, non-technical paths. SUNDAY: descend Lysa hora on the classic red 'Bezrucova cesta' to Ostravice, ~9 km / -900 m, 2.5-3 h, then train back to Mosty u Jablunkova (1-2 changes, ~2-2.5 h). Difficulty: easy technically (T1-T2 forest tracks and gravel roads, no exposure, no chains), the challenge is purely distance. LIGHTER ALTERNATIVE if 29 km is too much: Friday camp at Skalka, Saturday ridge only as far as Bily Kriz/Visalaje (~23 km) and finish there by bus - but note there is NO legal camp at Bily Kriz, so this must end as a day-hike-out, not a second night. LOWER-EFFORT ALTERNATIVE: base at Taboriste Pulcin (Zlinsky kraj) for both nights and do the Pulcinske skaly / Makyta circuits - legal for multiple nights, but a ~1 h 45 - 2 h drive, which kills the Friday-evening budget. [uncertain] Exact ridge kilometrage and elevation gain - the 23 km Mosty-Bily Kriz-Visalaje figure comes from a Czech ridge-route description, the Visalaje-Lysa hora and Lysa hora-Ostravice legs are my map estimates; verify on mapy.com before committing.
- **Friday-evening feasibility:** THE BEST FRIDAY BUDGET OF ANY OPTION IN THIS RESEARCH - but only for the Skalka variant, and the 18:00 camping floor changes the arithmetic in your favour. Worked example for late July (Ustron sunset ~20:40, usable dusk to ~21:15): leave Ustron 17:00-17:30 after work, arrive Mosty u Jablunkova 18:15-18:30, on the trail by 18:45, at Chata Skalka 20:00-20:15 - in daylight, with the 18:00 pitching floor already satisfied, reception still likely open to take the 50 CZK registration. Even a 18:30 departure from Ustron leaves you arriving ~21:15 with a headtorch for the last 20 minutes on an easy forest track. This comfortably beats the '<= 1.5 h of walking, minimal darkness' rule. IMPORTANT CONSTRAINT SPECIFIC TO THIS RANGE: you cannot arrive early and settle in - the tent may not go up before 18:00, so an unusually early finish just means an hour sitting at the chata (which is fine - it serves food). CONTRAST: the Lysa hora variant as a Friday target is NOT feasible - 70-85 min drive plus a 2-2.5 h, +700-900 m ascent from Ostravice/Visalaje means a 22:00-23:00 arrival in full darkness. Pulcin (~2 h drive) and Uvez (~1 h 30 plus walk-in) are also outside a comfortable Friday budget. So: Friday = Skalka, always.
- **Camp 1:** NOUZOVE TABORISTE AT CHATA KCT SKALKA, above Mosty u Jablunkova, Skalka summit 932 m, on the CZ/SK border ridge at the eastern end of the Moravskoslezske Beskydy. NAME/OPERATOR: Klub ceskych turistu, chata caretaker Josef Szotkowski; chata-skalka.cz, +420 608 566 008 / +420 555 500 540. LOCATION: on the first clearing at the top of the red-marked climb from Mosty u Jablunkova railway station (~4.5-5 km, +450-500 m, 1 h 15-1 h 30); the pitches are a levelled, compacted terrace right by the hut, with a timber shelter and an approved fire ring. LEGAL BASIS: a formal exemption from the Act 114/1992 camping-and-fire ban, granted by Sprava CHKO Beskydy / AOPK CR, opened April 2024 - it is the flagship site of the whole nouzova-nocoviste programme, so its legality is documented and uncontroversial. RULES: max 20 people / 10 tents or tarps; year-round; ADVANCE REGISTRATION REQUIRED (online or at reception); 50 CZK per person per night; tents only between 18:00 and 09:00; one night only; fire only in the designated pit and nowhere else; foot access only. PERKS that make it unusually comfortable for a wild-ish camp: campers may use the chata's toilets, showers, power sockets and buy food and drink - so you carry no water for the first night and can charge phones. This is the single strongest Friday-night camp in the whole comparison set.
- **Camp 2:** NOUZOVE TABORISTE BELOW BEZRUCOVA CHATA, LYSA HORA 1323 m - the highest point of the Moravskoslezske Beskydy and of the whole Beskid Silesian-Moravian complex. LOCATION: a grassy area immediately below the KCT Bezrucova chata on the summit plateau (approx. N 49 32.7', E 18 26.9'), reached from Visalaje/Krasna (south), Ostravice (north-east, red 'Bezrucova cesta') or Malenovice (north-west). LEGAL BASIS: the same AOPK CR exemption regime, reported live from June 2026 - Czech coverage on 24 June 2026 ran under the headline 'Beskydy povolily bezplatne stanovani u Lyse hory'. RULES: FREE, no registration reported, tents 18:00-09:00, one night only, STRICT no-fire (unlike Skalka there is no fire pit here), carry out all waste, monitored by conservation staff. ADVANTAGES: two staffed huts on the summit (Bezrucova chata and Chata Emil Zatopek) selling food, drink and water, so again zero water carry; guaranteed sunset and sunrise above the surrounding forest; a meteorological station and permanent staffing means help is close. DRAWBACKS: it is the most visited peak in the range with heavy day-hiker traffic until dusk; it is also the windiest and wettest spot in the Beskids - an exposed summit tent in a July thunderstorm is a genuinely bad place to be, so bring a storm-worthy pitch or be ready to retreat into a hut bunk (double room ~1200 CZK, ~800 CZK single occupancy). The north-eastern flank is NPR Mazak with a statutory off-trail entry ban, so there is no legal fallback pitch in the trees below. THIRD OPTION IF NEEDED: Pustevny (~1015 m), added June 2026, same rules, sits mid-range between Skalka and Lysa hora but far off the border-ridge line. FOURTH: utulna Uvez near Velky Javornik - a free 2024 A-frame shelter, max 3 people, no fire, no booking - useful only for a Roznov-side itinerary.
- **Actual spacing vs 15-25 km target ⚠️:** MEASURED AGAINST THE 15-25 KM CRITERION: the two flagship legal camps are Skalka -> Lysa hora, approx. 29-30 km along the marked red ridge and connector (23 km Mosty/Skalka -> Bily Kriz -> Visalaje, plus ~6-7 km and +550-600 m up to the summit). That is ABOVE the 15-25 km target, not below it - the opposite of the Polish Beskids/Gorce problem where legal sites cluster too close together. This is an honest mismatch and it is the main planning tension in this range: you either accept a 29-30 km, 9-11 h Saturday, or you break the rules somewhere in between (there is no legal camp at Velky Polom, Bily Kriz or Visalaje). Sub-options and their spacings: Skalka -> Pustevny is roughly 40+ km (too far for one day); Pustevny -> Lysa hora approx. 18-20 km via Radhost-Martinak-Bily Kriz-type links, which WOULD hit the criterion but requires a Pustevny start that is a 1 h 30 drive from Ustron and therefore not Friday-feasible; Pulcin and Uvez are standalone, not part of any two-camp chain. VERDICT: the spacing criterion is met only by the Pustevny -> Lysa hora pairing, which fails the Friday-drive test; the Friday-feasible pairing (Skalka -> Lysa hora) overshoots by ~5 km. [uncertain] All figures are map estimates, not GPS-measured; the Pustevny-Lysa hora link distance in particular is a rough read.
- **Water sources ⚠️:** Best water security of any option in this comparison, because the itinerary is a chain of staffed huts rather than a dry crest. RELIABLE, HUMAN-SUPPLIED: Chata Skalka (taps, showers, bar - fill up at camp 1); Hotel Tetrev and the KCT Sulov shelter at Bily Kriz, roughly midway on the Saturday leg; refreshments at Visalaje; on Lysa hora both Bezrucova chata and Chata Emil Zatopek sell drinks and water. Restaurants in Mosty u Jablunkova at the start. Practical consequence: you never need to carry more than ~1.5-2 l, and you can plan the whole trip without a filter - a real weight saving versus the Slovak ridges. NATURAL SOURCES: the Beskids are wet sandstone-flysch mountains with numerous springs and streams in the valleys, and Lysa hora is the rainiest place in the range - but the RIDGE itself between Skalka and Bily Kriz runs along the watershed with few springs directly on the crest, and 2026 has been a drought year in this corner of the country (Zlinsky kraj declared drought-driven fire restrictions in May 2026). DO NOT plan on finding a flowing spring on the border ridge in late July. Lysa hora summit has no natural water source of its own. RULE OF THUMB: fill at Skalka before leaving on Saturday morning, top up at Bily Kriz (Tetrev/Sulov), top up again at Visalaje before the final climb, and buy at the summit. Fallback: treat/filter stream water in the valleys (Ostravice, Recice), never on the crest. [uncertain] Whether any specific marked studanka on the Skalka-Velky Polom-Bily Kriz section is flowing in a drought July - I found no source confirming a named, reliable spring on that stretch, so treat the ridge as dry.
- **Fire / cooking:** GAS/LIQUID-FUEL STOVE IS THE DEFAULT AND THE ONLY THING YOU SHOULD PLAN AROUND. Open fire is banned across the whole CHKO outside designated places (Act 114/1992 Sb.), and at Lysa hora and Pustevny the no-fire rule is explicit and strict - Czech reporting on the new sites stresses 'prisny zakaz rozdelavani ohne' and notes that a tourist stove is the tolerated substitute. TWO EXCEPTIONS where a legal fire exists on paper: Chata Skalka has an approved fire pit (fire outside that pit is 'prisne zakazano'), and Taboriste Pulcin supplies fire rings and even firewood. BOTH ARE CURRENTLY SUSPECT: Zlinsky kraj (Pulcin) has banned all open flame in the countryside since 6 May 2026 until further notice due to drought, and a comparable declaration in Moravskoslezsky kraj would close the Skalka pit too. NOTE the AOPK CHKO Beskydy page has at times been read as prohibiting even stoves at the nouzova nocoviste, while news coverage says stoves are tolerated - if a ranger appears, a low-profile canister stove used carefully on bare ground is the defensible choice; an open flame is not. Verify the regional fire declaration before departure.

**Safety & Costs**

- **Rescue / insurance:** DIFFERENT FROM BOTH POLAND AND SLOVAKIA - DO NOT ASSUME 'CZECH = FREE'. Horska sluzba CR operates a Beskydy district and publishes a price list of special services, valid from 1 Jan 2024, charged in euro and applied to everyone including foreigners: treatment at a rescue station EUR 60; rescue/transport up to 3 km with up to 3 rescuers EUR 230; over 3 km or with 4-10 rescuers EUR 460; a surcharge of EUR 25 per additional rescuer per hour beyond 10. Helicopter (LZS) medical evacuation is part of the state emergency medical service and is NOT charged to the patient the way Slovak helicopter rescue is - the big four-figure helicopter bill that makes Slovakia dangerous is not the Czech pattern. Horska sluzba explicitly warns that it is not a healthcare provider and that the EHIC does not cover its services. WHAT THIS MEANS FOR A POLE: carry the EHIC/EKUZ for hospital and ambulance treatment (Poland-Czechia cross-border cover works normally), and add a cheap travel/mountain policy that names 'zasah horske sluzby' or mountain rescue - a EUR 300-500 exposure is small enough that many Polish tourist policies (PZU, Warta, Signal Iduna 'sporty gorskie' variants) cover it for a few zloty a day. Bottom line: materially cheaper and lower-risk than Slovakia's HZS regime, but not free like GOPR in Poland. Beskydy dispatch and weather: horskasluzba.cz; emergency 112 (or 155 for medical).
- **Bears / ticks:** BEARS: present but rare and transient - the Beskids are the only part of Czechia with regular brown-bear records, all of them animals wandering in from the Slovak Carpathian population, typically one to a few individuals at a time and not resident. Winter carnivore mapping in 2025 found bear sign in the Kysucke Beskydy and Kysucka vrchovina immediately across the border, and 2026 brought increasing bear reports on the Slovak side near the Zlinsky kraj boundary. Wolves and lynx are genuinely resident (CHKO Beskydy is their Czech stronghold) but pose no practical risk. Practical bear protocol is mild compared with Slovakia: hang or bag food away from the tent, do not cook in the porch, make noise on overgrown sections - a bear spray is not standard practice on the Czech side and is not required. NOTE the Skalka/Velky Polom camp and ridge sit ON the Slovak border, i.e. exactly where transient bears cross, so apply Slovak-grade food hygiene there. TICKS AND TBE: this is the real risk. Czechia has one of the highest tick-borne-encephalitis incidences in Europe and the Moravian-Silesian and Zlin regions are endemic; Lyme borreliosis is also common. Ticks are active at exactly the July-August window and thrive in the grassy clearings that the camping sites use. Countermeasures: TBE vaccination (FSME-IMMUN/Encepur, a 3-dose course - too late to start for this weekend but the right move before next season), permethrin-treated trousers/socks or long trousers tucked into socks, DEET/icaridin on skin, and a full body check every evening and again the next morning; remove ticks with a fine tweezers/tick key and record the date. Avoid sitting directly on grass at the Skalka and Lysa hora pitches - use a groundsheet.
- **Weather / storms:** Classic Central-European July-August pattern, amplified by orography: warm humid mornings, convective build-up from late morning, and afternoon/early-evening thunderstorms with heavy downpours, gusts and lightning, most often between 14:00 and 19:00. Lysa hora is the wettest and windiest station in the Beskids and one of the wettest places in Czechia, holding national rainfall records - it makes its own weather and is regularly in cloud when valleys are clear, so a summit camp can be a gale-and-fog experience even on a fine day. The ridge from Skalka to Bily Kriz is largely FORESTED, which is a real safety advantage: unlike the Slovak open crests you are rarely the tallest object, so the classic 'be off the ridge by 15:00' rule can be relaxed to 'be off the open summits and clearings by 15:00-16:00'. The exposed points that do demand the 15:00 discipline are Velky/Maly Polom's clearings, the Bily Kriz saddle and above all the treeless Lysa hora plateau. Planning rule for the recommended itinerary: start Saturday at 08:00-08:30 (you must be packed by 09:00 anyway), aim to be at Visalaje by mid-afternoon, and make the final Lysa hora climb only if the sky is stable - otherwise sit out the cell at Visalaje and go up late. 2026 has additionally been a drought year with fire warnings, so expect hot, dry spells punctuated by violent isolated storms. Forecasts: chmi.cz, horskasluzba.cz (Beskydy/Pustevny station pages), windy.com.
- **Bail-out plan:** Unusually good - this is a busy, hut-dense, road-crossed range and you are never far from an exit. ALONG THE RIDGE: Chata KCT Skalka (staffed, beds, phone, road access from Mosty); Hotel Tetrev and the KCT Sulov shelter at Bily Kriz, roughly midway on the Saturday leg, sitting on a public road with a car park and weekend buses; Visalaje (restaurant, bus stop, road down to Krasna/Ostravice). ON LYSA HORA: two staffed huts (Bezrucova chata, Chata Emil Zatopek), a permanently manned meteorological/transmitter station and heavy day-hiker traffic - the single safest 'bad night' bail-out in the whole comparison set, because you can simply buy a bunk. VALLEY EXITS: Mosty u Jablunkova (rail on the Bohumin-Cadca corridor), Ostravice (branch-line terminus), Bila and Stare Hamry on the I/56 with buses. GSM: coverage on the border ridge and on Lysa hora is good - the ridge carries Czech and Slovak masts and the summit has telecom infrastructure; wooded saddles and the north-east flank toward Ostravice are the weak spots. Polish SIMs roam free in the EU; save 112 and note that on the border ridge 112 may connect to Slovak dispatch. Nearest hospitals: Trinec (Podlesi) and Frydek-Mistek. Carry the Horska sluzba Beskydy number and the chata numbers offline.
- **Total fees ⚠️:** Cheapest of the realistic options. Typical worked total for two people, Friday-Sunday, Skalka -> Lysa hora itinerary: parking at Mosty u Jablunkova station 0 CZK (free/nominal); Skalka camping 50 CZK/person/night = 100 CZK; Lysa hora camping 0 CZK; motorway vignette 0 CZK if routed on I/11 (else 300 CZK for a 10-day e-vignette); train Ostravice -> Mosty u Jablunkova on Sunday roughly 150-200 CZK/person = 300-400 CZK; no park entry fee, no reservation fee, no permit. TOTAL roughly 400-500 CZK (~70-90 PLN) for two, before food and drink at the huts. If you instead park at Malenovice or Ostravice add 50-150 CZK/day. Pulcin variant: 10 CZK/person/night plus a longer drive. There is NO national-park entry ticket in a CHKO. [uncertain] 2026 train fares and Malenovice/Ostravice parking tariffs.

**Season, Crowds & Logistics**

- **Season fit:** Excellent for late July - August, and the best all-round seasonal fit in this comparison. Ridge altitudes of 900-1320 m mean pleasant temperatures (typically 18-25 C by day on the crest, 8-14 C at night on Lysa hora - take a comfort-5 C bag for the summit camp, the summit is markedly colder and windier than the forecast for the valley). Trails are dry, snow-free and non-technical; the forested character gives shade on hot days, which the open Slovak crests do not. Sites are open year-round so there is no season window to miss. The seasonal downsides are the July-August thunderstorm pattern, peak tick activity, and 2026's drought (dry springs, elevated fire risk, likely open-flame bans). Blueberries and mushrooms are in season on the ridge - picking for personal use is legal in a CHKO. Long daylight (sunset ~20:40 at end of July) is what makes the Friday-evening Skalka approach work.
- **Crowd level:** Sharply two-tier. LYSA HORA IS THE MOST VISITED PEAK IN THE BESKIDS - a summer Saturday brings hundreds of walkers and runners, the Malenovice and Ostravice car parks fill by mid-morning, and the summit plateau is busy until dusk; a tent pitched there at 18:00 will have an audience for a couple of hours. THE BORDER RIDGE Skalka - Murinkovy vrch - Burkov vrch - Maly/Velky Polom IS QUIET - it is a long-distance corridor rather than a day-trip target, and Chata Skalka is a small hut, not a resort. Pustevny and Radhost are the other crowd magnet (chairlift, coach parties) and are worth avoiding on a Saturday. The capped 20-person / 10-tent Skalka site and the mandatory pre-registration mean the Friday camp cannot become a festival - but it also means it CAN sell out on a fine summer weekend, so book. FRIDAY-EVENING ADVANTAGE: substantial. You beat the Saturday-morning car-park crush entirely, you have the Skalka approach almost to yourself at 19:00, and you meet the Lysa hora crowds only as they are leaving on Saturday evening. Czech school holidays run all July and August, so weekend day-visitor pressure is at its annual maximum.
- **Navigation / maps:** PRIMARY TOOL: mapy.com (formerly mapy.cz) by Seznam - the Czech turisticka layer is the best hiking mapping in Central Europe, renders every KCT marked trail and signpost, works offline after a region download, and covers the Slovak and Polish sides too, so one app carries the whole border ridge. Secondary: KCT paper sheet no. 96 (Moravskoslezske Beskydy) and no. 98; OsmAnd or Locus Map with OSM data as a backup GPX engine; hiking.sk if you stray onto the Slovak side of the crest. MARKING: the Czech KCT system is the ORIGINAL of the four-colour scheme Poland uses, so it reads natively to a Polish hiker - red for the main long-distance ridge (here the border hrebenovka and the Bezrucova cesta up Lysa hora), blue/green/yellow for connectors, with the same white-colour-white bar and the same painted turn arrows. Differences to expect: Czech signposts (rozcestniky) are named and give times as well as distances, chatas rather than summits are used as reference points, and the ridge signage is duplicated in Slovak on the border section - do not be confused by seeing Slovak place names on the same post. Practical note: the marked trails over the Travny massif were abolished in 2020 and may still appear on old maps and old GPX tracks - ignore them. Also cross-check any route against the NPR off-trail bans (Mazak on Lysa hora, Pulcin-Hradisko) before drawing a shortcut. Verify the nouzova nocoviste on beskydy.aopk.gov.cz in the departure week - the network changed twice in 2026 alone.

> ⚠️ _Fields above contain self-flagged agent estimates/caveats (verify before departure): Distance / time from Ustroń, Parking, Public transport, Camping rules, 2026 status (live blockers), Hourly / seasonal restrictions, Fine risk, Suggested route, Actual spacing vs 15-25 km target, Water sources, Total fees_

---

### 6. Kysucke Beskydy / CHKO Kysuce (SK)

**Access & Travel**

- **Distance / time from Ustroń ⚠️:** ESTIMATED BY MAP READING, NOT ROUTE-ENGINE VERIFIED - treat as +/- 15%. This is the nearest Slovak range to Ustron and the scope's '1 h - 1 h 15' bracket is broadly right for the main trailhead, slightly optimistic for the far end. (a) Oscadnica - Laliky / Dedovka (the standard Velka Raca trailhead, 573 m): ~65-75 km, ~1 h 10 - 1 h 30, two viable routes - EITHER Ustron - Wisla - Milowka - Rajcza - Zwardon border - Skalite - Cadca - Oscadnica (all Polish/Slovak first-class roads), OR the slightly faster Ustron - Wisla - Istebna - Jablunkov (CZ) - Svrcinovec - Cadca - Oscadnica, which clips a corner of Czechia. (b) Zwardon (Polish trailhead for the same border ridge, ~700 m, useful as a high-altitude Friday start): ~55 km, ~55-65 min - the fastest way onto this crest from Ustron. (c) Novy Bystrica / Vychylovka side of CHKO Kysuce: ~85-95 km, ~1 h 30. (d) Makov / Bumbalka pass (western, Javorniky end of CHKO Kysuce): ~90-100 km, ~1 h 30 - 1 h 45 - NOT a 1 h drive, the scope's grouping of Bumbalka with Velka Raca overstates its proximity.
