So a one- or two-night tent at the four anchors is inside the rules. There is no permit, no booking and no fee for the tent itself as such, but courtesy (and in practice the hut keepers' expectation) is to check in at the hut, buy something, and use its toilet/water - Chata pod Chlebom in particular runs on an eco/off-grid setup. Dogs are allowed only on a leash. No hard published cap on party size was found; the sites are small clearings, so 1-3 tents is the realistic capacity at each [uncertain]. There is no Slovak equivalent of the Polish 'Zanocuj w lesie' programme - outside these four spots the fallback is the Forest Act 326/2005 §31, which likewise forbids camping on forest land outside designated places.
- **2026 status (live blockers) ⚠️:** Checked 2026-07-28. (1) BEAR INCIDENT, still the most operationally relevant item: on 2 June 2026 a she-bear attacked and seriously injured a 36-year-old man at Stráňavy (Žilina district, on the NW foot of the Krivánska Malá Fatra), on a marked trail at the forest/meadow edge. The intervention team documented multiple bear signs, the animal was not found, a camera trap was installed, and the municipality of Stráňavy asked residents and visitors not to enter the forest until further notice. Whether that local appeal is still in force in late July was not confirmed [uncertain] - re-check the obec Stráňavy and Správa NP Malá Fatra sites in the departure week. (2) FIRE RISK: as of late July 2026 the Slovak Ministry of Agriculture has issued a nationwide warning of significantly elevated forest-fire risk (prolonged heat, drought, locally strong wind); most of Slovakia has been placed in the top fire-danger category on recent weekends, some state/military forest districts have already declared a 'čas zvýšeného nebezpečenstva vzniku požiaru' with temporary forest-entry bans (e.g. Levočské vrchy). No entry ban specific to NP Malá Fatra was found [uncertain], but the practical consequence is: assume NO open fire at all, and expect that a ban could appear at short notice. (3) TRAILS: the official NP Malá Fatra closure page lists no extraordinary 2026 closures beyond the routine spring ones, which have already expired (they run 1 March - 15 June). Re-check https://www.npmalafatra.sk/navstevnici/uzavery-turistickych-chodnikov/ in the departure week - storm/windthrow closures are typically announced there at a few days' notice. (4) Both key huts (Chata pod Suchým, Chata pod Chlebom) are reported open year-round; confirm opening hours and water availability by phone before relying on them.
- **Hourly / seasonal restrictions ⚠️:** HARD BLOCKER - this is the single biggest planning constraint of this range. The visitor regulations allow movement on marked trails ONLY between 06:00 and 21:00 in the summer period (16 April - 31 October), and 07:00-18:00 in winter (1 November - 15 April). The restriction does NOT apply in the immediate surroundings of existing buildings (i.e. once you are at the hut you are fine), but it does apply to the whole walk-in. Practical consequence for a Friday-evening start: to reach Chata pod Suchým legally you must be past the park boundary and at the hut by 21:00, which means starting to walk from Nezbudská Lúčka by about 18:00-18:15 (2 h 40 - 3 h 00 of ascent), which in turn means leaving Ustroń by roughly 15:30-16:00 - earlier than the 17:30-18:00 assumed in the workspace outline, because the ascent is closer to 3 h than to 2 h with a full pack. SEASONAL CLOSURES: every year from 1 March to 15 June three sections are closed for wildlife breeding and post-winter erosion - Medziholie - Veľký Rozsutec - Medzirozsutce (red), Obšívanka - Malé nocľahy (blue), and Vendovka - Malý Kriváň (blue); together about 6 km out of the 150+ km network. For a July/August trip these are all REOPEN, so they are not a blocker for the current season - but note that Vendovka - Malý Kriváň sits directly on the planned day-2 corridor, so the same route in spring would be broken. Cable car Vrátna - Snilovské sedlo operates roughly 08:00-16:00/17:00 in summer, which is irrelevant for a Friday-evening arrival but matters if you plan to use it as a bail-out [uncertain].
- **Fine risk ⚠️:** Slovak side (Act 543/2002 Z.z. on nature and landscape protection + Forest Act 326/2005). Camping outside designated places, lighting a fire, leaving marked trails or breaching the národný park visitor regulations are 'priestupky' (misdemeanours). On-the-spot (bloková) fines run up to about 300 EUR; in full administrative proceedings the statutory range for natural persons runs from roughly 331.93 EUR up to 9,958.17 EUR depending on severity, though the amounts actually issued for a single illegal tent are at the low end - a warning or a two/three-digit block fine is the normal outcome [uncertain]. Enforcement bodies you can actually meet on this ridge: the park rangers (stráž prírody, Správa NP Malá Fatra), the forest service, and the police. Practical exposure is real rather than theoretical here, because the four legal anchors are all at manned huts and rangers know exactly which spots are legal - a tent 500 m up the ridge from Chata pod Chlebom, inside NNR Chleb, is a visible and easily-proved violation. For reference, the Polish equivalent (mandat karny for wild camping in a national park) is typically 100-500 PLN, up to 1,000 PLN.

**Route & Camps**

- **Suggested route:** Three-part linear traverse of the Krivánska ridge (red main ridge trail, 'hrebeňovka'), Nezbudská Lúčka -> Štefanová/Terchová, roughly 33-35 km and ~2,300-2,600 m of ascent in total. DAY 0 (Friday evening): Nezbudská Lúčka (~360 m) -> Starhrad (475 m) -> Chata pod Suchým (1,075 m). 7.3 km, +709 m by the red trail via Starhrad, 2 h 40 - 3 h 37 depending on source and pack weight; blue valley variant is slightly shorter but steeper (+775 m). Moderate, well-marked, no exposure. DAY 1 (Saturday, the big day): Chata pod Suchým -> sedlo pod Suchým -> Suchý (1,468 m) -> Vráta -> Malý Kriváň (1,671 m) -> Veľký Kriváň (1,709 m, range high point) -> Snilovské sedlo (1,524 m, cable-car top station) -> Chleb (1,646 m) -> descend south to Chata pod Chlebom (1,423 m). Approx. 13-15 km, +1,300-1,450 m / -900 m, 6 h - 7 h 30 with a pack. Alpine grassy ridge, several chained/rocky steps around Suchý and the Kriváň section, fully exposed to weather - this is the 'be off the ridge by mid-afternoon' segment. DAY 2 (Sunday): Chata pod Chlebom -> back up to the ridge at Chleb/Hromové -> Steny -> Poludňový grúň (1,450 m) -> Stoh (1,607 m) -> Medziholie -> descend to Štefanová -> Terchová. Approx. 11-13 km, +550 m / -1,300 m, 5-6 h. Optional add-on: Veľký Rozsutec (1,610 m) from Medziholie (+1 h 30 return, chains/ladders, closed 1 March - 15 June - open in summer). Return leg: bus Štefanová/Terchová -> Varín, then bus or train to Nezbudská Lúčka for the car. Overall difficulty: moderate-to-demanding mountain hiking (T2-T3), no technical gear needed in summer, but chains and steep grass make it slow in rain.
- **Friday-evening feasibility ⚠️:** TIGHT - this is the weakest point of the whole option and it is the direct consequence of the 21:00 curfew. Arithmetic for late July (sunset in Žilina ~20:35-20:45, usable civil twilight to ~21:15): drive Ustroń -> Nezbudská Lúčka ~2 h; ascent to Chata pod Suchým 2 h 40 - 3 h 00 with a full pack (the guidebook 2 h 40 assumes a day pack). To be at the hut by the 21:00 legal cut-off you must start walking by ~18:00, i.e. leave Ustroń by ~15:45-16:00. A post-work 17:30-18:00 departure from Ustroń (the workspace assumption) puts you on the trail at 19:30-20:00 and at the hut at 22:15-23:00 - after dark AND in breach of the visitor regulations. VERDICT: feasible only with a half-day off on Friday or an early finish (leave by 16:00 at the latest). MITIGATIONS if you cannot leave before 17:00: (a) start from Vrátna/Starý dvor and camp at Chata na Grúni (1,100 m) instead - only ~45-60 min of ascent, so a 20:00 trailhead arrival still works, at the cost of shifting the whole trip east and shortening day 2; (b) start from Šútovo up the Šútovská dolina to Chata pod Chlebom (steep, ~2 h 30-3 h, and the lower valley is a national nature reserve - do not stop short of the hut) [uncertain]; (c) sleep Friday in the valley (Nezbudská Lúčka / Terchová accommodation or a legal campsite) and start at 06:00 Saturday, converting the trip into two long walking days; (d) accept a paid bunk at Chata pod Suchým for night 1 and use the tent only on night 2. Option (a) is the cleanest 'leave after work' fix and keeps everything legal.
- **Camp 1:** Chata pod Suchým (1,075 m), on the western end of the ridge above Nezbudská Lúčka / Strečno. Legal basis: named in the NP Malá Fatra visitor regulations as one of four places where camping and bivouacking are allowed, within 100 m of the building. It is a manned, year-round hut (meals, drinks, bunks, water, toilet) sitting in a small clearing on the forest edge just below the ridge crest - sheltered from ridge wind, which is exactly what you want for a night-one tent. Access: 7.3 km / +709 m from Nezbudská Lúčka railway halt via Starhrad (red), or ~2 h from Strečno/Varín on the green trail. Pitching ground is limited (a few flat patches around the hut), so arriving after dark on a busy Friday you may be squeezed - another argument for the earlier departure. Etiquette: announce yourself at the hut, use its water and toilet, no fire.
- **Camp 2 ⚠️:** Chata pod Chlebom (1,423 m), the highest tourist hut in Malá Fatra, on the Predná úboč locality on the SOUTHERN slope just below the main ridge between Chleb and Snilovské sedlo. Legal basis: named in the visitor regulations, camping/bivouac allowed within 100 m of the building BUT explicitly excluding the area of the Chleb national nature reserve - in practice this means you must pitch on the hut's own slope terrace side, not up towards the ridge/reserve. The hut was rebuilt after two fires as a modern off-grid/eco building, open year-round, open to non-residents daily 08:00-22:00 with the kitchen to 21:00 - so an evening arrival still gets a hot meal. It was deliberately sited next to a spring, which makes it the single most reliable water point on the whole ridge. Reaching it costs a ~200 m descent off the ridge from Chleb (and the same climb back on Sunday morning) - budget 25-30 min each way. Space around the hut is genuinely small; on a peak-season Saturday expect competition for flat ground [uncertain].
- **Actual spacing vs 15-25 km target ⚠️:** Camp 1 (Chata pod Suchým) -> Camp 2 (Chata pod Chlebom) is approximately 13-15 km along the ridge with about 1,300-1,450 m of cumulative ascent - ESTIMATED by subtraction from published stage data rather than measured, because no source gives this exact pair [uncertain]. Cross-check: hiking.sk's 4-day traverse gives 'Chata pod Chlebom -> Veľký Kriváň -> Malý Kriváň -> Suchý -> Starhrad -> ferry -> Špicák' as 20 km, of which the Chata pod Suchým -> Starhrad -> ferry -> Špicák tail is ~7-8 km; the Žilina region's figure of 35 km for the whole Nezbudská Lúčka -> Zázrivá ridge with Chata pod Suchým at km ~7.3 and Chata pod Chlebom 'about midway' points the same way. VERDICT vs the 15-25 km criterion: this sits AT OR JUST BELOW the lower threshold - honestly, ~13-15 km, not 15-25 km. It is however far more demanding than the raw distance suggests (+1,400 m over an alpine ridge with chained steps), so in TIME it behaves like a 20 km day (6-7.5 h). If you want to genuinely hit 18-20 km, extend day 1 past Chata pod Chlebom over Hromové/Steny to Poludňový grúň and back down, or start day 0 lower (Strečno instead of Nezbudská Lúčka) - but there is no legal third camping anchor in between, so the spacing is fixed by where the law lets you sleep, not by the terrain.
- **Water sources:** Genuinely thin on the crest - plan to carry 2-3 l for the ridge day. RELIABLE: Chata pod Suchým (hut water, camp 1); Chata pod Chlebom (hut plus the spring it was built next to - the most dependable point on the ridge, camp 2); the cable-car top station at Snilovské sedlo (refill possible during operating hours, roughly 08:00-16:00/17:00 in summer). SEMI-RELIABLE: a spring in the saddle below Malý Kriváň, described by ridge-traverse write-ups as abundant and effectively the only true spring on the crest itself; 'prameň pod Koniarkami' is also named in stage descriptions. UNRELIABLE / DO NOT COUNT ON: the spring near Medziholie is repeatedly reported as barely flowing; springs in the Lúčanská continuation are described as so slow that collecting water takes very long. In the 2026 drought summer, downgrade every non-hut source by one level and treat Chata pod Chlebom as the only guaranteed refill between the valleys. Valley refills: Nezbudská Lúčka before the ascent, Štefanová/Terchová at the finish. Boil or filter anything not from a hut tap.
- **Fire / cooking ⚠️:** No open fire in practice. The visitor regulations permit fire only at built fire-rings within 10 m of existing structures outside settlement boundaries - i.e. at a hut's own fireplace, with the hut's consent, and nowhere else. Lighting a fire anywhere else in the park is a misdemeanour under Act 543/2002. Additionally, in late July 2026 Slovakia is under a nationwide elevated-forest-fire-risk warning, which makes any fire a bad idea regardless of the letter of the rules. Gas/liquid-fuel stoves: not explicitly prohibited by the visitor regulations and universally used at the designated bivouac spots; the safe reading is that a stove is acceptable at the four designated camping anchors (on a non-flammable base, away from dry grass), but is NOT a licence to cook anywhere on the ridge [uncertain]. Bring a canister stove, plan cold breakfasts as a fallback, and carry all rubbish out.

**Safety & Costs**

- **Rescue / insurance:** BUY A POLICY - Slovakia is not Poland. Under Slovak law any person in the mountains is liable for the cost of Horská záchranná služba (HZS) rescue or search operations (only minors and persons without legal capacity are exempt); Polish GOPR/TOPR being free does not carry over. Typical costs: an average ground intervention ~400 EUR; a search-and-rescue or helicopter operation 2,000-4,000 EUR, with helicopter flight time alone billed at 60 EUR+ per minute (a 30-45 min operation = 1,800-2,700 EUR). Minimum sensible cover for HZS costs is 20,000 EUR; a standard Polish/Slovak 'poistenie do hôr' or an EU travel policy with a mountain-activities rider costs roughly 1-3 EUR per person per day. EHIC/EKUZ covers hospital treatment but NOT the rescue/transport bill - this is the classic trap. Check that the policy explicitly names 'turistika/vysokohorská turistika' and does not exclude unorganised overnight camping. HZS emergency number 18 300 (or 112); the HZS mobile app 'Horská záchranná služba' transmits coordinates and is worth installing.
- **Bears / ticks:** BEARS: this is bear country and Malá Fatra has an above-average incident record. Most recent: 2 June 2026, Stráňavy (NW foot of the range, Žilina district) - a she-bear attacked and seriously injured a 36-year-old hiker on a marked trail; the animal was never found and the municipality issued a stay-out-of-the-forest appeal. Slovakia has had repeated bear maulings in this massif in recent years. Camping discipline matters more than usual: cook and eat away from the tent, never store food or scented items in the tent, use the hut's bins or hang food, make noise on wooded ascents (the Nezbudská Lúčka -> Chata pod Suchým forest section at dusk is exactly the high-risk profile - low light, dense cover, forest/meadow edge). Bear spray is legal to carry in Slovakia and worth having. This is a further strong argument against arriving at camp after dark. TICKS / TBE: the Žilina region is one of Slovakia's two highest-incidence areas for tick-borne encephalitis (Žilina ~36.6% and Banská Bystrica ~47.4% of all national cases, 2019-2023), and Slovakia recently recorded its highest TBE case count in 60 years. Peak activity May-September, i.e. exactly the trip window. Countermeasures: TBE vaccination (the only effective protection, needs to be started weeks ahead), permethrin-treated or long trousers, DEET/icaridin repellent, full body check every evening, avoid pitching in tall grass. Also note unpasteurised sheep/goat cheese sold at Slovak salaše is a documented alimentary TBE route. Other: no venomous snake risk worth planning for beyond the common European adder in rocky, sunny spots.
- **Weather / storms:** Classic Central-European continental convective summer. July-August daytime maxima on the ridge 15-22 C, valley 25-33 C in a heat spell; nights at 1,400 m commonly 6-12 C, so a comfort-limit ~5 C bag is right. The dominant hazard is the afternoon thunderstorm: convection builds from late morning, cells fire typically between 13:00 and 18:00, and the entire day-1 route (Suchý - Malý Kriváň - Veľký Kriváň - Chleb) is an unsheltered grassy crest above 1,500 m with chained rock steps that become genuinely dangerous when wet or in lightning. OPERATING RULE: be off the exposed crest and down at Chata pod Chlebom by about 15:00 - which for a 13-15 km / +1,400 m day means leaving Chata pod Suchým by 07:00-07:30 at the latest (the 06:00 park opening is convenient here rather than restrictive). Secondary risks: fast-moving fog on the crest (Steny/Poludňový grúň section navigationally awkward in cloud), strong NW wind funnelling over Snilovské sedlo, and in 2026 an unusually hot/dry pattern that raises both fire risk and dehydration risk. Bail-out to the valley is possible from Snilovské sedlo (cable car or Vrátna path) and from Medziholie. Check meteo: shmu.sk (SHMÚ warnings), windy.com, and the hut webcams (Chata pod Suchým and Chata pod Chlebom both run cameras).
- **Bail-out plan:** Dense and well-distributed - one of the strengths of this range. From the day-1 crest: Snilovské sedlo is the key escape point - cable car down to Vrátna (summer service ~08:00-16:00/17:00) or a 1 h 15 walk down the same line; Chata na Grúni (1,100 m, manned, also a legal camping anchor) sits just below on the Vrátna side. From the western half: retreat to Chata pod Suchým then Strečno/Varín (~2 h) or Nezbudská Lúčka (~2 h). From the eastern half: Medziholie -> Štefanová (~1 h) with buses to Terchová/Varín, or north to Šútovo via the Šútovská dolina (long, ~3 h). Manned huts with a roof and a phone: Chata pod Suchým, Chata pod Chlebom (08:00-22:00), Chata na Grúni, plus the whole Vrátna/Štefanová accommodation cluster. GSM: coverage on the open crest and summits is generally good (Žilina basin transmitters), patchy in the Šútovská and Vrátna gullies and on the wooded western approach - if you need to call, climb back to the crest. Emergency numbers 112 / 18 300 (HZS). Road access with public transport at both ends (Nezbudská Lúčka railway halt, Terchová/Štefanová buses) means an abandoned trip does not strand you.
- **Total fees ⚠️:** There is no entry ticket to NP Malá Fatra and no fee for camping at the four designated anchors. Realistic weekend cash outlay per person, ESTIMATED: Slovak 10-day e-vignette 10.80 EUR per car (optional - avoidable via I/11 + I/18); parking in Nezbudská Lúčka 0 EUR (free lot by the ferry) or ~5 EUR/day at the marked lot, so 0-15 EUR per car for the weekend; mountain-rescue insurance ~2-6 EUR per person for the weekend; hut spending (dinner + beer at Chata pod Chlebom, tea at Chata pod Suchým) 15-30 EUR per person, effectively the price of goodwill at the camping anchors; return bus/train Štefanová/Terchová -> Varín -> Nezbudská Lúčka ~3-5 EUR per person. Optional cable car Vrátna -> Snilovské sedlo (one-way, if used as a bail-out or a shortcut) roughly 15-20 EUR [uncertain]. TOTAL: roughly 35-70 EUR per person for the weekend excluding fuel, plus 11-26 EUR per car of vignette+parking. This is the cheapest of the three Group-C Slovak options because there is no entry ticket and no campsite fee.

**Season, Crowds & Logistics**

- **Season fit:** Excellent for July-August - this is the range's prime window. The 1 March - 15 June seasonal closures (including Vendovka - Malý Kriváň, which is on the day-1 line, and Medziholie - Veľký Rozsutec, the Sunday add-on) have expired, so the full ridge including Veľký Rozsutec is open. Snow is gone from the crest by late June; the grassy ridge is dry and fast, days are long (sunset ~20:40 end of July), and both huts are in full operation. Downsides of the same window: peak thunderstorm frequency, peak tick activity, peak crowds, and in 2026 an added drought/fire-risk layer that makes the already-thin water situation worse. Shoulder seasons: September is arguably better (fewer people, fewer storms, still snow-free) but daylight shrinks fast against the 21:00/06:00 curfew and turns to the 07:00-18:00 winter window on 1 November; May-early June is off the table for the ridge day because of the seasonal closures. Winter here is a genuine alpine undertaking (crampons and ice axe on the ridge) and the 07:00-18:00 window makes a two-camp weekend impractical.
- **Crowd level:** High on the eastern half, low on the western half. Vrátna dolina / Štefanová / Veľký Rozsutec is one of the single busiest hiking destinations in Slovakia - car parks full by mid-morning, queues at the chained sections, the access road closed when the valley fills. The Snilovské sedlo - Chleb - Veľký Kriváň section inherits that traffic from the cable car. By contrast the WESTERN approach (Nezbudská Lúčka - Starhrad - Chata pod Suchým - Suchý) is comparatively quiet even in August, which is a strong argument for the proposed west-to-east direction. Friday-evening advantage: you are walking up when the day crowd is walking down, the Nezbudská Lúčka car park is empty, and you claim tent ground at Chata pod Suchým before the Saturday wave. You then cross the busy middle section on Saturday and reach Chata pod Chlebom in the late afternoon - the one point where you may find competition for the small pitching area, because it is a popular Saturday-night target. Sunday morning you descend against the flow. Tent space at both anchors is limited and unbookable, so early arrival is the whole game.
- **Navigation / maps:** Marking is the Slovak KST paint-blaze system - same visual grammar as Poland (coloured stripe between two white bars), but colour denotes route rank/continuity, not difficulty: the RED blaze is the main ridge trail (hrebeňovka) and is the spine of the whole plan. Signposts give walking TIMES, not distances - Polish hikers routinely mis-plan on this. Maps and apps: mapy.cz (best all-round for Slovakia, offline packs, hut/spring POIs), hiking.sk (the definitive Slovak trail database with stage times, hut pages and live closure news), turistickamapa.sk / freemap.sk (official-ish KST layer), OsmAnd or Locus Map with the OpenAndroMaps Slovakia dataset, plus turisticky.sk and the Správa NP Malá Fatra site for the visitor regulations and closure notices. Download offline maps before crossing - the wooded western approach has patchy data coverage. Carry the paper VKÚ/CBS sheet 'Malá Fatra - Vrátna' 1:25,000 as a backup. Two navigational traps: the Steny / Poludňový grúň section is genuinely confusing in cloud, and the descent from Chleb to Chata pod Chlebom leaves the red ridge trail - watch for the branch or you will overshoot to Snilovské sedlo.

> ⚠️ _Fields above contain self-flagged agent estimates/caveats (verify before departure): Distance / time from Ustroń, Parking, Public transport, Camping rules, 2026 status (live blockers), Hourly / seasonal restrictions, Fine risk, Friday-evening feasibility, Camp 2, Actual spacing vs 15-25 km target, Fire / cooking, Total fees_

---

### 9. Veľká Fatra (SK)

**Access & Travel**

- **Distance / time from Ustroń ⚠️:** ESTIMATE, not verified in a live routing engine [uncertain]. All approaches go Ustroń -> Cieszyn/Zwardoń -> Čadca -> Žilina, then east along the Váh (I/18 through Strečno, joining D1 near Vrútky/Ružomberok). Trailhead-by-trailhead: (a) SMREKOVICA (1,350 m, the recommended Friday base) - Ustroń -> Žilina -> Ružomberok -> turn off at Podsuchá 10 km south of Ružomberok on the Banská Bystrica road, then 8 km of surfaced mountain road to the top: roughly 165-180 km, about 2 h 40 - 3 h 00; (b) VYŠNÁ REVÚCA (Liptovské Revúce, ~750 m, on road I/59 Ružomberok - Donovaly) - roughly 175-185 km, about 2 h 45 - 3 h 05; (c) KRÁĽOVA STUDŇA (1,278 m) - only via a 14 km rough forest road up the Zalámaná valley from Harmanec, itself ~215 km / 3 h 15 from Ustroń, so ~3 h 45 - 4 h door-to-camp: not viable for a Friday evening. Veľká Fatra is the MIDDLE of the three Group-C Slovak options - roughly 40-50 min further than Malá Fatra, 20-30 min closer than the Low Tatras. Main delay risk is the single-carriageway I/18 Žilina - Strečno - Vrútky bottleneck on a Friday afternoon; the Višňové tunnel bypass is not usable, so budget a 20-30 min buffer.
- **PL/SK/CZ border ⚠️:** Poland -> Slovakia, Schengen internal border: no routine checks, ID card sufficient. Currency PLN -> EUR; huts on this ridge (Chata pod Borišovom, Horský hotel Kráľova studňa, Horská chata Smrekovica) are cash-friendly and card acceptance is unreliable at altitude - carry EUR notes. The forest-road permit for Kráľova studňa is bought by SMS from a Slovak short number, which works from a Polish roaming SIM but is worth testing beforehand [uncertain]. Trail blazes use the same KST/KČT paint system as Poland (colour = route rank, red = main ridge), but Slovak signposts state TIMES rather than distances. Slovak emergency numbers: 112, and 18 300 for Horská záchranná služba. Note also that Veľká Fatra straddles the Žilina and Banská Bystrica regions and the ridge is the boundary - not a legal issue, but relevant when reading regional fire-risk or bear notices.
- **Parking ⚠️:** SMREKOVICA: surfaced road all the way up (turn-off at Podsuchá, 10 km from Ružomberok, then 8 km of mountain road); parking exists at Horská chata Smrekovica / the former military recreation complex. One source quotes 2.50 EUR per hour, which is almost certainly a short-stay tariff and not what a two-night stay would cost - the real weekend price was not confirmed [uncertain]; assume 5-15 EUR for the weekend and confirm by phone with the chata. VYŠNÁ REVÚCA: only small paid parking - at the COOP Jednota shop and a strip beside the road above Chata Horec; capacity is a handful of cars and there is no large trailhead lot, so an evening arrival is fine but a Saturday morning one is not [uncertain]. KRÁĽOVA STUDŇA: the 14 km Zalámaná-valley forest road is closed to general traffic; entry requires a 6 EUR permit from Mestské lesy Banská Bystrica bought by SMS with the number plate to 2200, valid for one return trip, and parking is allowed only in the hotel's own marked lot. Higher ground clearance is recommended - the surface degrades from asphalt to rough gravel with potholes. Overnight standing at all three is tolerated rather than formally guaranteed [uncertain]. Overall, parking pressure in Veľká Fatra is a fraction of Malá Fatra's - the range simply does not draw Vrátna-scale crowds.
- **Public transport ⚠️:** Workable but thin - noticeably worse than Malá Fatra. Rail: Ružomberok is on the main Žilina - Košice line (fast, frequent) and is the gateway for the Liptov side; Turčianske Teplice/Martin serve the Turiec side. Bus: SAD Liptovský Mikuláš/Žilina run Ružomberok - Podsuchá - Liptovské Revúce (stop 'Liptovské Revúce, Vyšná Revúca, Jednota') - Donovaly - Banská Bystrica, so both Vyšná Revúca and the Podsuchá turn-off are reachable; Smrekovica itself (8 km and 600 m above Podsuchá) has no scheduled service [uncertain]. On weekends and holidays from 1 May to 1 November a dedicated tourist bus ('Turbus') runs Banská Bystrica - Kráľova studňa, which is the one genuinely useful public link onto the ridge. A linear A->B is therefore possible in the Smrekovica/Ružomberok -> Vyšná Revúca or -> Dolný Harmanec direction with a bus/train return, but connections are sparse on Sunday afternoons - which is exactly why the loop variant below (car left at Smrekovica) is the recommended shape. Check cp.sk / imhd.sk for the departure weekend.
- **Vignettes:** Slovak e-vignette required if you use the D1 motorway (the Ružomberok bypass and the Vrútky - Ivachnová sections) and D3 (Skalité - Žilina). 2026 tariffs for a car up to 3.5 t: 1-day 8.10 EUR, 10-day 10.80 EUR, 30-day 17.10 EUR, 365-day 90 EUR; buy only at eznamka.sk or the official app (also at Pošta 1 regional post offices without markup since March 2026). The 10-day sticker at 10.80 EUR is the sensible weekend buy. A vignette-free route exists (I/11 via Čadca, then I/18 along the Váh, then I/59 or the Podsuchá road) and costs roughly 20-30 min extra, but the D1 sections around Ružomberok are hard to avoid cleanly on the way to Podsuchá - just buy the vignette. Vignettes are number-plate-linked and camera-enforced; no Czech vignette is needed on this route. Additional road cost unique to this range: the 6 EUR forest-road permit if you drive to Kráľova studňa.

**Camping Legality**

- **Protection status ⚠️:** National park. NP Veľká Fatra (declared as a national park in 2002, upgraded from a protected landscape area declared 1973), 40,371 ha, 79% forested, administered by Správa Národného parku Veľká Fatra based in Martin (info@npvf.sk, +421 43 428 45 03). Governed by Act 543/2002 Z.z. Baseline protection degree in the park is 3 (which already carries the statutory ban on camping, bivouacking and fires), with degree 4-5 in the national nature reserves - and this range is dense with them: NNR Harmanecká tisina, NNR Jánošíkova kolkáreň, NNR Lubochnianska dolina, NNR Kornietová, NNR Padva, NNR Čierny kameň and others sit directly on or beside the main ridge. The park has been re-zoned into zones A-D under the 2022-2025 state-land reform, with zone A the strictest [uncertain - the zone map was not readable in the sources found]. Natura 2000 SCI/SPA overlays the whole massif; it is one of the most important brown-bear and lynx areas in Slovakia. Practical reading: default = everything forbidden, and only the six named bivouac anchors in the visitor regulations make a tent legal.
- **Camping rules:** Bivouacking and camping are permitted ONLY 'vo vzdialenosti do 50 metrov od jestvujúcich útulní, chát a salašov' (within 50 m of existing shelters, huts and shepherd's huts) and ONLY at six named localities: (1) at the SNP memorial by Kráľova studňa; (2) Pod Suchým vrchom; (3) v sedle Kýšky; (4) Smrekovica - within 50 m of the salaš at Močidlo; (5) Šiprúň - within 50 m of the salaš at Predné; (6) the Rakytový Grúň locality between Vyšná Lipová and Kľak. The permitted purpose is explicitly 'jednorázové, prípadne núdzové prenocovanie' - a ONE-OFF or emergency overnight stay, which is stricter wording than Malá Fatra's and means one night per site, not a two-night base camp. Note the 50 m radius, half of Malá Fatra's 100 m. CRITICAL CORRECTION to the workspace scope: Chata pod Borišovom is NOT on the list, and camping/bivouacking at the hut is NOT permitted - hikers on hiking.sk confirm this and point to sedlo Kýšky, a short walk further along the ridge, as the nearest legal spot (the hut keeper is reportedly relaxed about it in practice, but that is informal tolerance, not permission). Off-trail walking is allowed only in the specific areas listed in Appendix 2 of the visitor regulations; dogs on a leash only. There is no permit, booking or fee for the tent itself at the six anchors. Outside them, the Forest Act 326/2005 §31 independently bans camping on forest land.
- **2026 status (live blockers) ⚠️:** Checked 2026-07-28. (1) TRAILS: the Správa NP Veľká Fatra site shows ZERO trail closures at the time of checking - no seasonal spring closure regime equivalent to Malá Fatra's 1 March - 15 June programme was found for this park [uncertain]. Separately, an EU-funded maintenance/clearing programme covering nearly 60 km of paths is scheduled to run until 15 September 2026, so expect working parties and possibly short local diversions. (2) BEARS: on 15 July 2026 the park's intervention team captured a problem bear that had repeatedly attacked livestock, fitted it with a GPS collar and applied aversive therapy before release - i.e. there is an actively managed problem animal in the area right now. Veľká Fatra also has a record of attacks on people, including a mountain rescuer mauled on the ridge. The park explicitly advises planning activity in daylight and avoiding forest movement at dawn, dusk and night. (3) FIRE / DROUGHT: Slovakia is under a nationwide elevated-forest-fire-risk warning in late July 2026 (prolonged heat, drought, local strong wind), with most of the country repeatedly in the top danger category and some forest districts declaring 'čas zvýšeného nebezpečenstva vzniku požiaru' with temporary entry bans. No Veľká Fatra-specific entry ban was found [uncertain]. (4) The drought compounds this range's known weak point: ridge springs, already scarce, are reported dry in dry spells - see water_sources. RE-CHECK in the departure week: npvelkafatra.sk (news + closures), the SHMÚ fire/heat warnings, and phone Chata pod Borišovom / Horská chata Smrekovica about spring flow.
- **Hourly / seasonal restrictions ⚠️:** MAJOR ADVANTAGE OVER MALÁ FATRA: the NP Veľká Fatra visitor regulations contain NO night-time or hourly restriction on walking - there is no 06:00-21:00 curfew, so a late Friday arrival and a walk-in after sunset are legal (bears, not the law, are the reason to avoid it). Verified against the published regulations, but the absence of a rule is harder to prove than its presence, so treat as high-confidence rather than certain [uncertain]. Seasonal restrictions that DO exist: rock-climbing sites are open only in defined windows (e.g. Krpcovo 15 July - 1 December), winter activity is constrained by avalanche risk in the 1 November - 31 March period, off-trail walking is restricted to the areas listed in Appendix 2, and cycling to the routes in the same appendix. No 1 March - 15 June ridge closure of the Malá Fatra type was found. For a late-July trip nothing on the proposed route is seasonally closed. The one genuinely seasonal item that matters logistically is positive: the Banská Bystrica - Kráľova studňa Turbus runs only 1 May - 1 November, weekends and holidays.
- **Fine risk ⚠️:** Same Slovak framework as everywhere else in the country: Act 543/2002 Z.z. on nature and landscape protection plus Forest Act 326/2005. Pitching a tent outside the six designated anchors, lighting a fire, or leaving marked trails outside the permitted zones is a priestupok. On-the-spot (bloková) fines up to about 300 EUR; in full administrative proceedings the statutory band for natural persons runs from roughly 331.93 EUR to 9,958.17 EUR by severity, with a single illegal tent realistically landing at the low end or as a warning [uncertain]. Enforcement in practice comes from the park's stráž prírody rangers, the forest service (this park has a large state-forest footprint and active forestry) and the police. Specific risk pattern for this range: the tempting spot is right at Chata pod Borišovom, which is NOT legal - and it is a manned hut on a busy ridge, so a tent there is visible. Sedlo Kýšky, 20-40 min away, is legal and free. Do not improvise a camp at sedlo Ploskej or on Ploská either, however good the ground looks - neither is on the list.

**Route & Camps**

- **Suggested route:** RECOMMENDED SHAPE - a car-friendly ridge out-and-back loop from Smrekovica, which solves both the Friday-evening problem and the car-shuttle problem that the linear Vyšná Revúca -> Smrekovica plan in the workspace scope creates. DAY 0 (Friday evening): drive to Smrekovica (1,350 m, surfaced road) and pitch within 50 m of the salaš at Močidlo - effectively a zero-walk camp, legal, and reachable however late you leave Ustroń. DAY 1 (Saturday): Smrekovica -> Tanečnica -> Rakytovské sedlá -> Rakytov (1,567 m) -> sedlo pod Čiernym kameňom -> Ploská (1,532 m) -> sedlo Ploskej -> Borišov (1,510 m) -> sedlo Kýšky / Chyžky (~1,470 m). Approx. 16-18 km, +900-1,100 m / -600-700 m, 6 h - 7 h 30 with packs, on the red main ridge trail - open grassy crest with big views, no technical difficulty, but relentless up-and-down ('like a seesaw'). Optional 20-30 min detour down to Chata pod Borišovom (1,300 m) for a meal, water and a beer, then back up to camp legally at Kýšky. DAY 2 (Sunday): reverse the ridge Kýšky -> Borišov -> sedlo Ploskej -> Ploská -> Čierny kameň -> Rakytov -> Smrekovica, approx. 16-18 km, 6-7 h, back at the car. ALTERNATIVE LINEAR VARIANT (the workspace scope's shape, needs two cars or a bus return): Day 1 Vyšná Revúca -> rázcestie pod Krížnou -> Krížna (1,574 m) -> Ostredok (1,592 m, range high point) -> Koniarky -> Chyžky/Kýšky = 21 km, +1,400 m / -845 m, ~8 h, difficulty 3.5/5 - camp at sedlo Kýšky (NOT at Chata pod Borišovom); Day 2 Kýšky -> sedlo Ploskej -> Ploská -> Rakytov -> Smrekovica, ~15-17 km, 6-7 h, finishing at a car you must have left there or a bus you must catch to Podsuchá/Ružomberok. A THIRD variant starts Friday at Kráľova studňa (6 EUR forest-road permit, legal camp at the SNP memorial) and runs north over Krížna and Ostredok, but the 3 h 45 - 4 h drive kills it for a Friday.
- **Friday-evening feasibility:** GOOD - materially better than Malá Fatra, because there is no legal curfew AND there is a legal camp you can drive to. Arithmetic for late July (sunset ~20:35-20:45, civil twilight to ~21:15): SMREKOVICA VARIANT - leave Ustroń at 17:30-18:00 after work, arrive at Smrekovica 20:20-21:00, pitch by the salaš at Močidlo in the last light or by headtorch. Legal, no walk-in, no bear exposure on a dusk forest trail, and you wake up already at 1,350 m on the ridge. This is the single strongest Friday-evening proposition among the three Slovak Group-C candidates. Risk: the last 8 km of mountain road in the dark, and the unverified parking tariff. VYŠNÁ REVÚCA VARIANT - arrive 20:30-21:00, then a 3 h ascent (+842 m) to the ridge and a further 20-40 min to sedlo Kýšky: that is a 23:00-00:30 arrival, entirely in the dark, through bear habitat at exactly the hours the park warns against. Legal, but a bad idea - only attempt it with a departure from Ustroń by 15:30-16:00. KRÁĽOVA STUDŇA VARIANT - 3 h 45 - 4 h including 14 km of rough forest road that you do not want to drive at night, plus the SMS permit; feasible only with an early-afternoon departure. VERDICT: choose Smrekovica for Friday and design the weekend around it; the ~1.5 h penalty in drive time versus Malá Fatra is more than repaid by not having to beat a 21:00 legal deadline on foot.
- **Camp 1 ⚠️:** SMREKOVICA - within 50 m of the salaš at Močidlo (locality Smrekovica, ~1,350 m, on the Liptov branch of the main ridge above Podsuchá/Ružomberok). Legal basis: explicitly named in the NP Veľká Fatra visitor regulations as one of six places where bivouacking and camping are permitted, within a 50 m radius of the salaš. Character: subalpine meadow/spruce edge at the northern end of the ridge, wind-exposed but with tree shelter available; the Horská chata Smrekovica (62 beds, restaurant) and the former military recreation complex with a restaurant are within about a kilometre, giving you a roof, a phone, a toilet and a hot meal as backup. Water: a spring by the Smrekovica hotel is one of the named ridge sources. Access: surfaced road right to the top, which is what makes it the Friday camp. Caveat: the exact position of the salaš at Močidlo relative to the hotel and the car park is worth pinning on mapy.cz before you go, because the 50 m radius is measured from the salaš, not from the hotel [uncertain].
- **Camp 2:** SEDLO KÝŠKY (also written Chyžky, ~1,470 m), the saddle on the main ridge just south of Borišov, roughly 20-40 min above and beyond Chata pod Borišovom. Legal basis: named in the visitor regulations as 'v sedle Kýšky', camping/bivouac permitted within 50 m of the existing shelter there. This is the correct legal substitute for the Chata pod Borišovom camp assumed in the workspace scope - camping at the hut itself is NOT allowed. Character: a grassy ridge saddle, exposed to wind and to afternoon storms, with a wide outlook over both the Turiec and Liptov sides. Practical routine: drop the 200 m down to Chata pod Borišovom (1,300 m, open year-round, 20 beds, ~25 EUR/night if you want a bunk instead, meals and drinks, a washbasin with cold water and springs in the vicinity) to eat, refill water and use the toilet, then climb back to Kýšky to sleep legally. BACKUP legal anchor 30-60 min further south on the same ridge: 'Pod Suchým vrchom' - but note its spring is reported to run dry in summer, so carry water up from Borišov either way. Do not pitch on Ploská or in sedlo Ploskej - neither is a designated site.
- **Actual spacing vs 15-25 km target ⚠️:** Recommended (Smrekovica loop): camp 1 Smrekovica -> camp 2 sedlo Kýšky is approximately 16-18 km along the red ridge trail with roughly +900-1,100 m of cumulative ascent and 6 h - 7 h 30 of walking - ESTIMATED from stage times rather than measured, since no source publishes this exact pair [uncertain]. This SITS INSIDE the 15-25 km criterion, near the middle of the band, and is the cleanest fit of any option researched so far. Cross-checks used: the full Smrekovica -> Krížna ridge is quoted at ~9 h total, with Kýšky roughly two-thirds along; kakac.sk gives Vyšná Revúca -> Krížna -> Ostredok -> Koniarky -> Chyžky -> Chata pod Borišovom as 21 km / +1,400 m / -845 m / 8 h, which places Kýšky at ~19-20 km from Vyšná Revúca; natreku.cz's 3-day traverse gives Vlkolínské lúky -> Smrekovica -> Rakytov -> Chata pod Borišovom as ~24 km, of which the Ružomberok-side approach is ~8 km, again leaving ~16 km for the Smrekovica -> Borišov ridge. LINEAR VARIANT spacing (workspace scope shape): Vyšná Revúca -> Kýšky day 1 is ~19-20 km and Kýšky -> Smrekovica day 2 is ~15-17 km, i.e. also inside the criterion, at the cost of a car shuttle. Either way, Veľká Fatra is one of the very few ranges on the candidate list where the 15-25 km target falls out of the LEGAL anchor spacing naturally, rather than having to be forced.
- **Water sources:** THE critical constraint of this range and the reason to over-carry. The ridge is built of limestone and dolomite (karst), so surface water drains away and the crest itself is notoriously dry in high summer; the honest planning assumption is 3 l carried per person for the ridge day, refilled only at named points. NAMED SOURCES on the proposed line, north to south: spring by the Smrekovica hotel (camp 1, reliable); springs in the Rakytovské sedlo / sedlo pod Čiernym kameňom area (several, the northern flank of Ploská is described as spring-rich); a marked drinking-water point near Ploská and in sedlo Ploskej; Chata pod Borišovom - hut water plus springs around it, and in summer an outdoor cold-water basin (the most reliable refill on the ridge and the reason to make the 200 m detour); Kráľova studňa (the historic 'King's Well' at the southern end, the single most dependable source in the massif and the anchor the workspace scope correctly identifies). UNRELIABLE: the spring 'pod Suchým vrchom' is documented by hikers as DRY in summer - do not plan on it; several small ridge seeps flow so slowly that filling a bottle takes many minutes. Written accounts split on this range - long-distance traverse reports say 'at least one spring a day, no water worries', while ridge-day reports say sources are limited and much better in spring than in autumn. In the 2026 drought summer, believe the pessimistic version. Filter or boil anything not from a hut tap; sheep grazing (salaše) upstream is common here, so untreated meadow water is a real giardia/campylobacter risk.
- **Fire / cooking ⚠️:** No open fire in practice. The visitor regulations allow 'kladenie ohňa' only in the immediate surroundings of recreational buildings, within 50 m, and only at built fire-rings, plus at one extra named locality (Lúky pod Čerencom, cadastral area Rakša). At the six bivouac anchors that means: only if the salaš/hut has an official fire-ring and its keeper agrees. Anywhere else a fire is a misdemeanour under Act 543/2002, and in late July 2026 the nationwide elevated fire-risk warning makes it indefensible anyway. Gas or liquid-fuel stoves: not prohibited by the regulations and standard practice at the designated spots; the safe reading is that a stove is fine at the six anchors, on bare ground away from dry grass, and is not a licence to cook anywhere on the ridge [uncertain]. Given the drought, cook early, never leave a stove unattended, and consider carrying no-cook food for one of the two dinners. Pack out all rubbish - the salaše are working sheep operations, not waste points.

**Safety & Costs**

- **Rescue / insurance:** BUY A POLICY. Under Slovak law anyone in the mountains is liable for the costs of Horská záchranná služba rescue and search operations (only minors and persons without legal capacity are exempt) - the Polish GOPR/TOPR 'free rescue' rule does not travel. Typical bills: an average ground intervention ~400 EUR; a search or helicopter operation 2,000-4,000 EUR, with helicopter flight time alone at 60 EUR+ per minute (30-45 min = 1,800-2,700 EUR). Sensible minimum cover for HZS costs 20,000 EUR; typical price 1-3 EUR per person per day for a Slovak/Polish 'poistenie do hôr' or an EU travel policy with a mountain rider. EHIC/EKUZ pays for hospital treatment but NOT the rescue and transport bill. Check the policy names 'turistika' explicitly and does not exclude unorganised camping. Veľká Fatra-specific note: HZS coverage here is real but response times are longer than in the Tatras - the massif is big, deeply forested and the ridge is far from roads, so self-sufficiency (first-aid kit, spare layers, a means of calling out) matters more than in Malá Fatra. HZS 18 300 or 112; install the HZS app for coordinate transmission.
- **Bears / ticks:** BEARS - high and currently ACTIVE. Veľká Fatra is one of Slovakia's core brown-bear areas (the park cites 1,200+ animal species including bear and lynx), a bear has attacked and injured a mountain rescuer on this massif, and a hiker with a dog met a she-bear with four cubs on a ridge approach. Most current: on 15 July 2026 the park's intervention team trapped a problem bear that had repeatedly attacked livestock, collared it with GPS and applied aversive therapy. The park's own advice is to plan activity in daylight, avoid forest movement at dawn/dusk/night, move in a group, make noise (talk, clap, whistle), and if you see a bear at distance, back away calmly. For a camping trip this translates into hard rules: cook and eat well away from the tent, never keep food or scented items inside it, hang or hut-store food, and - decisive for this plan - do NOT do a 3 h forest ascent from Vyšná Revúca after dark, which is precisely why the Smrekovica drive-in Friday camp is recommended. Bear spray is legal in Slovakia. Extra local factor: the six legal bivouac anchors are at salaše (working sheep folds) - livestock attracts bears and salaše keep guard dogs; approach a salaš in daylight and speak to whoever is there. TICKS / TBE: the ridge straddles the Žilina and Banská Bystrica regions, which together account for ~84% of Slovakia's tick-borne encephalitis cases (Banská Bystrica ~47.4%, Žilina ~36.6%, 2019-2023), and Slovakia recently logged its highest TBE count in 60 years - this is statistically the WORST TBE region on the whole candidate list. Peak activity May-September. Countermeasures: TBE vaccination (start weeks ahead), permethrin-treated clothing, DEET/icaridin, nightly full body checks, avoid pitching in tall grass. Do NOT buy unpasteurised sheep cheese or žinčica from the salaše on this ridge - alimentary TBE transmission from raw sheep/goat dairy is documented in Slovakia and the salaše are literally your campsites here.
- **Weather / storms:** Continental convective summer, same pattern as the neighbouring ranges but with more exposure time because the ridge is long and treeless. July-August: ridge daytime maxima 14-21 C, valley 25-33 C in a heat spell; nights at 1,350-1,500 m typically 5-12 C (a comfort-limit ~5 C bag is right, and Kýšky is a wind-exposed saddle - take a solid pitch and good pegs). The dominant hazard is the afternoon thunderstorm: convection builds from late morning, cells fire typically 13:00-18:00, and the whole Rakytov - Ploská - Borišov crest is open grassland above 1,450 m with no shelter and no trees - a textbook lightning-exposure line. OPERATING RULE: be at camp 2 (sedlo Kýšky) or down at Chata pod Borišovom by about 15:00, which for a 16-18 km / +1,000 m day means leaving Smrekovica by 07:00-07:30. There is no legal curfew here, so the early start is purely a weather decision. Escape from the crest is genuinely slow - the valleys on both sides are long and forested - so treat the forecast as a go/no-go rather than a nuisance. Secondary: fog on the broad summit plateau of Ploská is navigationally awkward (few features, several trail junctions); strong westerly wind funnels across the Rakytovské sedlá. 2026 adds an unusually hot, dry pattern raising fire and dehydration risk. Check shmu.sk warnings and windy.com; the Chata pod Borišovom and Smrekovica sites carry local conditions.
- **Bail-out plan:** Thinner than Malá Fatra - this is the trade-off for the emptiness. From the middle of the ridge the key refuge is CHATA POD BORIŠOVOM (1,300 m, manned year-round, 20 beds, meals, ~25 EUR/night) about 20-30 min below sedlo Kýšky - a roof, a phone and a paid bed if the weather collapses or someone is hurt. From there or from sedlo Ploskej, the yellow trail drops east to Vyšná Revúca in ~2 h (bus stop 'Liptovské Revúce, Vyšná Revúca, Jednota', buses to Ružomberok), or west into the Turiec valleys (Belianska/Gaderská dolina) - long, 3 h+, and with poor onward transport. From the northern third, retreat to Smrekovica (Horská chata, restaurant, 62 beds, surfaced road, your car) - almost always the fastest exit on the recommended loop. From the southern end, Kráľova studňa has a mountain hotel and the weekend Turbus to Banská Bystrica. GSM: generally usable on the open crest and summits, unreliable in the Lubochnianska, Gaderská and Revúca valleys and in the forest belt below the ridge - if you need to call, stay high. Emergency 112 / HZS 18 300. Because the range is quiet, do not count on passing hikers to raise the alarm for you: carry a power bank and tell someone your plan.
- **Total fees ⚠️:** No entry ticket to NP Veľká Fatra and no fee for the tent at the six designated anchors. Realistic weekend cash outlay, ESTIMATED [uncertain]: Slovak 10-day e-vignette 10.80 EUR per car; parking at Smrekovica - one source quotes 2.50 EUR/hour, which cannot be the multi-day rate, so budget 5-15 EUR per car for the weekend and confirm with the chata; parking at Vyšná Revúca a few EUR/day if you use the linear variant; the Kráľova studňa forest-road permit 6 EUR per return trip if you use that variant; mountain-rescue insurance ~2-6 EUR per person for the weekend; hut spending at Chata pod Borišovom (dinner, drinks, water) 15-30 EUR per person, or ~25 EUR per person if you take a bunk instead of the tent; return bus Vyšná Revúca -> Ružomberok ~2-3 EUR per person if the linear variant is used. TOTAL: roughly 35-70 EUR per person for the weekend excluding fuel, plus ~16-26 EUR per car of vignette and parking. Slightly more than Malá Fatra because of the longer drive and the paid mountain road/parking, but still cheap - there is no park fee, no campsite fee and no cable car.

**Season, Crowds & Logistics**

- **Season fit:** Very good for July-August, with one caveat that is bigger here than anywhere else on the list: WATER. The positives - no seasonal trail closures found, no legal curfew, all huts and salaše in operation, the Kráľova studňa Turbus running (1 May - 1 November), long days, snow-free grassy ridge, and the flower-rich meadows of Krížna and Ploská at their best in early summer. The negatives - the karst ridge is at its driest exactly in July-August, and the 2026 drought sharpens that; peak thunderstorm season on a long treeless crest; peak tick and bear activity. Multiple accounts rate the spring-to-early-summer transition (June) as the sweet spot: springs still flowing, meadows in flower, and 'very few people even in mid-season'. September is the other good window - cooler, fewer storms, fewer ticks - but springs are at their annual low and daylight shortens. May and early June can still hold snow on the northern flanks of Ostredok and Ploská. Winter turns this into a serious multi-day ski-touring/snowshoe undertaking with avalanche exposure (the park flags avalanche constraints 1 November - 31 March).
- **Crowd level:** LOW - the quietest of the three Group-C Slovak candidates and one of the strongest arguments for this range. Veľká Fatra is repeatedly described as offering 'beautiful views and very few people even in the middle of the season'; it is the big Slovak range that visitors skip on their way to the Tatras. Hotspots that do draw people are the day-trip magnets rather than the ridge: Kráľova studňa (road access + weekend Turbus), the Gaderská and Blatnická valleys, Vlkolínec/Sidorovo above Ružomberok, and Chata pod Borišovom itself, which is a popular Saturday-night target and can be full - book if you want a bunk. The ridge between Rakytov and Kýšky sees a fraction of Malá Fatra's traffic. Friday-evening advantage: the Smrekovica camp is a drive-in, so there is no race for ground; you then have the ridge largely to yourself on Saturday morning. Tent space at sedlo Kýšky is unbookable and small but rarely contested. Parking capacity at both Smrekovica and Vyšná Revúca is small in absolute terms, which is the one place where a Saturday-morning start would bite - another reason to arrive Friday.
- **Navigation / maps:** Standard Slovak KST paint blazes; RED marks the main ridge trail (hrebeňovka) from Smrekovica through Rakytov, Ploská, Borišov, Kýšky, Ostredok and Krížna to Kráľova studňa, and it is the spine of every variant here. Approaches are yellow, green and blue. Signposts give TIMES, not distances - the usual trap for Polish hikers. Maps and apps: mapy.cz (best overall for Slovakia; carries the huts, salaše and many springs as POIs - essential on this dry ridge), hiking.sk (definitive stage times, hut pages, closure news, and the forum threads that establish where camping is actually tolerated), freemap.sk / turistickamapa.sk for the official KST layer, OsmAnd or Locus with OpenAndroMaps Slovakia. Also npvelkafatra.sk for the visitor regulations and its appendices (the bivouac list and the off-trail zones are in the annexes, not the main text). Paper backup: VKÚ/CBS sheet 'Veľká Fatra' 1:50,000. Navigational traps specific to this range: the broad, featureless summit plateau of Ploská in fog; the junction complex at sedlo Ploskej where the descent to Vyšná Revúca and the descent to Chata pod Borišovom diverge; and the fact that the hut sits 200 m BELOW the ridge, so the ridge trail does not pass it - watch for the marked branch or you will walk past your water and your dinner. Pre-mark the salaš at Močidlo and sedlo Kýšky as waypoints before departure, since the legality of your camp depends on being within 50 m of the right building.

> ⚠️ _Fields above contain self-flagged agent estimates/caveats (verify before departure): Distance / time from Ustroń, PL/SK/CZ border, Parking, Public transport, Protection status, 2026 status (live blockers), Hourly / seasonal restrictions, Fine risk, Camp 1, Actual spacing vs 15-25 km target, Fire / cooking, Total fees_

---

### 10. Nízke Tatry / NAPANT (SK)

**Access & Travel**

- **Distance / time from Ustroń ⚠️:** ESTIMATE, not verified in a live routing engine [uncertain]. This is the FURTHEST option on the whole candidate list. Recommended trailhead SEDLO ČERTOVICA (1,232 m, the highest road pass in Slovakia, road I/72 Kráľova Lehota - Podbrezová): Ustroń -> Zwardoń/Skalité -> D3 -> Žilina -> I/18 + D1 -> Ružomberok -> Liptovský Hrádok -> Kráľova Lehota -> I/72 up to the pass. Roughly 235-250 km, about 3 h 05 - 3 h 30 - consistent with the ~3 h 15 assumed in the workspace outline. Other trailheads: DONOVALY (western end, road I/59) ~200-215 km / ~2 h 50 - 3 h 05, the closest NAPANT access; JASNÁ / DEMÄNOVSKÁ DOLINA (Chopok north) ~230 km / ~3 h 05; MAGURKA (osada, road from Partizánska Ľupča) ~225 km / ~3 h 10 [uncertain]; TELGÁRT / ŠUMIAC (eastern end under Kráľova hoľa) ~300-315 km / ~4 h 00 - out of scope for a weekend. Round trip from Čertovica is 6.5-7 h of driving for a two-night weekend, which is the single biggest argument against this range and must be weighed against the fact that it is the only high Slovak range where a tent on the ridge is legal. Delay risks: the I/18 Žilina - Vrútky single carriageway on Friday afternoon, and the Ružomberok - Liptovský Mikuláš D1 gaps.
- **PL/SK/CZ border:** Poland -> Slovakia, Schengen internal border: no routine checks, ID card sufficient. PLN -> EUR; the útulne on this ridge take cash only (útulňa Andrejcová charges 5 EUR/person in cash), and card acceptance at Čertovica motorest/chalets is likely but not guaranteed - carry EUR. Trail blazes are the standard KST system, and the Low Tatras main ridge is also the 'Cesta hrdinov SNP' long-distance route (European E8), so you will see its red blazes and its own signage the whole way. Signposts give TIMES, not distances. Emergency numbers 112 and HZS 18 300; HZS has a permanently manned station AT sedlo Čertovica, which is unusual and useful. Note the ridge is the boundary between the Žilina (Liptov) and Banská Bystrica (Horehronie) regions - relevant when reading regional weather, fire-risk and bear notices.
- **Parking ⚠️:** SEDLO ČERTOVICA is the strongest parking situation of any option researched: four public car parks with a combined capacity of about 200 spaces right on the pass. Caveat from the official Čertovica site: the lots are run by THREE different operators, so opening conditions and tariffs are not uniform and no single 2026 price could be confirmed [uncertain] - budget 0-10 EUR/day per car and expect to pay at whichever lot you use. On holidays and peak weekends demand can still exceed the 200 spaces, and the operators recommend arriving by bus - a Friday-evening arrival sidesteps this entirely. Because the pass carries a state road and a permanently manned HZS station, multi-night standing is normal here rather than exceptional. DONOVALY: large resort parking, paid, plentiful [uncertain]. JASNÁ / Biela Púť / Srdiečko: large paid resort lots serving the cable cars. MAGURKA: small village parking at the end of the public road from Partizánska Ľupča, free or nominal [uncertain]. HIADEĽSKÉ SEDLO cannot be driven to - the forest roads up from Hiadeľ/Moštenica are closed to motor vehicles under the visitor regulations (motor traffic is restricted to public roads and marked lots; forest and field roads are off limits).
- **Public transport ⚠️:** The best of the three Group-C Slovak ranges for a linear A->B, but with sparse weekend frequency. Rail: Liptovský Hrádok and Liptovský Mikuláš on the Žilina - Košice main line serve the northern side; Brezno and Podbrezová on the Banská Bystrica - Margecany line serve the southern side; Telgárt Penzión (the famous spiral) is the eastern railhead under Kráľova hoľa. Bus: the I/72 line Brezno/Podbrezová - Čertovica - Kráľova Lehota - Liptovský Hrádok crosses the pass, making Čertovica one of the few Slovak high saddles with a scheduled bus stop [uncertain on 2026 frequency]; Donovaly is served by the frequent Banská Bystrica - Ružomberok buses; Jasná has resort shuttles from Liptovský Mikuláš. This means a genuine linear traverse - e.g. Čertovica -> Ďumbier -> Chopok -> Ďurková -> Donovaly, returning by bus Donovaly -> Ružomberok -> Liptovský Hrádok -> bus over Čertovica to the car - is possible on paper, but the last leg is the weak link and Sunday-evening departures are thin. Verify on cp.sk in the departure week before committing; otherwise use the out-and-back shape that returns to the car.
- **Vignettes:** Slovak e-vignette needed for D3 (Skalité - Žilina) and D1 (Žilina - Ružomberok - Liptovský Mikuláš), which is effectively unavoidable on this route unless you accept a much slower drive. 2026 tariffs for a car up to 3.5 t: 1-day 8.10 EUR, 10-day 10.80 EUR, 30-day 17.10 EUR, 365-day 90 EUR - buy at eznamka.sk or the official app (Pošta 1 branches also sell at face value since March 2026). Take the 10-day at 10.80 EUR. Road I/72 over Čertovica itself is toll-free, as are I/59 to Donovaly and the Demänovská valley road. No Czech vignette needed. Vignettes are plate-linked and camera-enforced - buy before crossing. Note that this is the longest motorway leg of the three Group-C options, so the vignette is least optional here.

**Camping Legality**

- **Protection status:** National park. Národný park Nízke Tatry (NAPANT), declared 1978, roughly 72,842 ha plus a large buffer zone - the LARGEST national park in Slovakia, administered by Správa NAPANT in Banská Bystrica (podatelna@napant.sk, +421 48 28 550 10). Governed by Act 543/2002 Z.z.; baseline protection degree 3 in the park (statutory ban on camping, bivouacking and fires), with degree 4-5 in the numerous national nature reserves - Ďumbier, Salatín, Skalka, Demänovská dolina, Ohnište and others sit on or beside the main ridge. Natura 2000 SCI/SPA over the whole massif. The park holds one of Europe's densest brown-bear populations plus lynx and wolf, and NAPANT operates its own brown-bear intervention team (Zásahový tím medveďa hnedého). KEY POINT for this project: despite that strict baseline, NAPANT's visitor regulations carve out more ridge-top tent anchors than any other high Slovak range - it is the ONLY 2,000 m Slovak massif where you can legally sleep in a tent on the main ridge itself.
- **Camping rules ⚠️:** Camping and bivouacking outside designated places are prohibited, but NAPANT's visitor regulations designate an unusually generous set of ridge anchors where a single overnight stay IN A TENT is explicitly permitted: (1) Predné sedlo, under Kráľova hoľa; (2) sedlo Andrejcová, at the útulňa; (3) sedlo Priehyba; (4) sedlo Ramža, at the útulňa; (5) sedlo Čertovica - specifically below the Horská záchranná služba chalet or behind the Motorest Čertovica building; (6) Ďurková (útulňa pod Chabencom) - listed as available in the event the shelter is full; (7) Hiadeľské sedlo, at the útulňa; and (8) the hamlet of Magurka. Sources differ slightly on whether the list is 7 or 8 entries (Magurka is a settlement rather than a saddle and is sometimes omitted) [uncertain]. The permitted purpose is a SINGLE overnight stay - these are transit anchors for a ridge traverse, not two-night base camps. Practical detail at the shelters: útulňa Andrejcová (1,410 m) has capacity 30 inside plus 12 TENTS outside at 5 EUR per person, with a spring, a fireplace and pitches among the dwarf pine; útulňa Ramža (1,260 m) holds about 20 with a drinking spring 300 m west; útulňa pod Chabencom / Ďurková (1,623 m) has a spring and pitching ground. Shelters are free-or-nominal, unstaffed and first-come - bring your own sleeping bag; quiet hours from 22:00. Dogs are allowed but must be on a leash AND muzzled - stricter than Malá Fatra or Veľká Fatra. Outside the designated anchors, Forest Act 326/2005 §31 independently bans camping on forest land.
- **2026 status (live blockers) ⚠️:** Checked 2026-07-28. (1) TRAILS: the NAPANT homepage lists NO active trail closures, fire bans or standing bear warnings as of late July 2026; recent news items are a photo competition, a forest-bathing event and minor infrastructure work (information panels at Krpáčovo, a barrier at Chmaroška, a new bench on Kráľova hoľa). The routine seasonal closures (1 October - 30 June) are all expired for a late-July trip. (2) HUT CLOSURE that affects planning: Chata Kosodrevina (1,510 m, top of the Srdiečko - Kosodrevina lift, a standard bail-out and refreshment point on the Chopok south side) is CLOSED from 1 April 2026 to 3 December 2026 - do not count on it. (3) BEARS: NAPANT has warned hikers to AVOID the trail from Trangoška to Chata M. R. Štefánika because a bear living there has completely lost its wariness through feeding by tourists and cottage owners and may be dangerous; whether that warning is still current in late July was not confirmed [uncertain]. Slovak media reported two separate bear-surprise incidents with tourists in the Tatras in June 2026, and a bear attacked two hunters in the Low Tatras. Bear mating season runs into July and raises activity and boldness. (4) FIRE / DROUGHT: Slovakia is under a nationwide elevated-forest-fire-risk warning in late July 2026 (heat, drought, local strong wind), with much of the country repeatedly in the top danger category and some forest districts declaring temporary entry bans; no NAPANT-specific ban was found [uncertain]. RE-CHECK in the departure week: napant.sk news, hiking.sk closure feed, SHMÚ warnings, and phone the útulne/Čertovica for spring flow and bear activity.
- **Hourly / seasonal restrictions ⚠️:** HARD BLOCKER - and STRICTER than Malá Fatra, which is the single most important correction to the workspace outline's assumption that NAPANT is the unconstrained option. NAPANT's visitor regulations permit movement on marked trails within the national park only in daytime 'hodinu po východe a hodinu pred západom slnka' - from ONE HOUR AFTER SUNRISE to ONE HOUR BEFORE SUNSET. In late July in central Slovakia (sunrise ~05:15, sunset ~20:35) that is roughly 06:15 to 19:35 - a shorter legal window than Malá Fatra's fixed 06:00-21:00. CRUCIAL EXCEPTION: the restriction does not apply to sections near built-up areas or state roads. Sedlo Čertovica is crossed by state road I/72 and has a motorest, chalets and a manned HZS station, so arriving there and camping at the designated spot in the evening is outside the restricted regime - which is exactly why Čertovica is the recommended Friday camp. TANAP relaxed its equivalent rule to plain 'daylight hours' in December 2023; whether NAPANT has since followed suit, and how actively the rule is enforced, could not be confirmed [uncertain] - plan to the published wording. SEASONAL CLOSURES (1 October - 30 June, i.e. all reopened for a July trip): Kotliská - Žiar (yellow 8447), Poľana - sedlo Sinná (yellow 8619), Demänovské sedlo - sedlo Javorie - Krupovské sedlo (8620); plus the red 0801 Chata M. R. Štefánika - Ďumbier winter pole variant, usable only with continuous snow cover of at least 15 cm. Nothing on the proposed weekend route is seasonally closed in late July.
- **Fine risk ⚠️:** Same Slovak framework: Act 543/2002 Z.z. plus Forest Act 326/2005. Pitching outside the designated anchors, lighting a fire, leaving marked trails, breaching the daylight-movement rule, or bringing a dog without a muzzle are all priestupky. On-the-spot (bloková) fines up to about 300 EUR; in administrative proceedings the statutory band for natural persons runs from roughly 331.93 EUR to 9,958.17 EUR by severity, with a single illegal tent realistically at the low end or a warning [uncertain]. Enforcement pressure here is HIGHER than in Veľká Fatra: NAPANT is Slovakia's largest park, it runs its own ranger service and a bear intervention team, and the Chopok/Ďumbier corridor is heavily patrolled because of the ski resorts and the traffic they generate. Two specific exposures on this plan: (a) camping anywhere on the Ďumbier - Chopok crest between Čertovica and Ďurková, where there is NO legal anchor for 28 km - this is the classic mistake; (b) walking the ridge outside the one-hour-after-sunrise / one-hour-before-sunset window, which is easy to do accidentally on a long stage. The upside is that the legal spots are unambiguous, free (except Andrejcová's 5 EUR) and marked on mapy.cz - there is no need to improvise.

**Route & Camps**

- **Suggested route:** The whole main ridge is ~95-100 km (Telgárt/Kráľova hoľa to Donovaly), normally walked in 4-5 days, so a weekend takes a slice. Published stage data: Telgárt - Andrejcová 16 km / +1,350 / 4 h 30; Andrejcová - Čertovica 29.5 km / +1,300 -1,600 / 7 h 30; Čertovica - Ďurková 28 km / +1,900 -1,350 / 7 h 30; Ďurková - Donovaly 27 km / +1,100 -1,700 / 7 h 00. VARIANT A - RECOMMENDED for one car and a Friday-evening start (eastern, Kráľovohoľská half, out-and-back): DAY 0 Friday - drive to sedlo Čertovica (1,232 m) and pitch at the designated spot below the HZS chalet / behind the motorest; zero walk-in, legal, outside the daylight-movement regime. DAY 1 Saturday - Čertovica east along the red ridge (Cesta hrdinov SNP) over Malá/Veľká Vápenica and Zadná hoľa to útulňa Ramža (1,260 m, ~3 h), then on over Priehybka to sedlo Priehyba, camp: approx. 17-19 km, +900-1,100 m, 6-7 h. DAY 2 Sunday - reverse Priehyba -> Ramža -> Čertovica, approx. 17-19 km, 6-7 h, back at the car by mid-afternoon. Terrain: rolling meadow-and-forest ridge at 1,200-1,600 m, technically easy, big views, very few people. VARIANT B - the classic high traverse (spectacular, needs a car shuttle or a bus return): Friday camp at Čertovica; Saturday Čertovica -> Chata gen. M. R. Štefánika -> Ďumbier (2,046 m, the range high point, chained rocky sections) -> Chopok (2,024 m) -> Kamenná chata -> Dereše -> Chabenec -> útulňa Ďurková (1,623 m), 28 km / +1,900 m / 7 h 30 - a very big day, marginally OVER the 25 km ceiling; Sunday Ďurková -> Latiborská hoľa -> Kozí chrbät -> Hiadeľské sedlo -> Donovaly, ~20 km / 6-7 h, then bus back to the car. VARIANT C - western, shortest drive: Friday drive to Donovaly (~2 h 50) and walk Donovaly - Kežka - Kozí chrbát - Hiadeľské sedlo (2 h 35) to camp at the útulňa there; Saturday Hiadeľské sedlo -> Veľká Chochuľa -> Ďurková (~7 h); Sunday return. Variant C is the only one whose Friday leg is on foot, and it collides with the daylight rule (see friday_evening_feasibility).
- **Friday-evening feasibility:** MIXED - saved by one specific fact. The drive is the longest on the list (3 h 05 - 3 h 30 to Čertovica) and the legal walking window closes ONE HOUR BEFORE SUNSET (~19:35 in late July), so any variant requiring a Friday walk-in inside the park is effectively dead for a post-work departure. VARIANT A/B (Čertovica): leave Ustroń 17:00-17:30, arrive at the pass 20:15-21:00, pitch at the designated spot behind the motorest / below the HZS chalet. This works because Čertovica sits on state road I/72 and is therefore exempt from the daylight-movement restriction, and because the walk-in is zero metres. Legal, safe (no dusk forest walking in prime bear habitat), and you wake at 1,232 m already on the ridge. Leaving by 16:30 gets you there in daylight, which is better for finding the spot and for the last winding kilometres of I/72. VARIANT C (Donovaly -> Hiadeľské sedlo): 2 h 50 drive plus 2 h 35 walk means leaving Ustroń by about 14:00 to be at the saddle before the 19:35 cut-off - a half-day off, not an after-work start. VERDICT: Nízke Tatry IS realistic for a Friday-evening / Sunday format, but only in the Čertovica drive-in shape; treat the ~6.5-7 h of round-trip driving as the real cost. If the group can leave Ustroń at 15:00-16:00, Variant B becomes the best trip on the whole candidate list; if departure is 18:00 or later, Variant A still works and Variant C does not.
- **Camp 1:** SEDLO ČERTOVICA (1,232 m), the highest road pass in Slovakia, on road I/72 between Kráľova Lehota and Podbrezová. Legal basis: named in NAPANT's visitor regulations as a designated camping/bivouac place, specified as 'below the Horská záchranná služba chalet or behind the Motorest Čertovica building' - so pitch at one of those two spots, not just anywhere on the pass. Why it is the right night-one camp: it is drive-in (four public car parks, ~200 spaces), it is exempt from the daylight-movement restriction because it sits on a state road, it has a motorest, chalets, a small ski centre and a permanently manned HZS station within a few hundred metres, and it is a listed water/resupply point on the ridge. Character: a broad, wind-exposed saddle with dwarf pine and ski infrastructure - not wild, and traffic noise from I/72 is real, but that is the price of a legal zero-effort Friday camp. It is also the natural midpoint of the whole 100 km traverse, so both the eastern (Variant A) and the high western (Variant B) weekends start from it.
- **Camp 2:** VARIANT A: SEDLO PRIEHYBA, on the eastern (Kráľovohoľská) ridge between útulňa Ramža and Andrejcová. Legal basis: named in the visitor regulations as a designated camping place. It is one of the listed drinking-water points on the ridge, which is what makes it usable as a dry camp; there is no manned facility, so it is a genuine ridge bivouac. Approx. 17-19 km east of Čertovica. VARIANT B: ÚTULŇA ĎURKOVÁ / útulňa pod Chabencom (1,623 m), just below the main crest west of Chopok, built on the site of a former shepherd's hut. Legal basis: listed in the regulations, with tenting explicitly available when the shelter is full; there is a spring beside it and established pitching ground. It is the highest of the ridge shelters and the natural end of the big Ďumbier-Chopok day. BACKUP anchors on the same ridge, all legal and all with water: útulňa Ramža (1,260 m, ~20 places, spring 300 m west) about 3 h east of Čertovica - the fallback if Saturday goes badly; útulňa Andrejcová (1,410 m, 30 inside + 12 tent pitches, 5 EUR/person, spring, fireplace) further east; Hiadeľské sedlo (útulňa) at the western end; Predné sedlo under Kráľova hoľa at the far east; and the hamlet of Magurka. This density of legal fallbacks - eight anchors spread along ~100 km of crest, several with water and a roof - is the reason this range is the strongest legal-camping proposition in high Slovakia.
- **Actual spacing vs 15-25 km target ⚠️:** Variant A (recommended): Čertovica -> sedlo Priehyba is approximately 17-19 km with ~900-1,100 m of cumulative ascent, 6-7 h - ESTIMATED by interpolating published stage times (Chata M. R. Štefánika -> Čertovica -> Ramža = 4 h 35 total, of which Čertovica -> Ramža is ~3 h; Ramža -> Andrejcová = 6 h 50, with Priehyba between them), since no source publishes the Čertovica -> Priehyba pair directly [uncertain]. This sits COMFORTABLY INSIDE the 15-25 km criterion. Variant B: Čertovica -> Ďurková is a published 28 km / +1,900 m / 7 h 30 - marginally ABOVE the 25 km ceiling and a genuinely hard day with a full pack over two 2,000 m summits; the honest framing is 'one long day, not two moderate ones'. Variant C: Hiadeľské sedlo -> Ďurková is ~18-20 km. The structural point is that NAPANT is the ONE range on the whole candidate list where the legal anchors are spaced at 15-30 km by design (because they were placed to serve a multi-day traverse), rather than at the 8-15 km typical of Polish Beskid/Gorce shelters - so the 15-25 km criterion is met naturally rather than by contrivance. The trade-off is that between Čertovica and Ďurková there is NO legal anchor for 28 km, so on Variant B you are committed once you leave the pass.
- **Water sources ⚠️:** Markedly better than Veľká Fatra - this is a crystalline (granite/gneiss) massif in its high western half and the ridge shelters were sited at springs. Named drinking-water points along the whole traverse, east to west: Telgárt, útulňa Andrejcová (spring at the shelter), sedlo Priehybka, sedlo Priehyba, útulňa Ramža (spring ~300 m west of the shelter), sedlo Čertovica (chalets and motorest - the last resupply point before the high section), Chata gen. M. R. Štefánika, Kamenná chata pod Chopkom, útulňa Ďurková (spring at the shelter), Skalka, Hiadeľské sedlo (the last water is above the saddle on the Donovaly side), and Donovaly. One long-distance account states plainly that 'there is no shortage of springs on the whole route'. CAVEATS: (a) the high crest itself between Čertovica and Ďurková - Ďumbier, Chopok, Dereše, Chabenec - carries essentially NO water for 28 km except at the two manned huts (Štefánika, Kamenná chata), so Variant B needs 2.5-3 l carried and both huts must be assumed open; (b) Chata Kosodrevina is closed all season in 2026, removing one refill option on the Chopok south side; (c) in the 2026 drought, downgrade every unnamed seep and confirm the útulňa springs by phone or on hiking.sk before relying on a dry-camp plan. Filter or boil anything not from a hut tap - sheep grazing and heavy hut traffic both apply.
- **Fire / cooking ⚠️:** 'Táborenie a zakladanie ohňa mimo miest na to určených je zakázané' - camping and lighting fires outside designated places are prohibited, under the visitor regulations and Act 543/2002. The practical exception is that some of the designated anchors have a built fireplace: útulňa Andrejcová is documented as having one (ohnisko), and the Čertovica motorest/chalet area has its own facilities. Use only an existing fire-ring at a designated anchor, and in late July 2026 - with a nationwide elevated forest-fire warning in force after prolonged heat and drought - the right answer is no fire at all. Gas/liquid-fuel stoves: not prohibited by the regulations and universal practice at the útulne and designated saddles; the safe reading is that a stove is acceptable at the designated camping places, on bare ground, away from dwarf pine and dry grass, and is not a licence to cook anywhere on the ridge [uncertain]. Dwarf pine (kosodrevina) burns explosively - never light anything among it. Pack out all rubbish; the útulne are unstaffed and have no waste collection.

**Safety & Costs**

- **Rescue / insurance:** BUY A POLICY - and here it matters more than anywhere else on the list, because this is 2,000 m terrain with chained sections and long approaches. Slovak law makes any person in the mountains liable for the costs of Horská záchranná služba rescue and search operations (minors and persons without legal capacity exempt). Typical bills: average ground intervention ~400 EUR; search or helicopter operation 2,000-4,000 EUR, with helicopter flight time at 60 EUR+ per minute (30-45 min = 1,800-2,700 EUR). Minimum sensible cover for HZS costs 20,000 EUR; price 1-3 EUR per person per day for a Slovak/Polish 'poistenie do hôr' or an EU travel policy with a mountain rider. EHIC/EKUZ covers hospital treatment but NOT rescue and transport. Make sure the policy names 'turistika / vysokohorská turistika' and does not exclude unorganised camping. NAPANT-specific positives: HZS runs an oblastné stredisko covering the Low Tatras with a permanently manned station AT sedlo Čertovica - your Friday campsite is next door to mountain rescue - plus posts on the Jasná/Chopok side. HZS 18 300 or 112; install the HZS app.
- **Bears / ticks:** BEARS - the highest exposure of the three Group-C ranges. The Low Tatras hold one of Europe's densest brown-bear populations (plus lynx and wolf), and NAPANT runs a dedicated bear intervention team. Current specifics: NAPANT has warned hikers to AVOID the Trangoška -> Chata gen. M. R. Štefánika trail because a resident bear there has completely lost its wariness after being fed by tourists and cottage owners - that trail is a standard approach to the Ďumbier section, so Variant B should use the Čertovica ridge approach and not the Trangoška shortcut. Slovak media reported two separate bear-surprise incidents involving tourists in the Tatras in June 2026, and a bear attacked two hunters in the Low Tatras. Bear mating season runs into July, which raises activity and reduces natural caution.
