Camping discipline is non-negotiable at ridge anchors: cook and eat well away from the tent, never store food or scented items inside, hang food or use the útulňa's arrangements, keep a clean site (the útulne attract food waste and therefore bears), make noise on wooded sections, avoid dawn/dusk/night movement in forest - which the daylight-movement rule enforces anyway. Bear spray is legal in Slovakia and worth carrying here specifically. TICKS / TBE: the ridge straddles the Banská Bystrica and Žilina regions, which together account for ~84% of Slovakia's tick-borne encephalitis cases (Banská Bystrica ~47.4%, Žilina ~36.6%, 2019-2023) - the worst TBE region in the country, and Slovakia recently recorded its highest case count in 60 years. Peak May-September. Vaccination is the only real protection; otherwise permethrin-treated clothing, DEET/icaridin, nightly body checks, avoid tall grass pitches. Avoid unpasteurised sheep dairy from salaše (documented alimentary TBE route). One mitigation specific to this range: much of the recommended Variant A ridge is above the tree line on short grass and dwarf pine, which is lower-risk tick habitat than the beech belt.
- **Weather / storms:** The most serious weather of the three - this is a 2,000 m range with a long, fully exposed, treeless crest. July-August: ridge daytime maxima 10-18 C on the high section, valley 25-33 C in a heat spell; nights at 1,200-1,650 m typically 4-11 C, and at Ďurková (1,623 m) can drop lower - take a comfort-limit ~0-5 C bag and a properly guyable tent, because Chopok is one of the windiest points in Slovakia and Čertovica and the ridge saddles funnel wind hard. Convective pattern: build-up from late morning, cells firing typically 13:00-18:00, with the Ďumbier - Chopok - Dereše crest an unbroken lightning-exposure line above 1,900 m for many kilometres, including chained rocky steps on Ďumbier that become dangerous wet. OPERATING RULE: be off the high crest by 14:00-15:00; on Variant B that means leaving Čertovica at 06:15 (the first legal minute) for a 28 km day, and it is genuinely tight - if the forecast shows early storms, do not start Variant B at all, switch to Variant A. Variant A's eastern ridge tops out around 1,500-1,600 m with forest never far below, so it is far more storm-tolerant, which is another argument for it. Secondary hazards: fog on the broad Chopok/Dereše plateaux; sudden temperature drops with frontal passage; in 2026 an unusually hot, dry pattern raising fire and dehydration risk. Check shmu.sk warnings, windy.com, and the Jasná/Chopok resort weather and webcams (the Chopok summit station reports wind, which is the number the tent depends on).
- **Bail-out plan:** The best-equipped escape network of the three ranges, but with long distances. On the high section (Variant B): Chata gen. M. R. Štefánika (manned, below Ďumbier) and Kamenná chata pod Chopkom (manned, on the ridge) are both roofed, staffed refuges within the day; from Chopok the Jasná cable cars (Funitel/chairlifts) drop you to Demänovská dolina in minutes during operating hours; from the Chopok south side the Srdiečko lifts serve Trangoška/Bystrá - but NOTE Chata Kosodrevina is closed 1 April - 3 December 2026, and the Trangoška trail carries a bear warning. On the eastern section (Variant A): útulňa Ramža and útulňa Andrejcová give shelter from weather (unstaffed, bring a bag); descents lead south to the Horehronie villages (Bacúch, Beňuš, Polomka) and north to the Liptov side, both 2-3 h. At the base of everything is SEDLO ČERTOVICA itself: state road I/72, scheduled buses, motorest, chalets, ~200 parking spaces and a permanently manned HZS station - the single best bail-out point on the whole candidate list, and it is where your car is. Western end: Hiadeľské sedlo útulňa, then Donovaly (resort, buses, services). GSM: good on the open crest and summits (resort and valley transmitters on both sides), poor in the Demänovská, Bystrá and Horehronie valleys and in the forest belt - stay high to call. Emergency 112 / HZS 18 300.
- **Total fees ⚠️:** No entry ticket to NAPANT and no fee for camping at the designated anchors, with one exception. Realistic weekend cash outlay, ESTIMATED [uncertain]: Slovak 10-day e-vignette 10.80 EUR per car (effectively mandatory on this route); parking at sedlo Čertovica - three different operators, no confirmed 2026 tariff, budget 0-10 EUR per day, so 0-20 EUR per car for the weekend; útulňa Andrejcová 5 EUR per person if you use that anchor (covers a tent pitch as well as a bunk) - Priehyba, Čertovica, Ramža and Ďurková carry no listed fee; mountain-rescue insurance ~3-8 EUR per person for a longer weekend; food and drinks at the Čertovica motorest / Chata gen. M. R. Štefánika / Kamenná chata 15-35 EUR per person; return bus if you run the linear Variant B (Donovaly -> Ružomberok -> Liptovský Hrádok -> Čertovica) ~8-14 EUR per person [uncertain]; optional Chopok cable car as a shortcut or bail-out roughly 20-30 EUR one way [uncertain]. TOTAL: roughly 35-80 EUR per person for the weekend excluding fuel, plus 11-31 EUR per car of vignette and parking. Fuel is the real differentiator: at ~480-500 km round trip this range costs roughly twice as much to reach as Malá Fatra.

**Season, Crowds & Logistics**

- **Season fit:** Excellent for July-August, and this is the range whose season window is widest. All the 1 October - 30 June seasonal closures are expired, the ridge is snow-free (though old drifts can linger in north-facing gullies under Ďumbier into early July), all útulne and the manned huts are in operation, springs are flowing, and the days are long enough to absorb the daylight-movement rule. Specific July-August negatives: peak thunderstorm frequency on a 2,000 m exposed crest; peak bear activity with mating season running into July and at least one habituated animal flagged by the park; peak TBE season in the country's worst TBE region; and the 2026 heat/drought layer. September is arguably the best month here (stable weather, fewer people, still snow-free) but the daylight window shrinks fast against the one-hour-after-sunrise / one-hour-before-sunset rule and Ďurková gets cold. From 1 October the seasonal closures return and the high traverse becomes a winter mountaineering objective with serious avalanche exposure on the Ďumbier-Chopok section - the two-camp weekend format is a summer-only proposition here.
- **Crowd level:** SHARPLY SPLIT, which you can exploit. The Chopok - Ďumbier corridor is one of Slovakia's busiest mountain areas: the Jasná resort's cable cars deposit day visitors straight onto the ridge, Kamenná chata and Chata gen. M. R. Štefánika are crowded on summer weekends, and Ďumbier's chained sections queue. Čertovica itself is a road pass with a motorest and can be busy with car traffic, and the four car parks (200 spaces) can fill on holiday weekends. By contrast the EASTERN Kráľovohoľská half - Čertovica -> Ramža -> Priehyba -> Andrejcová -> Kráľova hoľa - is genuinely quiet even in August: only two shelters in the whole eastern section, no lifts, no resorts, and traffic is almost entirely long-distance traverse hikers on the Cesta hrdinov SNP. That is the decisive argument for Variant A on a peak-season weekend. Friday-evening advantage: you take a Čertovica pitch and a parking space before the Saturday wave, and on Variant A you walk away from the crowds rather than into them. Capacity note: útulňa Andrejcová takes 30 inside plus 12 tents and Ramža about 20 - on a peak Saturday these do fill, and there is no booking, so a tent (rather than reliance on a bunk) is the right plan.
- **Navigation / maps:** The main ridge is the red-blazed Cesta hrdinov SNP (part of European long-distance route E8) and is among the best-waymarked lines in Slovakia - the ridge itself is hard to lose in clear weather. Standard KST paint blazes; signposts give TIMES, not distances. Maps and apps: mapy.cz (best overall; carries the útulne, springs and - importantly here - the designated camping saddles as POIs), hiking.sk (definitive stage times, útulňa pages, closure feed), freemap.sk / turistickamapa.sk for the official KST layer, OsmAnd or Locus with OpenAndroMaps Slovakia; napant.sk for the visitor regulations, the seasonal-closure list and the annexes that define the camping anchors. Paper backup: VKÚ/CBS sheets 'Nízke Tatry - Chopok' and 'Nízke Tatry - Kráľova hoľa' 1:50,000. Navigational traps: the broad Chopok/Dereše plateaux in fog (few features, several lift-related paths that are not tourist trails); the Ďumbier summit block where the summer red route and the winter pole variant diverge; and on the eastern ridge, several forest-road crossings where the blazes step off the crest. Pre-mark the exact designated camping points (Čertovica behind the motorest / below the HZS chalet; sedlo Priehyba; útulňa Ramža; útulňa Ďurková) as waypoints before departure - unlike Malá Fatra and Veľká Fatra, several of these anchors are not at a visible building, so knowing the coordinates is what keeps the camp legal.

> ⚠️ _Fields above contain self-flagged agent estimates/caveats (verify before departure): Distance / time from Ustroń, Parking, Public transport, Camping rules, 2026 status (live blockers), Hourly / seasonal restrictions, Fine risk, Actual spacing vs 15-25 km target, Water sources, Fire / cooking, Total fees_

---

### 11. Gorce (PL, GPN)

**Access & Travel**

- **Distance / time from Ustroń ⚠️:** ESTIMATE — not verified in a routing engine (data-source flag: manual estimate from road network, 2026-07-28). Ustron -> Lubomierz-Rzeki (Trusiowka bivouac + GPN car park, Kamienica valley): approx. 175-190 km, 2 h 40 min - 3 h 10 min. Practical road chain: Ustron -> S1 Bielsko-Biala -> S52/DK52 Wadowice -> DK28 Sucha Beskidzka -> Jordanow -> DK7 (Zakopianka) Rabka-Zdroj -> DK28 Mszana Dolna -> local road Lubomierz -> Rzeki (last ~15 km is a narrow valley road, slow). Ustron -> Koninki / Poreba Wielka (Oberowka bivouac, blue trail to Turbacz) is slightly shorter: approx. 165-180 km, 2 h 30 min - 3 h; from Mszana Dolna it is only ~10 km. A motorway-heavy alternative (A4 Cieszyn -> Krakow -> S7 -> Rabka) is ~225-235 km but similar or slightly better in time on a Friday afternoon; the DK28/DK7 variant is shorter but exposed to Friday holiday traffic on the Zakopianka. Budget the pessimistic end (3 h+) for a Friday-evening departure in high season. [uncertain]
- **PL/SK/CZ border:** Not applicable — Gorce and Gorczanski National Park (GPN) lie entirely inside Poland (Malopolska voivodeship). No border crossing, no currency change, no map/marking system change. All signage is the standard Polish PTTK colour-stripe system.
- **Parking ⚠️:** GPN itself runs exactly ONE car park: in the Kamienica valley at polana Trusiowka (Lubomierz-Rzeki), directly next to the bivouac site — this is the key logistical advantage for a Friday-night arrival. Reported rate history: 12 PLN/day (2023 director's order), 20 PLN (2021), 30 PLN/day (2024 reports); the current 2026 rate is not published on the GPN website and must be confirmed on site or by phone (GPN office +48 18 33 17 207) — assume ~25-35 PLN/day. [uncertain] All other car parks (Koninki/Poreba Wielka, Lubomierz-Przyslop, Konina, Lopuszna) are run by private local operators, typically 20-30 PLN/day, cash. Overnight standing is normal at Trusiowka because the car park serves the bivouac site; at private lots confirm with the operator. Koninki lots (ski complex / Ostoja Koninki) are large and rarely fill; Trusiowka is small and fills on sunny summer weekends by mid-morning — arriving Friday evening avoids this entirely. Wild roadside parking in the Kamienica valley is discouraged and can be ticketed.
- **Public transport ⚠️:** Possible but sparse — good enough to convert the trip into a linear A->B walk, not good enough to rely on without checking timetables. Nearest railway station: Mszana Dolna (line 104 Chabowka - Nowy Sacz, a few trains per day) and Rabka-Zdroj / Nowy Targ on the Krakow-Zakopane corridor. From Mszana Dolna, private minibuses (busy: Mszana Dolna <-> Koninki/Poreba Wielka, Mszana Dolna <-> Lubomierz-Rzeki) run several times a day, thinner at weekends and in the evening. This makes the classic linear variant realistic: leave the car at Koninki, walk Koninki -> Turbacz -> Gorc -> Rzeki, return Rzeki -> Mszana Dolna -> Koninki by bus. Timetables change seasonally and are not published in one authoritative place — verify on the operator's or gmina Niedzwiedz/Mszana Dolna page in the week of departure. [uncertain]
- **Vignettes ⚠️:** None. The whole approach is on Polish roads; Polish motorways on this route (S1, S52, S7, DK28) are toll-free for cars, and Poland has no vignette. No SK e-znamka or CZ e-dalnicni kupon needed. Only the A4 Katowice-Krakow section (if the Krakow variant is used) is tolled — approx. 20-25 PLN one way for a car. [uncertain]

**Camping Legality**

- **Protection status:** National park — Gorczanski Park Narodowy (GPN), established 1981, ~7,030 ha, the strictest Polish protection regime. The park core is under strict/active protection with a statutory camping ban; the surrounding Gorce ridges (Gorc, Luban, Hale Gorcowskie, the ridge east of Przelecz Przyslop) are OUTSIDE the park and fall only under general forest law, which is why the two student tent bases and the Turbacz shelter camping field can exist legally. Entry to the park is ticketed.
- **Camping rules:** Hard rule, quoted by GPN: 'Na terenie Gorczanskiego Parku Narodowego obowiazuje zakaz biwakowania z wyjatkiem dwoch wyznaczonych miejsc biwakowych' — camping inside GPN is banned except at two designated bivouac sites: (1) OBEROWKA, Poreba Wielka-Koninki, on the blue trail to Turbacz; (2) TRUSIOWKA, Lubomierz-Rzeki, in the Kamienica valley. Both are open ALL YEAR. Legal basis: art. 15 ust. 1 pkt 23 ustawy o ochronie przyrody (16.04.2004) — tents only in places designated by the park director. Fees from 1 May 2025: 10 PLN per adult per night, 5 PLN per child/youth under 18, 5 PLN per person for day use without an overnight, 5 PLN for lighting a campfire. Infrastructure at both: two fire rings, a roofed shelter (wiata), ecological toilets (May-October; a portable toilet at Trusiowka outside the season). Trusiowka is adapted for wheelchair users and sits next to the GPN car park; Oberowka's car park is ~800 m away. Contact the ranger before arrival: Oberowka — Michal Kosiarski, +48 660 490 273 / 18 3317 010; Trusiowka — Hubert Swierz, +48 660 471 698; site manager Bartlomiej Palac +48 888 952 371. No booking system is published, so phone ahead on a July weekend. OUTSIDE the park boundary, three further legal tent options exist and are the key to a real two-camp itinerary: Schronisko PTTK na Turbaczu (own camping field, 30 PLN/person/night — the shelter lies just outside the park), Studencka Baza Namiotowa GORC on Hale Gorcowskie (49°33'27.9"N, 20°14'49.7"E, 10 PLN own tent / 15 PLN base tent), and Studencka Baza Namiotowa LUBAN on polana Wierch Lubania (~1,200 m). 'Zanocuj w lesie' (State Forests bushcraft programme) does NOT apply inside GPN; it may apply in the surrounding Nadlesnictwo Limanowa/Nowy Targ forests — verify per forest district, as several districts suspended it in 2026 because of drought.
- **2026 status (live blockers) ⚠️:** Checked 2026-07-28. (1) TRAIL WORKS — from Monday 27 July 2026 GPN started renovation on two sections, scheduled to run until end of September 2026: the walking/cycling/horse route Koninki Hucisko - Konina Potasznia on the OBEROWKA - PASIEKA section, and the GREEN trail from Niedzwiedz to the PTTK shelter on Turbacz on the section from the park boundary to Turbaczyk. Trails are NOT closed, but expect machinery, material transport and rough surface — this directly touches the Oberowka approach, an argument for using Trusiowka as camp 1 this season. (2) NIGHT CLOSURES — GPN closes selected trail sections at night (sunset to 08:00) from 18 March to 5 May to protect capercaillie leks (green trail polana Stawieniec - Kudlon, yellow trail polana Adamowka - polana Przyslopek, black trail polana Pyrzowka - Kudlon - polana Kopa). NOT in force in late July, but it is the pattern to re-check if the trip slips to spring. (3) EDUCATIONAL TRAIL 'Partyzancka sciezka na Turbacz' was liquidated (2023) — old guidebooks and printed maps still show it. (4) No park-wide fire ban or 'zakaz wstepu do lasu' announced for GPN as of 2026-07-28, but summer 2026 is a drought/storm year in Poland (storm fronts 7 and 19 July 2026, ~29 of 429 forest districts under an entry ban at some point) — RE-CHECK gpn.gov.pl/aktualnosci and the ranger phone numbers in the week of departure. [uncertain]
- **Hourly / seasonal restrictions:** No general daily time window for hikers in GPN — unlike NP Mala Fatra there is no 6:00-21:00 rule, so a Friday-evening arrival and a dusk approach are legal. The only time-based restriction is the seasonal night ban (sunset to 08:00) on selected trails from 18 March to 5 May for capercaillie protection — irrelevant for a late-July trip. Bivouac sites Oberowka and Trusiowka operate all year, with the ecological toilets serviced May-October. Ticket sales points are staffed seasonally (Koninki, Lubomierz-Rzeki, the Education Centre in Poreba Wielka; mobile points on polana Garbowska, Starmaszka and Borek pass) — outside staffed hours buy online via eparki.pl or the mPay app before entering.
- **Fine risk ⚠️:** Wild camping inside GPN is a wykroczenie under art. 127 ustawy o ochronie przyrody (breach of an art. 15 prohibition) — punishable by arrest or a fine imposed by a court, and in practice by an on-the-spot mandate from Straz Parku or Police, typically in the 100-500 PLN band, with the statutory court maximum far higher (up to 5,000 PLN). [uncertain] Regional media report tourists being fined for overnight stays in GPN, so enforcement is real, not theoretical. Additional exposure: hiking without a valid entry ticket, and lighting a fire outside the designated fire rings (separate offence, also chargeable under the forest fire regulations). The risk-free path is trivially cheap here — 10 PLN buys a legal pitch.

**Route & Camps**

- **Suggested route ⚠️:** RECOMMENDED (loop around Dolina Kamienicy, car stays at Trusiowka, meets the 15-25 km spacing criterion). FRIDAY: drive Ustron -> Lubomierz-Rzeki, park at the GPN Trusiowka car park, pitch on the Trusiowka bivouac meadow (0.1 km from the car). SATURDAY: Rzeki -> Nowa Polana -> GPN boundary -> Gorc Kamienicki -> Gorc (1,228 m) -> polana Przyslop Dolny -> polana Jaworzyna Kamienicka (1,288 m) -> polana Gabrowska -> Schronisko PTTK na Turbaczu (1,283 m) / Turbacz summit (1,310 m). Verified on mapa-turystyczna.pl: 16.7 km, +994 m / -380 m, 5 h 55 min walking, 27 GOT points; blue then red trail, technically easy (forest and meadow paths, no exposure), the only real effort is the sustained 1,000 m climb out of the Kamienica valley. SUNDAY: Turbacz -> Hala Turbacz -> Przelecz Borek (1,009 m) -> Kudlon (1,274 m) -> polana Stawieniec -> Rzeki, approx. 11-13 km, mostly descending, 3 h 30 min - 4 h 30 min (green/black trails) [uncertain — distance estimated, not measured]. Total ~29 km, ~1,100 m ascent over two walking days — comfortable, with Saturday the long day. VARIANT A (linear, uses both GPN bivouacs): Fri camp Oberowka (Koninki) -> Sat blue trail Koninki -> Turbacz (8.2 km, +741 m, 3 h 30 min) and on over Jaworzyna Kamienicka and Gorc down to Rzeki -> Sun short walk out; needs a bus/second car shuttle and puts a 25 km day in the middle. VARIANT B (student bases, cheapest and most social): Fri Trusiowka -> Sat to SBN Gorc on Hale Gorcowskie (~8-9 km, 10 PLN own tent) -> Sun over Turbacz back down — but the Saturday leg falls well under the 15 km target.
- **Friday-evening feasibility:** GOOD — one of the best on the whole candidate list, because the Trusiowka bivouac is essentially AT the car park. Budget: leave Ustron 17:00-17:30 after work, 2 h 40 min - 3 h 10 min drive, arrive Rzeki 20:00-20:45; sunset in the Gorce at the end of July is approx. 20:30-20:40 with usable civil twilight until ~21:15. Walking distance from car to pitch is ~100 m, so even a 21:30 arrival works — the tent goes up by headtorch on a flat mown meadow with a wiata and fire ring. This removes the usual Friday risk entirely: no night approach, no navigation, no water carry. If Oberowka is chosen instead, the car park is ~800 m away — still trivial. Any variant that tries to reach the Turbacz shelter or Hale Gorcowskie on Friday night (3 h 30 min+ climb from Koninki or Rzeki) is NOT recommended: it would mean 2-3 hours in the dark on a rough, currently-under-repair trail. Recommendation: sleep low on Friday, do the climb fresh on Saturday.
- **Camp 1 ⚠️:** TRUSIOWKA — official GPN bivouac site on polana Trusiowka, Lubomierz-Rzeki, in the Kamienica valley (Turbacz forest district). Legal basis: one of exactly two sites designated by the GPN director under art. 15 ust. 1 pkt 23 ustawy o ochronie przyrody, therefore fully legal and open year-round. Flat mown meadow beside the Kamienica stream, at the mouth of the valley where the blue trail towards Gorc starts. Infrastructure: two fire rings, roofed shelter (wiata), ecological toilets (May-October), portable toilet off-season, wheelchair-accessible layout, GPN car park immediately adjacent. Fee 10 PLN/adult/night + 5 PLN for a campfire. Ranger Hubert Swierz +48 660 471 698 — phone ahead on a July weekend. Water: the Kamienica stream (filter/boil) plus the site's tap where available. [uncertain — whether a drinking-water tap is present]
- **Camp 2:** SCHRONISKO PTTK NA TURBACZU im. Wladyslawa Orkana — camping field belonging to the mountain shelter at ~1,283 m, just below Turbacz summit and just OUTSIDE the GPN boundary (GPN's own page lists the Turbacz, Stare Wierchy and Maciejowa shelters as lying outside the park), which is why tents are permitted there while they are banned on park land 200 m away. Legal basis: private/PTTK land outside the statutory park camping ban, tent pitches sold officially at 30 PLN per person per night (turbacz.net price list). Big advantages for camp 2: kitchen and hot meals, 110 beds as a bad-weather bailout, a year-round GOPR rescue station on site, water and toilets. Disadvantage: it is the busiest spot in the Gorce — expect company. ALTERNATIVES, both outside the park and cheaper: Studencka Baza Namiotowa GORC on Hale Gorcowskie (49°33'27.9"N, 20°14'49.7"E, season 15 June - 28 August 2026, last night 27/28 Aug; 10 PLN own tent, 15 PLN base tent, 5-8 PLN for PTTK guides/academic branch members; no booking needed for own tents; 33rd 'Gorcstok' festival 14-16 Aug 2026 = crowded), and Studencka Baza Namiotowa LUBAN on polana Wierch Lubania ~1,200 m (season 14 June - 10 September 2026, 'NaLesnik' day 1 August). The second GPN bivouac, OBEROWKA (Poreba Wielka-Koninki, blue trail to Turbacz, two fire rings, wiata, seasonal toilets, car park 800 m away, same 10/5/5 PLN tariff, ranger Michal Kosiarski +48 660 490 273), is the choice if a park-only, fully-official two-camp itinerary is wanted — at the cost of spacing.
- **Actual spacing vs 15-25 km target ⚠️:** MEETS the criterion only in the recommended variant, and MISSES it badly in the park-only variant — stated explicitly rather than smoothed over. Recommended pairing Trusiowka (Rzeki) -> Turbacz shelter over Gorc and Jaworzyna Kamienicka = 16.7 km measured on mapa-turystyczna.pl (+994 m, 5 h 55 min) — inside the 15-25 km target, at its lower end. Park-only pairing Oberowka -> Trusiowka is only about 8 km apart in a straight line and the two official bivouacs are the only legal anchors INSIDE GPN, so any itinerary confined to the park lands in the 8-15 km band the scope note warns about: Oberowka -> Turbacz shelter is 8.2 km (+741 m, 3 h 28 min, blue trail, measured), and Trusiowka -> SBN Gorc is roughly 8-9 km [uncertain — estimated]. Conclusion: the Gorce can hit 15-25 km only by using an out-of-park anchor (Turbacz shelter camping field or a student base) as camp 2, or by walking a big horseshoe Koninki -> Turbacz -> Gorc -> Rzeki (~25 km) as a single Saturday. Gorce are a genuinely small range (park ~70 km2) — the 15-25 km target fights the terrain here far more than in Beskid Sadecki or the Slovak ranges.
- **Water sources ⚠️:** Generally good by Polish-Beskid standards — flysch bedrock, many springs, and both valleys carry permanent streams — but the RIDGE polany are the dry part. Reliable: the Kamienica stream at Trusiowka/Rzeki (camp 1, filter or boil); Konina/Poreba streams at Koninki near Oberowka; the Turbacz shelter (tap/kitchen water, the only certain drinking source high on the ridge); 'Zrodelko Milosci', a built-up permanent spring at the eastern end of Hala Dluga, ~30-40 min east of the Turbacz shelter. Unreliable in a dry summer: the small spring below the bacowka on Hala Dluga has almost dried up in recent seasons; the Hala Turbacz / Jaworzyna Kamienicka / Gorc meadow section between the valley and the shelter has NO dependable water — carry 2-2.5 L for the 6-hour Saturday climb. The student bases (Gorc, Luban) have their own water points but they are seasonal and can be rationed in drought; ask on arrival. 2026 is a drought summer in southern Poland, so treat every spring except the shelter tap as 'may be dry'. [uncertain — 2026 spring yields not individually verified]
- **Fire / cooking:** Campfires are LEGAL but only in the built fire rings at Oberowka and Trusiowka, and only after paying the 5 PLN campfire fee; there are two fire rings at each site. Everywhere else in GPN and in the surrounding State Forests, open fire is prohibited (art. 15 ustawy o ochronie przyrody plus forest fire regulations) — a gas/liquid-fuel stove is the only legal cooking method on the ridge, at the Turbacz shelter camping field and at the student bases (the bases have their own kitchen/fire arrangements run by the staff). In a drought summer assume a stove-only trip and treat the 5 PLN fire at camp 1 as a bonus to confirm on site.

**Safety & Costs**

- **Rescue / insurance:** GOPR Grupa Podhalanska covers the Gorce; mountain rescue in Poland is FREE of charge for the victim (ustawa o bezpieczenstwie i ratownictwie w gorach i na zorganizowanych terenach narciarskich, 2011) — no HZS-style bill and no mandatory policy, unlike Slovakia. A year-round GOPR rescue station operates AT the Turbacz shelter, which is unusually good cover for a range this size. Emergency numbers: 601 100 300 (GOPR/TOPR), 985, or 112; the 'Ratunek' app transmits GPS position. Part of the GPN entry fee is explicitly declared as co-financing GOPR. Private accident/liability insurance is optional; standard travel or a PTTK/Alpenverein membership is enough. No SK/CZ rescue-cost exposure on this option.
- **Bears / ticks:** BEARS: present but scarce. GPN reports bear sightings almost every year, most often in late summer and autumn (beechnuts, bilberries, raspberries); tracks were documented in the Kiczory area, and the park runs the Polish-Slovak 'Drapiezne pogranicze' project logging wolf, lynx and bear records — Gorce sit on a migration corridor between the Beskids and the Tatras. Practical rule: food in a bag hung away from the tent or in the car at Trusiowka, no food waste at the fire ring, make noise on dawn/dusk forest sections. Risk of an encounter on a July weekend is low but non-zero; both camp sites are semi-managed and have never been reported as bear-problem sites. WOLVES and LYNX are present, effectively no risk to hikers. TICKS: this is the real hazard. Malopolska ticks are active essentially all year with numbers rising; national data for 2025 record 48,725 Lyme cases (+63% year on year) and 935 TBE cases (a 3.5x rise over six years). Gorce meadows and forest edges — exactly the terrain of this route and of both bivouac meadows — are prime tick habitat. Take permethrin-treated trousers or repellent, do a full body check every evening, and consider TBE (KZM) vaccination as a season-long investment; it is not a same-week fix.
- **Weather / storms:** Typical July-August pattern: clear or hazy mornings, cumulus building from 12:00-13:00, convective thunderstorms most likely 14:00-18:00, often violent and short, with a secondary risk of night storms in a humid airmass. Summer 2026 in southern Poland has been a drought-plus-storm season with damaging fronts on 7 and 19 July 2026. Gorce are less exposed than the Tatras — the ridge is largely forested with open polany, so lightning exposure is moderate and there is almost always tree cover within minutes — but the open stretches (Hala Dluga, Jaworzyna Kamienicka, Hale Gorcowskie, the Gorc summit meadows) are genuinely exposed and should be crossed before ~15:00. Practical plan: leave camp 1 by 07:00-08:00 so the Gorc-Jaworzyna-Turbacz meadow traverse is done by early afternoon and the shelter is reachable if a cell builds. Ridge temperature at 1,300 m is roughly 6-8 °C below the valley; nights on the meadows fall to 6-10 °C even in July, with heavy dew — bring a comfort-5 °C bag. Fog on the polany is common at dawn.
- **Bail-out plan:** Excellent for a Polish range this size. Primary bailout: Schronisko PTTK na Turbacz — open year round, 110 beds, kitchen, and a permanent GOPR station on site; from almost anywhere on the recommended Saturday leg it is 1-3 h away. Secondary shelters: Schronisko PTTK Stare Wierchy (north-west, above Koninki) and Bacowka PTTK Maciejowa (towards Rabka) — both give a roof and a road link. Road/transport bailouts: Koninki/Poreba Wielka (chairlift to Tobolow in season, private car parks, minibuses to Mszana Dolna, ~10 km to town) on the north-west side, and Lubomierz-Rzeki (GPN car park, valley road, minibus to Mszana Dolna) on the north-east side — the Kamienica valley descends continuously to the car, so 'walk downhill and you hit the car' holds for the whole loop. Nearest hospital and full services: Mszana Dolna / Rabka-Zdroj / Nowy Targ. GSM: good on the ridge and at the Turbacz shelter (all Polish operators), patchy to absent in the lower Kamienica valley and in the forest below Kudlon — do not count on a call from the descent, call from the ridge. Emergency: 601 100 300 / 985 / 112, plus the 'Ratunek' app.
- **Total fees ⚠️:** Per adult, for the recommended Friday-Sunday plan (prices from gpn.gov.pl and turbacz.net, checked 2026-07-28): GPN entry — 3-day ticket 20 PLN normal / 10 PLN reduced (single day 10/5, 7-day 50/25, 3-month named 200/100); Trusiowka bivouac 10 PLN/adult/night + 5 PLN campfire; Turbacz shelter tent pitch 30 PLN/person/night. Per-person total approx. 65 PLN (20 + 10 + 5 + 30). Add car park: GPN Trusiowka, most recently reported at 30 PLN/day, so 60-90 PLN per car for the weekend [uncertain — 2026 rate unpublished]. Cheapest legal variant: Trusiowka (10 PLN) + Studencka Baza Namiotowa GORC own tent (10 PLN) + 3-day park ticket (20 PLN) = 40 PLN per person. Tickets: stationary points at Koninki, Lubomierz-Rzeki and the Education Centre in Poreba Wielka; seasonal mobile points on polana Garbowska, Starmaszka and Borek pass; online via eparki.pl or the mPay app. Trail sections running along the park boundary, and the Wodzicki manor grounds in Poreba Wielka, are free. Note the price history: 8 PLN (Aug 2023) -> 9 PLN (May 2025) -> 10 PLN (current) — re-check before departure.

**Season, Crowds & Logistics**

- **Season fit:** Very well suited to late July / August, arguably peak season for this range. The high meadows (Hala Dluga, Jaworzyna Kamienicka, Hale Gorcowskie) are in full flower, bilberries and raspberries are ripe, both student tent bases are open (Gorc 15 Jun - 28 Aug 2026, Luban 14 Jun - 10 Sep 2026) and the bivouac toilets are serviced (May-October). Snow is a non-issue; the only summer negatives are afternoon storms, ticks at their seasonal peak, and drought-thinned springs. The Gorce also work as a shoulder-season and winter option because both GPN bivouacs and the Turbacz shelter are open all year — but for a Friday-Sunday tent trip in the current season, conditions are as good as they get.
- **Crowd level:** Two-speed. Turbacz and its shelter form the single busiest hub in the range: a summer Saturday brings large numbers up the blue trail from Koninki and from Nowy Targ/Lopuszna, the shelter and its camping field get full, and the Gorcstok festival at SBN Gorc (14-16 August 2026) draws a crowd. By contrast, the Kamienica valley (Rzeki, Trusiowka), the Gorc / Gorc Kamienicki ridge and the Kudlon descent are markedly quiet — GPN as a whole sees far less traffic than the Tatras or Pieniny. Friday-evening arrival is a decisive advantage: it secures a place on the small Trusiowka meadow before Saturday's day visitors and lets you climb the popular section in the morning against the flow. Trusiowka's car park is the tightest resource — small, and it fills on sunny weekend mornings.
- **Navigation / maps:** Polish standard: PTTK colour-stripe waymarking (blue/red/green/yellow/black), wooden signposts with walking times, dense and reliable in GPN. Best digital sources: mapa-turystyczna.pl (the routes used above — Rzeki->Turbacz 16.7 km / 5 h 55 min, Koninki->Turbacz 8.2 km / 3 h 28 min — come from there, with GOT point counts), plus OsmAnd or Locus Map with offline OpenStreetMap (Polish trail relations are well mapped), and the official trail pages at gpn.gov.pl/szlaki-piesze. Paper: the Compass 'Gorce' 1:50,000 sheet or the GPN visitor map. Two traps: printed maps and older guidebooks still show the 'Partyzancka sciezka na Turbacz' educational trail, which was liquidated in 2023; and the Koninki-Hucisko / Oberowka-Pasieka and Niedzwiedz-Turbaczyk (green) sections are under construction until end of September 2026, so on-the-ground routing may differ from the map. No cross-border marking differences apply — the whole range is Polish. Download the eparki.pl ticket to the phone before leaving GSM coverage.

> ⚠️ _Fields above contain self-flagged agent estimates/caveats (verify before departure): Distance / time from Ustroń, Parking, Public transport, Vignettes, 2026 status (live blockers), Fine risk, Suggested route, Camp 1, Actual spacing vs 15-25 km target, Water sources, Total fees_

---

### 12. Pieniny (PL/SK, PPN)

**Access & Travel**

- **Distance / time from Ustroń ⚠️:** ESTIMATE — not verified in a routing engine (data-source flag: manual estimate, 2026-07-28). Ustron -> Kroscienko nad Dunajcem / Szczawnica (the natural base for the Polish Pieniny): approx. 215-235 km, 3 h 00 min - 3 h 30 min. Road chain: Ustron -> S1 Bielsko-Biala -> S52/DK52 Wadowice -> DK28 Sucha Beskidzka -> Jordanow -> DK7 Rabka -> Nowy Targ -> DK969 Kroscienko/Szczawnica; the last 35 km from Nowy Targ is a slow single-carriageway road that queues badly on summer Fridays. Jaworki (trailhead for the Male Pieniny student tent base 'Pod Wysoka') is a further 8 km beyond Szczawnica. Sromowce Nizne (footbridge to Slovakia) approx. the same as Kroscienko. The Slovak side (Cerveny Klastor autocamp) is 15-20 km further, approx. 3 h 20 min - 3 h 50 min, via Niedzica/Lysa nad Dunajcom or via Nowy Targ - Trstena. This is one of the farthest options on the whole candidate list and the drive eats most of a Friday evening. [uncertain]
- **PL/SK/CZ border:** Schengen — no passport control, but several practical differences. The Polish and Slovak Pieniny are separated by the Dunajec; the classic pedestrian/cycle link is the footbridge Sromowce Nizne - Cerveny Klastor (walk/bike only, no cars), plus road crossings at Niedzica-Lysa nad Dunajcom and further east at Piwniczna/Mnisek. Currency: EUR in Slovakia, cash still useful at the PIENAP autocamp. Waymarking: Slovak KST markings use the same four colours but a different signpost convention (times in hours, no GOT points); use hiking.sk or mapy.cz for the Slovak side and mapa-turystyczna.pl for the Polish side. Crucially, the two parks have DIFFERENT rules: the Slovak Pieninsky narodny park (PIENAP) permits tents at two named facilities, the Polish PPN permits them nowhere — so 'crossing the river' changes the legal regime completely. Slovak mobile roaming is EU-regulated, no surcharge.
- **Parking ⚠️:** No park-run car parks; everything is municipal or private. Kroscienko nad Dunajcem: several lots near the centre and by the rafting pier, roughly 20-30 PLN/day [uncertain — 2026 rates not published centrally]. Szczawnica: paid lots at the Dolina Grajcarka trailhead and near the lower funicular station, similar rates, and they fill by mid-morning on summer weekends because Trzy Korony/Sokolica is a mass day-trip destination. Jaworki (Wawoz Homole, the approach to the 'Pod Wysoka' tent base): a large paid meadow car park, typically 20-30 PLN/day, fills early on sunny weekends. Sromowce Nizne (Kaciy Zamek / footbridge): paid lots, similar. Slovak side: the PIENAP autocamp at Cerveny Klastor has its own parking included in the pitch fee, and the Prielom Dunajca entrance lots are paid in EUR. Overnight standing is normal at commercial campsites and at the Jaworki lots but should be confirmed with the operator elsewhere; leaving a car overnight at a rafting-pier lot without a ticket invites a fine. Camping is far easier here than parking. [uncertain]
- **Public transport ⚠️:** The best public-transport access of any candidate on the list, which partly compensates for the driving distance. Kroscienko and Szczawnica are served by frequent minibuses (buses) from Nowy Targ and Nowy Sacz, and by direct long-distance buses from Krakow; Jaworki, Sromowce Nizne and Niedzica all have village bus links to Szczawnica/Kroscienko running through the day in summer. Nearest railheads: Nowy Targ (Krakow-Zakopane line) and Nowy Sacz. This makes a genuinely linear A->B walk realistic — e.g. park in Kroscienko, walk the whole Pieniny + Male Pieniny ridge to Jaworki, and ride the bus back — and it also solves the 'car is at the wrong end' problem that the Slovak-side variants create. Slovak side: buses Stara Lubovna - Cerveny Klastor - Lesnica, several a day. Summer timetables are dense but not published in one place — verify on e-podroznik.pl / cp.hnonline.sk in the week of departure. [uncertain]
- **Vignettes ⚠️:** None for the Polish-side plan — Polish roads on this route (S1, S52, S7, DK28, DK7, DK969) are toll-free for cars. If the Slovak side is added, the approach to Cerveny Klastor via Niedzica/Lysa nad Dunajcom or Mnisek uses only I. and II. class roads, which are NOT subject to the Slovak e-znamka; a vignette is only needed if a motorway/expressway (D1, R1, R3...) is used, e.g. a Zilina-Poprad routing. Slovak 10-day e-znamka approx. EUR 12, 1-year approx. EUR 60, bought at eznamka.sk (electronic, plate-based, no sticker). Czech e-vignette is irrelevant here. [uncertain — 2026 e-znamka tariff not re-verified]

**Camping Legality**

- **Protection status:** Two national parks facing each other across the Dunajec. POLAND: Pieninski Park Narodowy (PPN), one of Poland's smallest at approx. 2,346 ha, covering the Pieniny Czorsztynskie, the Trzy Korony massif and the Pieninki — a compact limestone/karst block, densely visited, with a strict visitor regulation. The Male Pieniny (Wysokie Skalki/Wysoka 1,050 m, Wawoz Homole, Biala Woda) lie OUTSIDE PPN in the Pieninski Park Krajobrazowy (landscape park) — a much softer regime, which is why the only mountain tent base in the region can legally exist there. SLOVAKIA: Pieninsky narodny park (PIENAP), administered from Cerveny Klastor, with its own navstevny poriadok. Net effect: the Polish national-park core is the single most restrictive small area on the whole candidate list, while the landscape-park fringe on both sides is workable.
- **Camping rules ⚠️:** ANSWER TO THE SCOPE QUESTION: there is NO legal tent pitch anywhere inside Pieninski Park Narodowy — zero designated bivouac sites, no Gorce-style exception. The PPN visitor regulation explicitly forbids 'biwakowanie' and lists no designated places, and the statutory basis is the same art. 15 ust. 1 pkt 23 ustawy o ochronie przyrody: tents only where the park director designates them, and the PPN director has designated none. PPN also bans open fire and smoking, bans dogs (assistance dogs excepted) and restricts cycling to three named sections (the Szczawnica road, a street in Kroscienko, and the raft-station path). SLOVAK SIDE — the answer is 'yes, but only in the valley': PIENAP's navstevny poriadok bans camping and bivouacking across the park EXCEPT at two named facilities — 'autokemping pred vstupom do Prielomu Dunajca v k.u. Cerveny Klastor' (the PIENAP administration's own autocamp) and 'areal zariadenia cestovneho ruchu Goralsky dvor' (listed under k.u. Haligovce in the official rules; some secondary sources place it at Cerveny Klastor). Secondary sources add a duration cap of max 2 days in a tent and max 1 night bivouacking without a tent [uncertain — not confirmed in the quoted regulation text]. Both are serviced valley campsites next to the road, not ridge bivouacs. LEGAL TENT OPTIONS THAT ACTUALLY WORK, all outside PPN: Studencka Baza Namiotowa 'Pod Wysoka' above Jaworki in the Male Pieniny (the only true mountain tent camp in the region), plus commercial campsites in the Dunajec valley — Camping Cypel Pieniny (Kroscienko), Pole Namiotowe Laskowscy (Sromowce Nizne), Pole Namiotowe OSP Sromowce Nizne (1 May - 30 Sep), Camping Polana Sosny (Niedzica), Pole nad Zalewem, Camp in Leg, Pole Knurow. The State Forests 'Zanocuj w lesie' programme does not apply inside PPN and coverage in the surrounding Nadlesnictwo Krosno/Nowy Targ forests must be checked per district.
- **2026 status (live blockers) ⚠️:** Checked 2026-07-28. No park-wide closure or fire ban announced for PPN or PIENAP as of this date, and no trail closures found in the searched sources. Standing status that will apply on any July weekend: (1) PPN trails are open only from one hour BEFORE sunrise to one hour AFTER sunset — a legal night-movement ban, permanent, not seasonal; (2) PIENAP is stricter still, permitting movement outside built-up areas only from one hour AFTER sunrise to one hour BEFORE sunset; (3) PPN fee collection at Trzy Korony and Sokolica runs 20 April - 15 November, so it is in force now; (4) the Dunajec raft/kayak season runs 1 April - 31 October; (5) the PIENAP autocamp at Cerveny Klastor operates 15 June - 30 September; (6) Studencka Baza Namiotowa 'Pod Wysoka' runs 28 June - 30 August 2026 (opened 26 June). Summer 2026 across southern Poland is a drought-and-storm season (damaging fronts 7 and 19 July 2026, dozens of forest districts hit by temporary entry bans at some point), and the Pieniny limestone dries out fast — RE-CHECK piepn.gov.pl and pienap.sk plus the local forest district in the week of departure. Trzy Korony's summit platform is periodically closed for maintenance or in high wind; check before relying on it. [uncertain]
- **Hourly / seasonal restrictions:** This is the item's second hard blocker after the camping ban. POLAND (PPN): hiking trails may be used only 'od godziny przed wschodem do godziny po zachodzie slonca' — from one hour before sunrise to one hour after sunset. At the end of July that means roughly 03:45 to 21:35, so an evening walk-in is technically legal but only just; walking the ridge at 22:00 is not. SLOVAKIA (PIENAP): markedly stricter — outside built-up areas visitors may move only 'od hodiny po vychode slnka po hodinu pred zapadom slnka', i.e. from one hour AFTER sunrise to one hour BEFORE sunset, roughly 05:45 to 19:35 in late July. Any Slovak-side plan that involves arriving after work on Friday and walking in is therefore illegal, not merely awkward. Seasonal: PPN fees at Trzy Korony/Sokolica are collected 20 April - 15 November and waived 16 November - 19 April; the Dunajec is open to boats 1 April - 31 October; the PIENAP autocamp is 15 June - 30 September only; the Male Pieniny student base is 28 June - 30 August 2026 only. There is no winter camping option anywhere in this item.
- **Fine risk ⚠️:** POLAND: pitching a tent inside PPN breaches art. 15 of the ustawa o ochronie przyrody and is a wykroczenie under art. 127 — the park's Straz Parku is explicitly empowered by the visitor regulation to 'nakladac mandaty karne'. Practical exposure is an on-the-spot mandate in the 100-500 PLN band, with a far higher statutory court maximum (up to 5,000 PLN). [uncertain — exact mandate tariff not published by PPN] Separate offences with their own tickets: lighting any fire (banned park-wide), bringing a dog, cycling off the three permitted sections, and walking the trails outside the sunrise-1h/sunset+1h window. PPN is small, heavily patrolled and heavily visited — the practical chance of being found is high, much higher than in a large forested range. SLOVAKIA: breaching the navstevny poriadok is a priestupok under zakon 543/2002 Z.z. o ochrane prirody a krajiny, enforced by the Straz prirody, with block fines in the low hundreds of euros and higher amounts by administrative decision. [uncertain — 2026 tariff not verified] Bottom line: this is the worst range on the list in which to improvise a pitch, and the legal alternatives are cheap (10 PLN at the student base).

**Route & Camps**

- **Suggested route ⚠️:** The Pieniny cannot deliver a wild two-camp weekend; what it CAN deliver is a valley-camp-to-mountain-camp traverse using legal sites on the fringe. RECOMMENDED (linear, Polish side, uses public transport to close the loop). FRIDAY: drive Ustron -> Kroscienko nad Dunajcem (3 h - 3 h 30 min), pitch at Camping Cypel Pieniny in Kroscienko, on the Dunajec, zero walking. SATURDAY: Kroscienko -> Zamkowa Gora -> Trzy Korony (982 m, ticketed viewing platform) -> Bajkow Gron -> Czertezik -> Sokolica (747 m, the famous relict pine) -> descent to Szczawnica -> Dolina Grajcarka -> Jaworki -> Wawoz Homole -> Studencka Baza Namiotowa 'Pod Wysoka' under Wysokie Skalki. Approx. 18-21 km, +900/-700 m, 6-7 h including the crowds and the ticket queues [uncertain — distance estimated by segment, not measured end-to-end]. SUNDAY: base -> Wysokie Skalki / Wysoka (1,050 m, the highest point of the Pieniny) -> the Male Pieniny border ridge -> Durbaszka -> descent to Szczawnica or back through Wawoz Homole to Jaworki; approx. 12-15 km, then a bus Jaworki/Szczawnica -> Kroscienko to recover the car. Technically easy throughout: waymarked paths, chains and steps on the Trzy Korony summit cone, no exposure elsewhere. SLOVAK VARIANT (weaker): camp at the PIENAP autocamp Cerveny Klastor, walk the Prielom Dunajca cycle/foot path and Tri Koruny from the south, cross the Sromowce Nizne footbridge — but the 1 h-after-sunrise/1 h-before-sunset movement rule and the fact that both legal Slovak pitches are road-side valley camps make this a day-trip structure, not a traverse.
- **Friday-evening feasibility:** MEDIOCRE — the drive is long and there is nothing legal to walk up to. Budget: leave Ustron 17:00-17:30, arrive Kroscienko 20:00-21:00 (worse if the Nowy Targ - Kroscienko road queues), sunset approx. 20:35 with usable twilight to ~21:10. That leaves no realistic window to gain height, and it would be pointless anyway because there is no legal pitch above the valley on the Polish side. The only workable Friday plan is therefore 'drive and drop': arrive at a valley campsite in Kroscienko, Szczawnica, Sromowce Nizne or Niedzica, pitch beside the car in the dark, and start climbing on Saturday morning. The Male Pieniny base 'Pod Wysoka' is roughly 1 h - 1 h 15 min uphill from the Jaworki car park and would be reachable on a Friday night in twilight — but it is 25 minutes' further driving and PPN's sunset+1h rule plus the base's own quiet hours make a 22:00 arrival antisocial rather than illegal. Recommendation: treat Friday purely as transfer, and accept that a range this far away with a mandatory valley night is a poor fit for the 'Friday after work' format.
- **Camp 1 ⚠️:** CAMPING CYPEL PIENINY, Kroscienko nad Dunajcem — open all year, on the Dunajec at the confluence, with Wi-Fi, a fire pit, a kitchen annex, electric hook-ups, showers and toilets; approx. 2 h 30 min on foot to Trzy Korony and 2 h to Sokolica, and the Saturday route starts at the gate. Legal basis: a licensed commercial campsite OUTSIDE the national park boundary, so the PPN tent ban does not apply. Price not published in the sources checked, budget 30-45 PLN per person with a car. [uncertain] ALTERNATIVES with the same legal basis: Pole Namiotowe Laskowscy in Sromowce Nizne, ul. Pieninska (year round; shop, Wi-Fi, kitchen building, electric hook-up; approx. 30 min walk to the Cerveny Klastor footbridge, approx. 2 h 30 min to Trzy Korony); Pole Namiotowe OSP Sromowce Nizne on the Dunajec bank (1 May - 30 September, hot showers, toilets, 200 m from the Slovak crossing); Camping Polana Sosny (Niedzica, year round); Pole nad Zalewem and Camp in Leg by Lake Czorsztynskie; Pole Knurow (summer only). SLOVAK EQUIVALENT and the only legal Slovak pitch on the west side: Autokemping Spravy PIENAP-u, Cerveny Klastor, at the entrance to Prielom Dunajca directly under Tri Koruny, season 15 June - 30 September, tel. +421 903 987 415, entrance approx. 1,000 m from the edge of the village coming from Stara Lubovna; tariff published only as a PDF, last dated 1 June 2024, so current prices must be confirmed. [uncertain]
- **Camp 2:** STUDENCKA BAZA NAMIOTOWA 'POD WYSOKA', above Jaworki in the Male Pieniny, under Wysokie Skalki — the only genuine mountain tent camp in the whole Pieniny region and the single most useful finding for this item. Legal basis: it sits OUTSIDE Pieninski Park Narodowy, in the Pieninski Park Krajobrazowy (landscape park, no statutory tent ban), and has operated legally since 1985; it is run by Studenckie Kolo Przewodnikow Beskidzkich Lodz together with Towarzystwo Beskidzkie under PTTK. 2026 season: 28 June - 30 August 2026 (the base opened 26 June). Capacity approx. 60 places in base tents plus 25 pitches for own tents. Prices: from 10 PLN per night for an own tent, 14 PLN in a base tent (some sources quote 8-10 PLN for members). Facilities: a wood-fired shelter/common room, a water source, a fire ring and basic toilets. Reservations: jaworki.skpblodz@gmail.com or by phone; own-tent guests are generally accepted without booking, but a July Saturday is its busiest night. Position: on the green trail towards Wysokie Skalki/Wysoka (1,050 m), above Wawoz Homole — perfectly placed as the far end of a Kroscienko -> Trzy Korony -> Sokolica -> Szczawnica -> Jaworki traverse. NO SECOND OPTION EXISTS INSIDE PPN: the park has zero designated bivouac sites, so any 'camp 2' on the Trzy Korony massif itself would be illegal. The nearest roofed fallbacks are Schronisko PTTK Trzy Korony in Kroscienko and Schronisko PTTK Orlica in Szczawnica — beds, not pitches.
- **Actual spacing vs 15-25 km target ⚠️:** PARTIALLY MEETS the 15-25 km criterion, but only by accident of geography — and only in the recommended cross-region variant. Camp 1 Kroscienko (Camping Cypel) -> camp 2 Jaworki ('Pod Wysoka') along the ridge over Trzy Korony, Sokolica, Szczawnica and Dolina Grajcarka is approx. 18-21 km, inside the target band [uncertain — segment-summed estimate, not a measured route]. But this is a valley-to-valley walk that happens to cross a national park, not two mountain camps: camp 1 is a serviced riverside campsite and camp 2 is a landscape-park student base. Inside PPN itself the spacing question is meaningless — there are ZERO legal anchors, so the achievable spacing between legal in-park camps is undefined/infinite. Scale check: PPN is only ~2,346 ha and its main ridge (Kroscienko -> Trzy Korony -> Sokolica -> Szczawnica) is barely 11-13 km end to end, so the park alone cannot generate a 15-25 km leg; the distance only appears once the Male Pieniny are bolted on. If the plan is confined to the Slovak side, the two legal pitches (Cerveny Klastor autocamp and Goralsky dvor) are only a few kilometres apart in the same valley — far below the criterion.
- **Water sources ⚠️:** The weakest point of the terrain. The Pieniny are karstic limestone: rainfall sinks immediately and the ridges are essentially DRY — there is no reliable water on the Trzy Korony - Czertezik - Sokolica ridge at all, and none on the Male Pieniny border ridge between Wysoka and Durbaszka. Compensating factor: the range is small and villages are never far, so the practical answer is 'fill up in the valley'. Reliable points: taps, shops and bars in Kroscienko, Szczawnica, Sromowce Nizne, Niedzica and Jaworki; all commercial campsites have mains water; Schronisko PTTK Orlica (Szczawnica) and Schronisko PTTK Trzy Korony (Kroscienko); the Studencka Baza 'Pod Wysoka' has its own water source (a spring/piped supply) [uncertain — 2026 yield in a drought summer not verified, ask on arrival]; on the Slovak side the PIENAP autocamp and the Prielom Dunajca path along the river. Streams: the Grajcarek in Dolina Grajcarka and the Bialy/Homolski stream in Wawoz Homole run in normal summers but can drop to a trickle in drought; the Dunajec itself is a large river but not a sensible drinking source without good filtration. Plan on carrying 2-2.5 L for the whole Saturday ridge section — there is nowhere to top up between Kroscienko and Szczawnica.
- **Fire / cooking:** Open fire is BANNED outright inside Pieninski Park Narodowy — the visitor regulation forbids 'palenia ognisk i wyrobow tytoniowych oraz uzywania otwartego ognia', which also makes smoking on the trails an offence. On the Slovak side PIENAP allows a fire outside built-up areas only within 30 m of an existing structure, i.e. effectively only at the two designated facilities. Practically: a gas or liquid-fuel stove is the only cooking method anywhere on the route, and even that should not be used inside PPN. Legal campfires exist only at the commercial campsites (Camping Cypel has a fire pit, as does Pole nad Zalewem) and at the Studencka Baza 'Pod Wysoka', which has its own fire ring and a wood-fired shelter — and even there, expect the staff to suspend fires during a drought/fire-risk period, which 2026 has repeatedly been.

**Safety & Costs**

- **Rescue / insurance ⚠️:** POLISH SIDE: GOPR covers the Pieniny (Grupa Krynicka) [uncertain — group assignment not re-verified] and mountain rescue in Poland is FREE for the victim under the 2011 ustawa o bezpieczenstwie i ratownictwie w gorach; no policy is legally required. Numbers: 601 100 300, 985, 112, plus the 'Ratunek' app. Terrain is low and close to roads, so response is fast. SLOVAK SIDE: Horska zachranna sluzba (HZS) rescue is CHARGEABLE to the rescued person — ground rescue commonly quoted at several hundred euros (order of EUR 300-500) and helicopter involvement at EUR 2,000-4,000 — so anyone stepping onto Slovak trails should carry mountain-rescue insurance. Cheapest fixes: the HZS/Alpenverein/PZA-type short-term policies, a PTTK or Alpenverein membership card with mountain cover, or a travel policy with an explicit 'ratownictwo gorskie' clause and a limit of at least EUR 30,000-50,000. [uncertain — 2026 HZS tariff not re-verified] Since the recommended itinerary is Polish-side only, the insurance question only bites if the Slovak variant is chosen; buy it anyway if the Sromowce Nizne footbridge is on the plan.
- **Bears / ticks:** BEARS: essentially a non-issue here. The Pieniny are small, fragmented by villages and roads, and are not established bear habitat — occasional transient animals move through the Male Pieniny / Beskid Sadecki corridor, but there is no resident population comparable to the Slovak ranges or even the Gorce. Wolves and lynx pass through; no practical risk. TICKS: the real hazard, and the Pieniny are high-risk terrain — warm limestone slopes, grazed meadows, forest edges and long grass along the Dunajec are ideal habitat, and the whole route runs through exactly that. Polish national data for 2025: 48,725 Lyme cases (+63% year on year) and 935 TBE/KZM cases, a 3.5x rise over six years; Malopolska is among the more affected voivodeships and ticks are now active almost year-round. Take permethrin-treated trousers or a DEET/icaridin repellent, tuck in, and do a full body check every evening — twice, because the two campsites are both in grass. TBE (KZM) vaccination is worth starting as a season-long measure but does nothing for a trip next week. Other: adders (zmija zygzakowata) sun themselves on the limestone rocks of Trzy Korony and Sokolica — watch hands on the chained sections.
- **Weather / storms:** Low range, so the mountain hazard is modest, but the exposure is concentrated. Typical July-August: clear mornings, cumulus from midday, thunderstorms most likely 14:00-18:00, with a drought-and-storm regime dominating summer 2026 in southern Poland (damaging fronts 7 and 19 July 2026). Trzy Korony (982 m) is a bare limestone cone with a metal viewing platform, chains and steps — the single worst place in the range to be caught by lightning, and the platform is closed in high wind. Sokolica (747 m) is similar on a smaller scale. Everything else is forested and sheltered. Practical rule: be off Trzy Korony and Sokolica by early afternoon, i.e. leave Kroscienko by 07:00-08:00 so the ticketed summits are done before 12:00 — which also beats the queues. The Dunajec gorge holds morning fog and is noticeably cooler and damper than the ridges. Nights in the valley campsites are mild (12-16 °C in July); the Male Pieniny base at ~900-950 m runs 8-12 °C with heavy dew. Heat is a bigger problem than cold: the south-facing limestone slopes above Szczawnica bake in the afternoon, and there is no water on them.
- **Bail-out plan:** The strongest bailout profile of any candidate — nothing in the Pieniny is more than about 2 hours from a road, a bus stop or a village. Roofed refuges: Schronisko PTTK Trzy Korony in Kroscienko nad Dunajcem and Schronisko PTTK Orlica in Szczawnica, both with beds and food; the Studencka Baza 'Pod Wysoka' has a wood-fired shelter that will take people out of a storm. Villages with shops, buses and accommodation strung along the whole route: Kroscienko, Szczawnica, Jaworki, Sromowce Nizne, Niedzica; on the Slovak side Cerveny Klastor and Lesnica. Public transport runs through the day in summer, so an abandoned trip is a bus ride, not an epic. Medical: health centres in Kroscienko and Szczawnica, hospitals in Nowy Targ and Nowy Sacz. GSM: good to excellent everywhere (all Polish operators; watch for accidental roaming onto Slovak networks along the Dunajec — EU roaming, so no surcharge, but 112 routing may land on the Slovak side, in which case say clearly that you are in Poland). Emergency: 601 100 300 / 985 / 112 in Poland, 18 300 (HZS) / 112 in Slovakia.
- **Total fees ⚠️:** PPN charges no general admission — 'Pieninski Park Narodowy nie pobiera oplat za wstep' — only for specific objects. Trzy Korony and Sokolica viewing galleries: 8.00 PLN normal / 4.00 PLN reduced from 1 June to 30 September, 6.00 PLN / 3.00 PLN from 20 April to 31 May and 1 October to 15 November, nothing from 16 November to 19 April; a ticket bought on Trzy Korony is valid the same day on Sokolica and vice versa, so the whole ridge costs 8 PLN. Other ticketed items (not needed for this route): Zamek Czorsztyn, the natural-history exhibition in Kroscienko, and the Dunajec raft and kayak trips. Accommodation: Studencka Baza 'Pod Wysoka' 10 PLN own tent / 14 PLN base tent; valley campsites approx. 30-45 PLN per person with a car [uncertain]. Parking approx. 20-30 PLN/day [uncertain]. Weekend total per adult on the recommended plan: approx. 8 (Trzy Korony/Sokolica) + 35 (Friday campsite) + 10 (student base) = approx. 53 PLN, plus 20-30 PLN parking. If the Slovak side is added, budget the PIENAP autocamp tariff in EUR (published only as a PDF dated 1 June 2024, current rate unconfirmed) and, if motorways are used, a EUR 12 ten-day e-znamka. [uncertain]

**Season, Crowds & Logistics**

- **Season fit:** Suited to late July in the sense that everything is open — PPN fee season (20 Apr - 15 Nov) is running, the Dunajec raft season (1 Apr - 31 Oct) is running, the PIENAP autocamp (15 Jun - 30 Sep) is open, and, critically, the Studencka Baza 'Pod Wysoka' operates only 28 June - 30 August 2026, so the recommended itinerary is possible ONLY in this narrow window. Against that: this is also the range's absolute peak-crowding period and its hottest, driest one, on waterless limestone with south-facing slopes. Wawoz Homole and Biala Woda are at their best in early summer. Spring (blooming limestone flora, no crowds, free summit access before 20 April) and September (fees still on, crowds thinner, water back in the streams) are both better than late July for everything except the student base, which is the whole point of the plan. Net: acceptable now, but the season is the reason the itinerary works rather than a virtue of it.
- **Crowd level:** The worst on the list. Trzy Korony is one of the most heavily visited summits in Poland: the summit platform is ticketed and metered, queues on a sunny summer Saturday are routine, and the Sokolica pine is a mass photo stop. The Dunajec raft trip pulls coach traffic into Kroscienko, Szczawnica and Sromowce Nizne, so the valleys are busy from mid-morning. A Friday-evening start helps only marginally, because the crowd is made of day visitors, not campers — the real lever is an early Saturday start (on the summits before 10:00) and using the Male Pieniny for the second half, which are far quieter. Wawoz Homole is busy at its mouth and quiet above. The Slovak side (Prielom Dunajca path, Cerveny Klastor) is busy with cyclists and rafting traffic but less so on the ridges. Campsite capacity: valley campsites are large and rarely turn people away; the student base has only ~25 own-tent pitches and can be full on a July Saturday — mail ahead.
- **Navigation / maps:** Polish side: standard PTTK colour-stripe waymarking, extremely dense and well signed — PPN is small and it is genuinely hard to get lost. Use mapa-turystyczna.pl for measured Polish routes and GOT points, plus OsmAnd or Locus with offline OSM; the official trail and fee pages are at piepn.gov.pl. Paper: Compass 'Pieniny' 1:25,000. Slovak side: KST waymarking, same four colours but a different signpost format (walking times only); use hiking.sk or mapy.cz, and remember that Slovak maps will not show the Polish trail numbering. Two traps specific to this item: (1) the two national parks have different and mutually inconsistent time-of-day rules (PL: sunrise-1h to sunset+1h; SK: sunrise+1h to sunset-1h), which most maps and guidebooks do not show at all; (2) the Male Pieniny border ridge runs literally along the state line, so a GPS track may flip between PL and SK map layers and between mobile networks — download BOTH offline map sets before leaving. Also download the PPN ticket/fee information in advance; ticket points at Trzy Korony and Sokolica are staffed seasonally and take cash and card, but coverage on the summits is not guaranteed.

> ⚠️ _Fields above contain self-flagged agent estimates/caveats (verify before departure): Distance / time from Ustroń, Parking, Public transport, Vignettes, Camping rules, 2026 status (live blockers), Fine risk, Suggested route, Camp 1, Actual spacing vs 15-25 km target, Water sources, Rescue / insurance, Total fees_

---

### 13. Tatry Wysokie (PL/SK, TPN/TANAP)

**Access & Travel**

- **Distance / time from Ustroń ⚠️:** ESTIMATE — not verified in a routing engine (data-source flag: manual estimate, 2026-07-28). Ustron -> Zakopane: approx. 195-215 km, 2 h 50 min - 3 h 20 min via S1 Bielsko-Biala -> S52/DK52 Wadowice -> DK28 Sucha Beskidzka -> Jordanow -> DK7 'Zakopianka' Rabka - Nowy Targ - Zakopane; the Zakopianka is the single worst Friday-afternoon bottleneck in southern Poland and can add 45-60 min in July. Ustron -> Lysa Polana / Tatranska Javorina (the trailhead for Bielovodska dolina and the ONLY legal camping spot inside TANAP): approx. 225-245 km, 3 h 15 min - 3 h 50 min, either through Zakopane and Bukowina Tatrzanska or via Nowy Targ - Niedzica - Spisska Stara Ves. Ustron -> Bobrovecka Vapenica (Bobrovec/Jalovec, western foot of the Zapadne Tatry): approx. 200-215 km, 3 h - 3 h 30 min via Cieszyn - Zilina - Ruzomberok - Liptovsky Mikulas (motorway, e-znamka required) or via Chyzne/Trstena. This is the farthest and slowest option on the entire candidate list, consistent with the scope note's ~3 h assumption — in practice 3 h is the optimistic case, not the expected one. [uncertain]
- **PL/SK/CZ border:** Schengen, no controls, but this is the item where the two legal regimes differ most. Main crossings: Lysa Polana / Tatranska Javorina (the natural one for Bielovodska dolina), Chyzne - Trstena and Cieszyn - Cesky Tesin for the motorway route. Currency EUR in Slovakia; the taborisko fee and Slovak parking are payable in EUR (cash or Slovak-operator SMS). Waymarking: Poland uses PTTK colour stripes with signposted times; Slovakia uses KST markings with hours only — cross-referencing a PL and a SK map at the ridge is normal. Legal asymmetry to keep in mind: Poland bans tents in TPN outright, Slovakia permits exactly one high-mountain site, and Slovakia charges for mountain rescue while Poland does not. Slovak motorways require an e-znamka; the Lysa Polana approach through Zakopane does not touch one.
- **Parking ⚠️:** POLAND: TPN-area car parks are among the most expensive in the country and are effectively rationed. The main trailhead lots (Kuznice, Palenica Bialczanska for Morskie Oko, Kiry for Dolina Koscieliska, Siwa Polana for Chocholowska, Lysa Polana) are run by the park, the commune or private operators at roughly 30-60 PLN per day, with Palenica Bialczanska full by 07:00-08:00 on a summer weekend and Kuznice not much later; from late morning drivers are turned away and pushed onto shuttle buses from Zakopane. Some lots do not permit overnight standing, and — critically — sleeping in a car on a TPN car park counts as illegal bivouacking. SLOVAKIA: paid lots at Tatranska Javorina/Lysa Polana and at every valley mouth (Tatranska Lomnica, Stary Smokovec, Strbske Pleso, Podbanske), roughly EUR 5-15 per day, generally easier to get into than the Polish side but also filling by mid-morning at Strbske Pleso. The Bielovodska dolina approach starts from the Lysa Polana / Javorina lots. Exact 2026 tariffs are not centrally published and change season to season — verify before departure. [uncertain]
- **Public transport ⚠️:** Good on both sides, and effectively mandatory in high season because the car parks fill. Poland: frequent buses and minibuses Krakow - Nowy Targ - Zakopane, trains to Zakopane, and a dense summer shuttle network Zakopane -> Kuznice, Palenica Bialczanska (Morskie Oko), Kiry, Siwa Polana and Lysa Polana, running from early morning to evening. Slovakia: the Tatra electric railway (TEZ) Poprad - Stary Smokovec - Strbske Pleso plus the cog railway and dense bus links along the southern foot; buses Poprad - Tatranska Javorina serve the Bielovodska trailhead a few times a day. This makes a linear PL->SK or SK->PL traverse over Lysa Polana genuinely feasible without a second car. Timetables tighten sharply in the evening on the Slovak side — the last bus from Tatranska Javorina is early. [uncertain — 2026 timetables not verified]
- **Vignettes ⚠️:** Depends on the routing. Zakopane / Lysa Polana approach through Poland only: no vignette, Polish roads on this route are toll-free for cars. Motorway approach to the Slovak side (Cieszyn -> D1 Zilina -> Ruzomberok -> Liptovsky Mikulas, needed for Bobrovecka Vapenica and the fastest access to the Zapadne Tatry): Slovak e-znamka required — approx. EUR 12 for 10 days, approx. EUR 17 for 30 days, approx. EUR 60 for a year, bought electronically at eznamka.sk against the licence plate, no sticker. The Czech D48 shortcut via Cesky Tesin - Frydek-Mistek would additionally need a Czech e-vignette (approx. CZK 200 for 10 days) — avoidable by staying on Polish and Slovak roads. [uncertain — 2026 tariffs not re-verified]

**Camping Legality**

- **Protection status:** Two of the strictest national parks in Central Europe facing each other across the main ridge. POLAND: Tatrzanski Park Narodowy (TPN), approx. 21,200 ha, the whole Polish Tatras, with a total year-round camping ban and, since 1 February 2026, a year-round night-movement ban. SLOVAKIA: Tatransky narodny park (TANAP), the oldest Slovak national park, with a 2023/2024 visitor regulation (navstevny poriadok, Vestnik vlady SR 2023) that bans camping and bivouacking everywhere except a single named site, closes the high trails for more than half the year, bans dogs at 65 high-altitude locations, and requires registration and alpine-club membership for climbing and ski-mountaineering. Both parks sit at the top of the protection hierarchy — 5. stupen ochrany in Slovak terms across the core. In practical terms for a tent trip, this is the single most restrictive item on the entire candidate list.
- **Camping rules:** POLAND (TPN): ABSOLUTE BAN, no exceptions, all year, in all weather. The prohibition covers tents, hammocks, tarps, sleeping under wiaty, and sleeping in a car on a park car park — TPN states this explicitly. The only legal overnight options inside the Polish Tatras are the PTTK mountain huts (Murowaniec, Piec Stawow, Morskie Oko, Roztoka, Ornak, Chocholowska, Kalatowki, Kondratowa) and, outside the park boundary, commercial campsites in Zakopane and the surrounding villages. SLOVAKIA (TANAP): exactly ONE legal high-mountain site — 'Taborisko pod Vysokou' in Bielovodska dolina. The navstevny poriadok wording is unambiguous: camping and bivouacking on TANAP territory is possible only at that locality, in the periods 1 June - 31 October and 15 December - 15 April, and 'len ako sucast viac dni trvajuceho skialpinistickeho alebo horolezeckeho prechodu' — only as part of a multi-day ski-mountaineering or climbing traverse. Statne lesy TANAPu, which run the site, put it even more plainly: the alpine camp 'is used exclusively for performance mountaineering and is available for organized groups of mountaineers'. KEY CONSEQUENCE FOR THIS TRIP: an ordinary hiker with a tent does NOT qualify — this is a climbers' basecamp with a legal purpose test, not a public campsite, and Slovak rules additionally require climbers and ski-tourers to register each outing and hold valid alpine-club (JAMES/UIAA) membership. Site rules: tents only on the wooden platforms, no open fire, camping stoves only, arrive no later than sunset and leave no earlier than sunrise (summer) or between 05:00 and 19:00 (winter), vacate by 12:00 and carry out all rubbish; toilets, tables and benches provided. Fee EUR 2.50 per person per night, paid IN ADVANCE either in cash at the horaren (forest lodge) in Bielovodska dolina or by SMS 'TABORISKO' to 8877 from a Slovak-operator card, valid if sent by 16:00 for the following night until 10:00. Everything else legal is a valley campsite outside both parks — including the Bobrovecka Vapenica recreation area (748 m, cadastre of Bobrovec near Jalovec, at the western foot of the Zapadne Tatry, with summer camps Dakota, Jevicko and Taborisko pri Studnicke), Autokemping Oravice, ATC Racova/Racková dolina, Tatranec at Tatranska Lomnica, Sport camp Tatranska Strba and Camp Sedmicka at Zdiar, and on the Polish side Camping pod Krokwia and Camping Ustup in Zakopane plus farm campsites in Koscielisko and Witow.
- **2026 status (live blockers) ⚠️:** Checked 2026-07-28. (1) TPN NIGHT BAN — the decisive new fact. From 1 February 2026 a ban on moving along TPN trails between 22:00 and 05:00 applies from 1 December to 28 February; this closes the last gap in a rule that already banned movement from dusk to dawn between 1 March and 30 November. Night hiking in the Polish Tatras is now prohibited all year. No exceptions were published in the TPN announcement [uncertain — whether guests walking the shortest route to a booked hut are exempted is not stated in the source and must be confirmed with TPN]. (2) TPN also applies seasonal wildlife closures (dusk-08:00 on selected sections 1 March - 15 May) and full seasonal closures of certain high routes 1 December - 15 May. (3) TANAP — the summer season on the high trails now opens 1 June (moved two weeks earlier by the 2024 navstevny poriadok) and runs to 31 October; outside that window the high-altitude trails are closed. The same regulation banned dogs at 65 high-altitude locations, restricted geocaching to 1 June - 31 October, and imposed registration plus alpine-club membership on climbers and ski-tourers. (4) Taborisko pod Vysokou is therefore OPEN right now (1 June - 31 October window) but only to qualifying climbing/ski-mountaineering parties. (5) TPN tickets have been at the current level since 1 March 2025. (6) Summer 2026 across the region is a drought-and-storm season (damaging fronts 7 and 19 July 2026) — re-check tpn.gov.pl/aktualnosci, tanap.sk and hiking.sk plus the HZS avalanche/weather bulletin in the week of departure. [uncertain]
- **Hourly / seasonal restrictions:** The hardest set of time constraints on the whole list — and the reason this item collides with a 'Friday after work' format. POLAND (TPN): movement on trails prohibited from dusk to dawn, 1 March - 30 November; prohibited 22:00-05:00, 1 December - 28 February (new, effective 1 February 2026). At the end of July dusk in Zakopane falls around 21:00-21:15, so a party leaving Ustron at 17:30 and arriving at a trailhead at 20:30-21:00 has essentially no legal walking window. Additional seasonal closures: dusk to 08:00 on selected trails 1 March - 15 May, and full closure of certain high routes 1 December - 15 May. SLOVAKIA (TANAP): high-altitude trails are open only 1 June - 31 October (the 2024 rules moved the opening from mid-June to 1 June) and are CLOSED 1 November - 31 May; daily movement is limited to daytime, defined as sunrise to sunset, with the earlier 'one hour after sunrise / one hour before sunset' rule relaxed in 2024 so that hut guests can walk in and out by the shortest route. The taborisko itself must be reached no later than sunset in summer and left no earlier than sunrise. Net effect: both sides legally forbid the evening approach that this weekend format depends on.
- **Fine risk ⚠️:** POLAND: TPN rangers issue on-the-spot mandates of UP TO 1,000 PLN for pitching a tent inside the park, and the offence (art. 127 ustawy o ochronie przyrody, breach of the art. 15 prohibition) carries a far higher statutory maximum if it goes to court. The same exposure applies to hammocks, tarps, sleeping under a wiata and sleeping in a car on a park car park, and separately to walking the trails inside the new night-ban window. TPN is intensively patrolled, drone- and camera-monitored around the popular lakes, and media regularly report fined bivouackers — the practical detection risk is high. SLOVAKIA: camping outside the taborisko is a priestupok under zakon 543/2002 Z.z. o ochrane prirody a krajiny, enforced by TANAP's straz prirody and the police, with block fines in the low hundreds of euros and materially higher amounts by administrative decision [uncertain — 2026 tariff not verified]; using the taborisko without meeting the climbing/ski-mountaineering purpose test, or without paying in advance, is also a breach. Cost-benefit is unambiguous: the legal alternatives (a hut bunk, a valley campsite, or EUR 2.50 at the taborisko if you qualify) are cheap relative to the fine and to the enforcement probability.

**Route & Camps**

- **Suggested route ⚠️:** HONEST ANSWER TO THE SCOPE QUESTION: the High Tatras do NOT fit a Friday-evening / two-tent-camps weekend, and no route proposal can make them fit without breaking a rule. The three structurally possible shapes are: (A) HUT-TO-HUT, NO TENT (the format the range is actually built for) — Friday: drive to Zakopane, sleep at Camping pod Krokwia or a pension; Saturday: Kuznice -> Hala Gasienicowa (Murowaniec) -> Zawrat or Krzyzne -> Dolina Pieciu Stawow Polskich (hut), approx. 12-14 km, +1,200 m, 6-7 h; Sunday: Piec Stawow -> Szpiglasowa Przelecz -> Morskie Oko -> Palenica Bialczanska, approx. 12 km, 5-6 h, bus back to the car. Two nights, zero tents, entirely legal, but it is not the requested trip. (B) VALLEY-CAMP DAY-TRIPS — Friday: pitch at a legal campsite (Camping pod Krokwia in Zakopane, or Bobrovecka Vapenica / Camp Sedmicka Zdiar / Tatranec on the Slovak side); Saturday and Sunday: two separate long day-hikes returning to the same tent. This keeps the tent but abandons the 'two camps, point-to-point' idea and the 15-25 km spacing entirely. (C) CLIMBERS' TRAVERSE VIA TABORISKO POD VYSOKOU — legal only for a party undertaking a multi-day climbing or ski-mountaineering traverse, registered, with valid alpine-club membership: approach from Lysa Polana / Tatranska Javorina up Bielovodska dolina to the taborisko (approx. 8-9 km, +300-400 m from the road, roughly 2 h 30 min) [uncertain — distance and altitude estimated], use it as a basecamp for climbing objectives around Vysoka, then continue or descend. RECOMMENDATION: treat this item as OUT OF SCOPE for the weekend brief and use it as a hut-to-hut trip in a different format; the ranges that actually support two legal tent camps 15-25 km apart are Nizke Tatry, Velka Fatra and Beskid Sadecki.
- **Friday-evening feasibility:** THE WORST ON THE LIST — effectively zero. Three independent blockers stack: (1) the drive is the longest of all candidates, 3 h - 3 h 50 min with the Zakopianka bottleneck, so arrival at a trailhead is 20:30-21:30 at best; (2) TPN prohibits movement on trails from dusk to dawn between 1 March and 30 November, and dusk in late July is around 21:00-21:15 — the legal walking window on arrival is nil; (3) TANAP restricts movement to daylight and requires arrival at the taborisko no later than sunset, and the taborisko is a 2 h 30 min walk from the road. On top of that, the trailhead car parks (Palenica Bialczanska, Kuznice) are full from early morning on summer weekends, so a Saturday-morning re-approach by car is also unreliable. The only workable Friday plan is 'drive and sleep at a valley campsite or pension', starting the real day on Saturday morning — which converts the trip into a base-camp weekend, not a traverse. If a two-camp tent traverse is the goal, choose a different range.
- **Camp 1 ⚠️:** There is NO legal camp spot inside the Polish Tatras — this must be stated plainly. Camp 1 has to be a valley campsite outside TPN: CAMPING POD KROKWIA, Zakopane, at the foot of the Wielka Krokiew ski jump — open all year, pitches for tents and campers, electric hook-ups, showers, waste disposal, walking distance to Kuznice and Dolina Bystrej (i.e. straight onto the Kasprowy / Hala Gasienicowa trails); crowded in summer, booking recommended. Legal basis: licensed commercial campsite outside the national park boundary. ALTERNATIVES: Camping Ustup on the Nowy Targ approach to Zakopane (grass pitches, electricity, shuttle-bus access to Kuznice and the valleys); small farm campsites in Koscielisko and Witow with bus links to Dolina Koscieliska and Chocholowska, quieter and near Termy Chocholowskie. SLOVAK EQUIVALENTS for a Slovak-side start: Autocamp Tatranec (Tatranska Lomnica), Sport camp Tatranska Strba, Camp Sedmicka (Zdiar, closest to Lysa Polana), ATC Racova/Rackova dolina, Autokemping Oravice, and the Bobrovecka Vapenica recreation area at 748 m in the cadastre of Bobrovec near Jalovec, at the western foot of the Zapadne Tatry, with summer camps Dakota, Jevicko and Taborisko pri Studnicke plus the Mnich mountain hotel.
